Management 


(JUDGMENT) 
(Passed on this 09" day of December - 2025) 


As per letter dated 24/[ 2/2020 by the Government of India, Ministry of Labour, New Delhi, the reference is 
made to this Tribunal under Section-l0 of Industrial Disputes Act, |947 (in short the ‘Act’)as per Notification 
No. L-2202/46/2020-(IR(CM-ID) dt. 24/2/2020. The dispute under reference relates to: 


“Whether the demand of Koyla Mazdoor Panchayat (HMS), CMPDL, Bilaspur, not to transfer (7) Shri 
Abhaya Kumar Srivastav S/o Late Sidhnath Lal Srivastayv, (2) Sri Radheshyam Jaiswal, (3) Sri Rajesh 
Kumar Singh, (4) Sri Shyamlal Marco, workmen, from Kusumda Camp to Korba and thereafter to 
Bilaspur office of CMPDIL, while there is an ongoing ID case, is justified and legal in view of the terms 
of memorandum of settlement no. 2$7-7(66)//207 क्‍या, € dated 02-07-20762 If not, to what relief these 
four workmen are entitled?” 


After registering case on the basis of the reference, notices were issued to the parties. The Workman side 
appeared and filed their statement of claim. None appeared for Management. They did not filed any written statement 
of defense. The case proceeded ex-parte against them vide order dated 0.02.2025. The Workman Union filed 
affidavit. None appeared for Management to cross-examine them in the light of affidavit. Ex-parte arguments were 
heard from side of Workman Union submitted by their Learned Counsel Mr. Rajesh Chan. I have gone through the 
record as well. 


The case taken by the Workman Union is that, the Applicant Workmen are working as Security Guard 
under the Management of Outsourcing Agency Contractor M/s Rahul Pandey Security Agency and are deputed at the 
site of CMPDIL Camp, at Kusmunda. There was a settlement between the Union and the Contractor on 02.0.208 
(Annexure W-| to the Statement of claim). The terms of settlement was that on work exigency and need basis the 
Security Guards employed at CMPDIL at various locations of different camps including Headquarter shall be rotated 
from time to time in fair manner and that the unwilling security guards deployed on rotational basis will be persuaded 


876 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : FEBRUARY 28, 2026/PHALGUNA 9, 947 [PART II—SEc. 3(ii)] 


and convinced by the Union to join at their new places of posting. Another settlement was reached at between the 
Union and the Security Agency on 28.06.209 in which the Security Agency agreed to issued Wage Slip from the 
current month. The Union again submitted letter dated 7.08.209 to the Regional Labour Commissioner with respect 
to their demands. No action was taken, hence the Union approached Hon’ble High Court at Bilaspur by way of Writ 
Petition No. 9720 of 20l9. Notice was given to the Authorities vide letter dated 02.2.20l9 on the complaint 
submitted by the Union, the Labour Enforcement Officer Bilaspur directed the Regional Director CMPDIL to make 
payment of unpaid dues of the Security Guards. Again the workers were threatened by the Security Agency. The 
Union again raised a dispute before RLC Bilaspur which was under process. During pendency of the proceeding 
before the Regional Labour Commissioner, Bilaspur, the Applicant Workmen were transferred by the Security 
Agency vide letter dated 20.09.2022 to Headquarter of CMPDIL at Bilaspur without obtaining approval from the 
Regional Labour Commissioner before whom the dispute was pending. Hence, this action of Management of Security 
Agency is in violation of the Act because it amounts to change in service conditions of the workmen during pendency 
of a dispute. Thus is unjust, illegal and arbitrary. The Union has prayed that holding this action of Management in 
transferring the applicants to Headquarter of CMPDI at Bilaspur by the Security Agency is unjust and arbitrary, the 
transfer order be quashed. Affidavit has been field by the Representative of the Union along with certain documents 
which are different representations raising disputes before Labour Commissioner and settlement as mention above, to 
be referred to as and when required. 


Learned Counsel for Workman Union has submitted that, the Transfer of the Applicant Workman during 
pendency of dispute before the Regional Labour Commissioner amounts to change in the service condition of the 
Workman done by the Management of Security Agency without getting required approval hence is in violation of 
Section 9A of the Act and secondly, it is in violation of the settlement reached at between the Union and the Security 
Agency as referred to earlier. 


Section 9A of the Act is being reproduced as follows: 


9A. Notice of change.-—No employer, who proposes to effect any change in the conditions of service 
applicable to any workman in respect of any matter specified in the Fourth Schedule, shall effect such change,— 
(a) without giving to the workmen likely to be affected by such change a notice in the prescribed manner of the 
nature of the change proposed to be effected; or (b) within twenty-one days of giving such notice: Provided that no 
notice shall be required for effecting any such change— (a) where the change is effected in pursuance of any 
2[settlement or award]; or (b) where the workmen likely to be affected by the change are persons to whom the 
Fundamental and Supplementary Rules, Civil Services (Classification, Control and Appeal) Rules, Civil Services 
(Temporary Service) Rules, Revised Leave Rules, Civil Service Regulations, Civilians in Defence Services 
(Classification, Control and Appeal) Rules or the Indian Railway Establishment Code or any other rules or 
regulations that may be notified in this behalf by the appropriate Government in the Official Gazette, apply. 


The question further arises is whether the transfer of an employee is change in conditions of his service. 
Transfer is an incident of service, and an employee has no right to remain at a place of his choice for any particular 
period, the only thing which is required to be observed is that the transfer is not be mala fide and is should be in the 
interest of the efficient working of the Organization and also in good faith. The Learned Counsel for the Workman 
Union could not establish that transfer of an employee from one place to other place is change of his conditions in 
service. Since, it is not established that transfer is change in service conditions, Section 9A of the Act is not attracted 
and it is held that no prior approval is required as submitted by Learned Counsel for the Union. 


As regards second argument that it is in violation of settlement dated 02.0!.208 which specifically mention 
that Security Guards employed at various allegations including headquarter shall be rotated from time to time in a fair 
manner. Hence the transfer of the applicant workmen cannot be said to be in violation of the settlement. 


On the basis of above discussion, the reference is answered as follows. 
AWARD 


Holding the demand of Koyla Mazdoor Panchayat (HMS), CMPDIL, Bilaspur, not to transfer (7) Shri 
Abhaya Kumar Srivastav S/o Late Sidhnath Lal Srivastav, (2) Sri Radheshyam Jaiswal, (3) Sri Rajesh Kumar 
Singh, (4) Sri Shyamlal Marco, workmen, from Kusumda Camp to Korba and thereafter to Bilaspur office of 
CMPDIL legal and justified, they are entitled to no relief. 


No order as to cost. 
DATE:- 09/2/2025 
P.K. SRIVASTAVA, PRESIDING OFFICER 


[भाग ए---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : फरवरी 28, 2026/फाल्गुन 9, 947 877 


नई दिल्‍ली, 27 जनवरी, 2026 


का.आ. 479.---औद्योगिक विवाद अधिनियम, i947 (947 का 4) की धारा ॥7 के अनुसरण में, केन्द्रीय 
सरकार डब्ल्यू.सी.एल. के प्रबंधतंत्र के संबद्ध नियोजको और उनके कर्मकारों के बीच, अनुबंध में निर्दिष्ट औद्योगिक विवाद में 
केन्द्रीय सरकार औद्योगिक अधिकरण-सह-श्रम न्यायालय, जबलपुर केपंचाट(एलसी/आर-23/2046) को प्रकाशित 
करती है, जो केन्द्रीय सरकार को 27/04,/2026 को प्राप्त हुआ था। 


[सं. WaA-2202/97/205-aTe आर(सी.एम-)] 
सलोनी, उप निदेशक/लिंक अधिकारी 


New Delhi, the 27th Januruary, 2026

Versues 
The Chief Manager,

Chhindwara - 48055] 
Management 


(JUDGMENT) 
(Passed on this 7" day of December - 2025) 


As per letter dated 04.02.206 by the Government of India, Ministry of Labour, New Delhi, the reference is 
made to this Tribunal under Section-l0 of Industrial Disputes Act, 947 (in short the ‘Act’)as per Notification 
No. L-2202/97/205-(IR(CM-ID) dt. 04.02.206. The dispute under reference relates to: 

"क्या मुख्य महाप्रबंधक; ated कोत्रफील्डस त्रिमिटेड कन्हान क्षेत्र FRAT Te, जिला लछिंदवाडा (मध्य प्रदेश 

दृगरा पूर्व कामगार श्री किशोर कुमार आत्मज श्री जियरिया; पूर्व टिंबरमेन घोडावागी कॉलरी AW | के एनसीडन्ल्यूए के 

प्रावधानों तवानुसार जारी कियान्‍्वयन आवेश76 के अनुसार जन्मतिथि संशोधन हेतू विए Haga अनुसार जन्मतिथि 

23.09.952 के स्थान पर 2.05.959 ना करना तथा फार्म बी में Tot जन्मतिथि 23.09.952 के आधार पर पूर्व 

कामगार श्री किशोर कुमार को विनांक3.03.20 को सेवानिवृत्त करना कथित हैं? Ae नही तो कर्मकार क्या अनुतोष 

पाने का अधिकारी हैं?" 


878 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : FEBRUARY 28, 2026/PHALGUNA 9, 947 [PART II—SEc. 3(ii)] 


The facts connected are mainly that, undisputedly the Workman was appointed by Management and he 
joined as a Tub Loader in Dumua Colliery with the Management of Western Coal Field Limited on 03.05.985. He 
was transferred to Ghorawari Colliery on 0.03.993. According to him, he had produced his documents which were 
his Registration Card with Employment Exchange mentioning his date of birth at the time of his first joining. His date 
of birth 2.05.959 was mentioned in his documents which were recorded by Management at the time of his joining, 
but it was unilaterally changed from I2.05.959 to 23.09.!952 by Management while he was working on transfer in 
Ghorawari Colliery. He made various representations in this respect, which were not heard. Ultimately, he filed a writ 
petition No. 7953/2007 which was disposed vide order dated 3.05.2009 after hearing. The Management of WCL was 
directed to consider the representation and supporting documents with respect to his this grievance of the petitioner 
workman and decide it in the light of binding instructions preferably within a period of three months from the date of 
filing of the representation. 


It is the case of the Workman that he filed a representation in the light of said order which was dismissed by 
the Management vide communication dated 29.08.2009 (Copy filed and proved as Exhibit W-7) without 
considering and in ignorance of the rules specifically Implementation Instructions, 76 (II. 76) which is unjust, 
illegal and arbitrary. The Management superannuated him on the basis of his incorrect date of birth 23.09.l952 which 
is also unjust, arbitrary and illegal. The Workman has further alleged that he again filed a W.P. No. 5289/20]3 
against dismissal of his representation seeking correction of his date of birth and his superannuation which was 
disposed by Hon’ble High Court vide order dated 05.02.20l5 with an observation that he may seek remedy with the 
Competent Labour Court. The Workman has thus prayed that, setting-aside his superannuation on the basis of his 
incorrect date of birth, he be deemed to be reinstated with all back-wages and pre as well post retrial benefits deeming 
him to be in continuous service till date of his superannuation on the basis of his date of birth 2.05.959. 


The Management has defended its action with the case that, he declared himself as an illiterate at the time 
of his first posting and also declared his date of birth 23.09.952 which was recorded in Form 8 which he accepted. 
This date of birth was replicated in his service records. He was issued last pay certificate on his transfer to Ghorawari 
Colliery mentioning date of birth 23.09.952. A pension scheme was introduced in the Mines in the year 988, he 
mentioned his same date birth in his Pension form PS-I and PS-II. His representation was correctly decided in the 
compliance of order of Hon’ble High Court as mentioned above. 


Both the sides have filed affidavits as his examination in chief and have been cross-examined. Both the 
sides have also filed and proved documents to be referred to as and when required. 


I have heard argument of Learned Counsel Mr. Vinod Napit for Workman and Mr. Neeraj Kewat for 
Management. Both the sides has filed written submissions also which are part of record. I have gone through the 
written submissions and the record. 


One of the submissions from the side of Management is that, since the dispute has been raised at the fag end 
of his service, the request should not be considered as it barred by unexplained delay and latches on part of the 
Workman. 


The judgment of Hon’ble Supreme Court in the case of Karnataka Rural Infrastructure Development 
Limited V.s. T.P. Nagrajappa (2027) 72,५८6 27 has been referred to in this respect. 


Since in this case, there is a specific direction of Hon’ble High Court in the said writ petition as referred to 
above, to consider the representation of the Workman in the light of relevant rules and circulars with respect to 
correction of his date of birth, the plea of delay and latches is not available to Management. The only issue requires 
to be considered here is whether the action of Management in dismissing the representation of the Workman with 
respect to change of his date of birth in the light of direction of Hon’ble High Court in the aforesaid Writ as 
mentioned above is correct in law or fact or not. 


The communication with respect to rejection of workman’s representation by Management vide letter dated 
29.08.2009 speaks that since the documents submitted by the Workman with his representation were not submitted to 
the Department at the time when he entered into employment, therefore, these documents filed at the fag end of his 
service could not be considered as they do not come within the rules of the Company. The relevant portion of this 
communication is being reproduced as follows: 


“ That Form-B is a statutory register prepared under the provisions of Mines Act. That at the time of 
entering into employment, documents submitted by you with your representation were not submitted to the 
Department. Therefore, these documents at the fag end of your service cannot be considered as they do not come 
within the rules of the Company. 


That while you have entered in services and accepted your date of birth as 23.09, 7952 and put signature 
as token of its correctness therefore, it is not possible to accept that your date of birth is /2.05. /959, ? 


As stated above, the ground of delay was not available to the Management for rejection of the claim because 
it was being considered in compliance of order by Hon’ble High Court in Writ Petition details mentioned above. 
Hence, the rejection of representation on this ground is nothing but perverse. 


[भाग ए---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : फरवरी 28, 2026/फाल्गुन 9, 947 879 


The Implementation Instructions - 76 provides a comprehensive procedure for deciding disputes with 
respect to date of birth of the employees which are being reproduced as follows: 


Determination of the age at the time of appointment 
i) Matriculates. 


In the case of appointees who have passed Matriculation or equivalent examinations, the 
date of birth recorded in the said certificate shall be treated as correct date of birth and the same 
will not be altered under any circumstances. 


ii) Non-matriculates but educated. 


In the case of appointees who have pursued studies in a recognised educational institution, the 
date of birth recorded in the School Leaving Certificate, shall be treated as correct date of birth and, 
thesame will not be altered under any circumstances. 


The Workman side has filed and proved his educational documents certificate of passing his 
Purav Madhyamik Pariksha in 975, his transfer certificate for Class 5“ for the year 97], his class 77 Mark- 
sheet of 973, extracts of his service extracts issued to him by the Management on 3.05.987 (Exhibit W- 
7) which establishes his claim that his date of birth is 2.05.959 which is held proved. The Management 
documents filed and proved are firstly, with respect to the place where he was transferred i.e. Ghorawari 
Colliery and secondly, are replications of his last pay certificate issued on his transfer with respect to entry 
regarding date of his birth hence, cannot be relied upon. 


In the light of above discussion and findings, the action of Management of WCL in superannuating 
the Workman Kishore Kumar on the basis of his date of birth 23.09.952 and not correcting his date of birth 
from 23.09.952 to 2.05.959 is held unjust, arbitrary and illegal. Consequently, the reference is answered 
as follows. 


AWARD 


Holding the action of Management of WCL in not correcting the date of birth of workman 
Kishore Kumar S/o Jhigariya from 23.09, 7/952/ 72.05.7959 in the light of his representation filed by him 
in compliance of order of Hon’ble High Court of M.P. in W.P. No. 7953/2007 unjust, illegal and 
arbitrary, the superannuation of the Workman on the basis of his date of birth 23.09, /952 is set-aside. He 
is reinstated from the date of his superannuation with all consequential pre and post retrial benefits till 
date of his superannuation on the basis of his date of birth 72, 05./959 deeming him to be in continuous 
service of the Management. 


No order as to cost. 
DATE:- 7/2/2025 
P.K. SRIVASTAVA, Presiding Officer 


नई दिल्‍ली, 2 फरवरी, 2026

New Delhi, the [20 February, 2026 


5.0. 80.—In pursuance of Section 7 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 947 (l4 of 947), the Central 
Government hereby publishes the Award (Reference. 24/2023) of the Central Government Industrial Tribunal- 
cum-Labour Court, Asansol as shown in the Annexure, in the industrial dispute between the Management of ECL 
and their workmen, received by the Central Government on ]/02/2026. 


[No. L-2202/36/2023 — IR (CM-ID)] 
SALONI, Dy. Director/Link officer 


880 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : FEBRUARY 28, 2026/PHALGUNA 9, 947 [PART II—SEc. 3(ii)]

REFERENCE CASE NO. 24 OF 2023 
PARTIES: Kartik Kora 
Vs. 
Management of J.K. Nagar (R) Colliery of M/s. ECL 


REPRESENTATIVES: 

For the Workman: Mr. Rakesh Kumar, President, Koyala Mazdoor Congress 
For the Management of ECL: Mr. P.K. Das, Advocate 

INDUSTRY: Coal. 

STATE: West Bengal. 

Dated: 06..2025 


AWARD 


On failure of conciliation proceeding, the Government of India through the Ministry of Labour, in exercise 
of powers conferred under clause (d) of Sub-section (l) and Sub-section (2A) of Section |0 of the Industrial Disputes 
Act, 947 (4 of 947), vide its Order No. L-2202/36/2023-IR(CM-ID) dated 02.05.2023 has referred the following 
dispute between the employer, that is the Management of J.K. Nagar Colliery under Satgram Area of M/s. Eastern 
Coalfields Limited (hereinafter referred as ECL) and their workman for adjudication by this Tribunal. 


SCHEDULE 


“Whether the action of the Management of M/s. Eastern Coalfields Ltd. in relation to its J K Nagar (R) 
Colliery under Satgram Area in dismissing the service of Shri Kartik Kora, UGL, U.M. No. 38088 vide their 
letter dated 280-03-20/2 is legal and justified. If not, what relief the concerned workman is entitled to? ” 


On receiving Order No. L-2202/36/2023-IR(CM-II) dated 02.05.2023 from the Government of India 
Ministry of Labour, New Delhi for adjudication of the dispute, a Reference case No. 24 of 2023 was registered on 
02.05.2023 and an order was passed for issuing notice to the parties through registered post, directing them to appear 
and submit their written statements along with relevant documents in support of their claims and a list of witnesses. 


2. Union filed written statement on 3.06.2023. The fact of the workman’s case as delineated in their written 
statement is that Kartik Kora was posted as Under Ground Loader (UG Loader) bearing U.M. No. — 380288 at J.K. 
Nagar Colliery under Satgram Area, ECL. He was a permanent employee of the company and could not attend his 
duty from 26.04.2004 due to his illness. After his recovery, he requested the management of the employer company to 
permit him to join duty and submitted treatment papers. The management did not allow him to resume duty and 
issued a chargesheet dated 4.05.2004 on the charge of habitual absence and unauthorized absence for more than ten 
days as per provisions of clause 26.23 and 26.29 of the certified standing order. It is the case of the workman that 
before issuance of chargesheet, Kartik Kora on a previous occasion he had absented from duty from .0.2003 to 
25..2003. Due to his absence from duty, a chargesheet was issued and he submitted his reply. Management 
conducted an enquiry and on being satisfied with reply, the management allowed him to join his duty vide order No. 
3868 dated 03/04.03.2004. The letter allowing him to join his duty was not served upon Kartik Kora and he was 
unable to join his duty. On visiting the Colliery, the workman learned that the management vide order No. 3868 dated 
03/04.03.2004 allowed him to join but on his reporting for duty the management issued another chargesheet against 
him bearing No. ECL/JKN/AGENT/P&IR/04/64l dated 4.05.2004. The workman participated in the enquiry and 
submitted his treatment papers. After holding an enquiry, the management recommended punishment of dismissal 
against the workman and the General Manager issued an order of dismissal vide Ref. No. 
SAT/PER/GM/Termination/l2/44 dated 28/29.03.20l2. After his dismissal from service, Kartik Kora submitted a 
mercy petition on the basis of the Memorandum of Settlement dated 22.05.2007. The management did not consider 
his mercy petition and did not allow him to join. The workman asserted that the order of dismissal passed against him 
is disproportionate to the nature of charge. The workman claimed that he should be allowed to join his duty by 
imposing some lighter punishment. 


[भाग II—ave 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : फरवरी 28, 2026/फाल्गुन 9, 947 88] 


3. The management of ECL contested the case by filing their written statement on 3,06.2023. In their written 
statement, the management contended that performance of Kartik Kora as an UG Loader was very poor due to his 
unauthorized absence from duty in habitual manner. It is the case of the management that prior to issuance of 
chargesheet, the management had issued a letter dated 04.03.2004 to the workman for joining his duty. After he was 
found guilty of unauthorized absence a punishment was awarded by stoppage of two SPRA with cumulative effect, on 
condition that such type of misconduct is not repeated in future. It is the case of the management that immediately 
after issuance of this letter, Kartik Kora committed the same misconduct of unauthorized absence from duty by 
absenting from 26.04.2004. A chargesheet was issued to him for his subsequent absence w.e.f. 26.04.2004 to 
4.05.2004. The workman submitted his reply to the chargesheet after two years and during the said two years, he did 
not send any information to the Colliery management about his inability to submit the reply. Further contention of the 
management is that during the period from 2006 to 20ll, he did not receive any medical treatment at the Colliery’s 
Hospital. As such his treatment by any outside doctor were not considered as authorized treatment. Management 
claimed that Kartik Kora lacked interest in work and was indifferent in attending his duty. The management held 
enquiry in respect of the charges providing reasonable opportunity to the ex-workman. The Enquiry Officer after 
completing enquiry submitted his report holding the charged employee guilty of the charge. A second Show Cause 
Notice was issued to the workman on 2.03.20l2 to which the ex-workman submitted his reply. After considering the 
Enquiry Proceedings, Findings of the Enquiry Officer and evidence along with connected papers, the General 
Manager, Satgram Area dismissed the workman from his service w.e.f. 28/29.03 202. It is urged that the Enquiry 
Proceedings was held in proper manner and the punishment imposed against the workman was justified and 
proportionate. Management urged that the Industrial Dispute has no merit,Kartik Kora is not entitled to any relief of 
reinstatement or back wages and the same is liable to be dismissed. 


4. The short point for consideration in this case is whether dismissal of Kartik Kora from service is legal and 
justified? If not, what relief the workman is entitled to? 


5. The concerned union in support of their case examined Kartik Kora as WW-|. The witness filed an affidavit- 
in-chief reiterating the case in their written statement. In paragraph 3,4 and 8, the witness stated that he requested the 
management to allow him to join duty but the management did not allow him. The witness did not mention any date 
on which he appeared before the management for the purpose of joining his duty. In paragraph |3 of his affidavit-in- 
chief, the witness stated that as per chargesheet, he was absent from duty only for eighteen days i.e. from 26.04.2004 
to 4.05.2004 but the management awarded him the highest punishment of dismissal by treating his absence for a 
period more than that mentioned in the chargesheet. Another contention of the workman, which appears from the 
paragraph 20 of the affidavit in chief is that he submitted mercy petition as per Memorandum of Settlement dated 
22.05.2007 with reference to which the management called him before the General Manager (P&IR) at ECL 
Headquarters on 06.09.20I3 along with his family members but no action has been taken by the authority for his 
reinstatement. During examination-in-chief, the workman produced the following documents: 


(i) Copy of chargesheet dated 4.05.2004 is produced as Exhibit W-|. 

(ii) Copy of reply against chargesheet is produced as Exhibit W-2. 

(0) Copy of application submitted by Kartik Kora for joining duty is produced as Exhibit W-3. 
(iv) Copy of Enquiry Proceedings is produced as Exhibit W-4. 


(v) Copy of application dated 0.08.2007 submitted by Kartik Kora for joining duty is produced as 
Exhibit W-5. 


(vi) Copy of Second Show Cause Notice is produced as Exhibit W-6. 
(vii) Copy of reply to Second Show Cause Notice by the workman is produced as Exhibit W-7. 


(viii) Copy of letter of dismissal issued by General Manager dated 28/29.03.202 is produced as Exhibit 
W-9. 


(ix) Copy of letter dated 5/6.02.208 issued by Sr. Manager (Pers), J.K. Nagar (R) Colliery that 
gratuity and Provident Fund amounts have not been disbursed to the workman, is produced as 
Exhibit W-0. 


It transpired from cross examination of WW-| that the workman claimed to have suffered from cerebral palsy during 
the period of his absence. He admitted that the management granted him opportunity to represent his case during 
enquiry but denied that the management terminated him in proper manner or that he is not entitled to reinstatement. 


6. Mr. Subir Dey, management witness filed his affidavit-in-chief and stated that chargesheet was issued to the 
workman on 4.05.2004 for his unauthorized absence but workman submitted his reply after two years and did not 
inform the management about his inability to attend Office. The witness further stated that Kartik Kora’s residence is 
situated nearby but he was negligent in attending his duty and committed misconduct by absenting from duty. The 
workman produced some medical papers in support of his illness from the year 2006 to 20I] but he did not report to 


862 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : FEBRUARY 28, 2026/PHALGUNA 9, 947 [PART II—SEc. 3(ii)] 


the Colliery Hospital for his treatment and his treatment by outside doctor is considered as unauthorized treatment. It 
is averred that the charge for unauthorized absence under clause 26.29 and habitual absence under clause 26.23 have 
been established against the workman on the basis of the enquiry. The witness further stated that after conclusion of 
enquiry where charge of misconduct was fully established, a second Show Cause Notice was issued to the ex- 
workman on |2.03.20l2 against which he submitted his reply. After careful consideration of all materials and the 
Enquiry Proceedings, findings of the Enquiry Officer, the General Manager issued a letter dated 28/29.03.202 and 
dismissed the workman from his service. It is claimed that the punishment is justified and proportionate to the nature 
of misconduct of the workman. 


In course of his examination-in-chief, the management witness produced the following documents: 
(i) Copy of chargesheet dated 4.05.2002 is produced as Exhibit M-I. 
(ii) Copy of reply submitted by workman is produced as Exhibit M-2. 
(0) Copy of application submitted by Kartik Kora for joining duty is produced as Exhibit M-3. 
(iv) Copy of letter appointing Enquiry Officer is produced as Exhibit M-4. 


(v) Copy of notices of Enquiry dated 30.0l.2006 and 5.03.2006 are produced as Exhibit M-5 and 
Exhibit ४-5/] respectively. 


(vi) Copy of Enquiry Proceedings is produced as Exhibit M-6. 

(vii) Copy of Enquiry Report is produced as Exhibit M-7. 

(viii) | Copy of treatment papers produced by the workman is produced as Exhibit M-8. 

(ix) Copy of Second Show Cause Notice is produced as Exhibit M-9. 

(x) Copy of reply to Second Show Cause Notice by the workman is produced as Exhibit M-0. 


(xi) Copy of letter of dismissal issued by General Manager dated 28/29.03.202 is produced as Exhibit 
M-I]. 


In his cross-examination, the management witness deposed that he was not aware about the outcome of the mercy 
appeal. He also stated that before issuance of chargesheet, the workman remained absent for eighteen days. Witness 
denied the suggestion that the order of dismissal for unauthorized absence of eighteen days is disproportionate 
or harsh. 


7. The salient points of argument advanced by Mr. Rakesh Kumar, union representative is that Kartik Kora was 
suffering from illness and only for his absence for 8 days, from 26.04.2004 to 4.05.2004, a chargesheet (Exhibit 
W-l) was issued against him. The workman submitted his reply to the chargesheet and prayed for allowing him to 
join his duty. He also submitted applications before the Manager, J.K. Nagar Colliery for permitting him to join duty 
but the management did not consider the same. It is argued that after submission of reply, the management waited for 
workman till 2006 and thereafter held enquiry on 27.05.2006. The workman participated in the Enquiry Proceedings 
on 27.05.2006 as he did not receive any notice of enquiry on earlier occasion. During enquiry the workman produced 
medical certificate issued by Doctor to establish that he was indisposed of during the period of absence but 
management without considering such claim held him guilty of the charge of habitual absence and unauthorized 
absence. It is admitted by the union that a second Show Cause Notice (Exhibit W-6/Exhibit M-9) was served upon the 
workman in response to which he submitted his reply which is admitted in evidence by Exhibit W-7/Exhibit M-0, 
wherein he explained the situation which prevented him from attending duty. Mr. Kumar strenuously argued that the 
management did not consider the reason for absence of the charged employee which was due to medical reasons and 
issued an order for dismissal against the workman in an arbitrary manner. It is further argued that the punishment 
imposed is harsh and disproportionate to the nature of misconduct. After his dismissal, the workman submitted a 
mercy petition before the management for his reinstatement in service and in response thereof, the management issued 
a letter dated 27.08.20l3 for appearing before the General Manager (P&IR), ECL Headquarters, Sanctoria for 
personal hearing along with his family members. The management however, did not communicate their decision 
regarding reinstatement. It is argued that the Enquiry Proceedings has been vitiated due to inordinate delay as the 
management kept the Enquiry Proceedings pending from the year 2006 to 20]. It is urged that there has been failure 
of natural justice in holding enquiry and the impugned order of dismissal is liable to be set aside and the workman 
should be reinstated in service and back wages be paid to him for the period he was out of work. 


8. In his reply Mr. P.K. Das, learned advocate for ECL argued that the dismissed workman was a habitual 
absentee. On previous occasions he had enjoyed unauthorized leave and Enquiry Proceedings were held where he was 
found guilty and despite imposition of lighter punishments, the workman did not rectify his conduct. It is submitted 
that due to unauthorized absence of the workman for more than ten days, a chargesheet was issued to him on 
4.05.2004. Copy of chargesheet was served upon the workman through registered post at his residence but he turned 
up after two years to submit his reply on 27.05.2006. The workman was provided reasonable opportunity to 


[भाग II—ave 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : फरवरी 28, 2026/फाल्गुन 9, 947 883 


participate in the Enquiry Proceedings two years after issuance of chargesheet. Referring to the Enquiry Proceedings, 
(Exhibit W-4), learned advocate for the management took me through the statement of Kartik Kora recorded during 
enquiry where he stated that he fell sick from 26.04.2004 to 24.0l.2006 and was declared fit for duty from 
02.09.20I. It is argued that such statement made by Kartik Kora established that he did not turn up for enquiry until 
the year 2006 and even after his recovery from illness, he did not make himself available for his duty until the year 
20Il. It is argued by learned advocate for the management that the wanton nature of the workman has been fully 
established in the course of enquiry held by the Enquiry Officer. Management claimed that the principles of natural 
justice have been duly observed by providing opportunity to the charged employee to establish his case but he failed 
to produce his treatment papers in support of his prolonged illness from the year 2004 to 20l]. It is argued that the 
workman is a habitual absentee and has disrupted the work of the management establishment. It is further argued that 
an opportunity was granted to the workman to respond to the findings of the Enquiry Officer and the workman 
submitted his reply to the second Show Cause Notice (Exhibit W-7) where he admitted his long absence and stated 
that in 2006, he was mentally unsound and he started his treatment from 2006 to 20l. Learned advocate stressed that 
there is no mitigating circumstance in favour of the workman for interfering with the order of dismissal. It is 
submitted that the punishment is justified and proportionate and the Industrial Dispute is liable to be dismissed. 


9. I have considered the rival arguments advanced on behalf of both parties and the claims made in the 
pleadings of the parties as well as the evidence adduced. Admittedly Kartik Kora was employed as U.G. loader at J.K. 
Nagar Colliery under ECL. Chargesheet was issued against the workman on two counts, namely unauthorized 
absence from duty for more than ten days and habitual absence. It appears from the chargesheet that on an earlier 
occasion, the workman had absented from 0.!!.2003 to 25..2003 without any information or leave and after the 
enquiry was held, a punishment was imposed for stoppage of two SPRA with cumulative effect. The second charge 
sheet against the workman was on account of his unauthorized absence from duty from 26.04.2004 till date of 
issuance of chargesheet on 4.05.2004. In paragraph (7) of the affidavit-in-chief, management stated that after 
issuance of chargesheet, the workman submitted his reply after two years. In course of cross-examination, the 
union did not challenge the averment made by the management regarding submission of reply to chargesheet after 
two years. This proves that the workman continued to remain absent from his place of work even beyond the date of 
issuance of chargesheet. Under such circumstances, it is unacceptable that the workman submitted application on 
05.07.2004 (Exhibit W-3) for allowing him to join duty. If such claim of the union is considered to be true, then he 
would have submitted his reply to the chargesheet much earlier, at least on 05.07.2004, the date on which he claims to 
have reported for duty. 


0. On a perusal of the Enquiry Proceedings, I find that the enquiry was initiated on 03.02.2006 i.e. after the 
workman had submitted his reply to the chargesheet. Management representatives, Sri Lallan Singh and Sri B.R. 
Choudhary were present but Kartik Kora did not appear and the enquiry was adjourned to the next date fixed on 
26.04.2006. The workman again remained absent on 26.04.2006 and the enquiry was deferred to 27.05.2006. From 
the Enquiry Report, it is abundantly clear that the workman submitted his reply to chargesheet on 24.0.2006. 
However, on a clear reading of Enquiry Report, I find that enquiry was held on 03.09.20ll. The Enquiry Officer in 
his report stated that the charge sheeted employee admitted the charge levelled against him and finally held that the 
charge under clause 26.23 and clause 26.29 of the Certified Standing Order was proved. The Enquiry Report does not 
bear any date. The disciplinary authority issued a second Show Cause Notice to the workman and the charged 
employee had also replied to the same reiterating his claim that he was suffering from mental illness and was absent 
from duty from 2006 to 20ll. The medical reports produced by the workman has been admitted in evidence as 
Exhibit M-8 series. It is gathered from the certificate issued by Dr. Sapan Kumar Basak, D.M.S. (Cal), a homeopathic 
practitioner that Kartik Kora, son of late Debu Kora was under his treatment from 26.04.2004 to 0.09.20l] and he 
was fit for joining his duty from 02.09.20ll. According to the medical certificate, the person was suffering from 
“Bell’s Palsy” which is a sudden weakness or paralysis on one side of the face. There is no document relating to 
medical treatment of person for such a long period from the year 2004 to 20l. The nature of illness undoubtedly did 
not prevent the workman from reporting about his inability to attend duty. The nature of seriousness of such ailment 
cannot be gauged as the concerned workman absented from duty for seven long years and could not produce his 
treatment papers. The workman thereby failed to satisfy the management about the reasons for his unauthorized 
absence for eighteen days. In his reply to the second Show Cause Notice, the charged employee claimed that he 
absented from duty from the yar 2006 to 20l! but he failed to reason out his absence from 26.04.2004 to 4.05.2004. 
Contradictions in the workman’s case are apparent as in the reply to the second Show Cause Notice (Exhibit W-7) he 
disclosed that he was suffering from mental illness from 2006 to 20ll. Kartik Kora (WW-|) himself stated that he 
was absent for l8 days from 26.04.2004 to 4.05.2004 and in the certificate (Exhibit M-8) of Dr. S.K. Basak it is 
mentioned that Kartik Kora was under his treatment from 26.04.2004 to 0.09.20ll. The conflicting statements 
renders the union’s case doubtful and unreliable. 


ll. Considering the facts and circumstances of this case, I hold that management of ECL has passed the order of 
dismissal against the workman after issuing chargesheet followed by a domestic enquiry where the workman was 
given opportunity to defend his case. The principle of natural justice was duly followed after extending reasonable 
opportunity to the workman. The workman on his part failed to give satisfactory reason for his absence. The medical 
certificate issued by homeopathic doctor for the period of unauthorized absence has not been supported by treatment 


884 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : FEBRUARY 28, 2026/PHALGUNA 9, 947 [PART II—SEc. 3(ii)] 


papers. Besides, the workman is duty bound to inform the employer company about his inability to attend his duty. A 
simple claim of being absent due to illness is not sufficient for reinstating an employee to his work. Therefore, it 
cannot be held that the absence from duty was beyond the control of the workman. Long unauthorized absence 
without information is an appropriate and justified reason and justified for dismissal of an employee by the 
management of the employer company. I find no illegality in the action taken by the management against the 
workman. The Industrial Dispute is bereft of merit and the same is dismissed. 


Hence, 
ORDERED 


The Industrial Dispute is dismissed on contest. The workman is not entitled to any relief of reinstatement or 
back wages. He is however entitled to receive his dues like gratuity and Provident Fund on account of his past service. 
Let an Award be drawn up on the basis of my above findings. Let copies of the Award in duplicate be sent to the 
Ministry of Labour, Government of India, New Delhi for information and Notification. 


ANANDA KUMAR MUKHERJEE, Presiding Officer 
नई दिल्‍ली, 72 फरवरी, 2026

सरकार ई सी एल के प्रबंधतंत्र के संबद्ध नियोजकों और उनके कर्मकारों के बीच, अनुबंध में निर्दिष्ट औद्योगिक विवाद में

REFERENCE CASE NO. 53 OF 2000 
PARTIES: Dependent of Ramswarup Dusadh 
Represented by Koyla Mazdoor Congress 
Vs. 
Management of Siduli Colliery of M/s. ECL 


REPRESENTATIVES: 

For the Workman: Mr. Rakesh Kumar, President, Koyala Mazdoor Congress 
For the Management of ECL: Mr. P.K. Das, Advocate 

INDUSTRY: Coal. 

STATE: West Bengal. 


Dated: 02.2.2025 


[भाग II—ave 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : फरवरी 28, 2026/फाल्गुन 9, 947 885 


AWARD 


On failure of conciliation proceeding, the Government of India through the Ministry of Labour, in exercise 
of powers conferred under clause (d) of Sub-section (l) and Sub-section (2A) of Section |0 of the Industrial Disputes 
Act, 947 (4 of 947), vide its Order No. L-2202/50/99/IR(CM-ID dated 28.06.2000 has referred the following 
dispute between the employer, that is the Management of Siduli Colliery of M/s. Eastern Coalfields Limited 
(hereinafter referred as ECL) and their workman for adjudication by this Tribunal. 


SCHEDULE 


“Whether the action of the management of Siduli Colliery of M/s. ECL in not providing employment to the 
dependent of Sh. Ramswarup Dusadh is legal & justified? If not, to what relief the workman is entitled ? ” 


Li. On receiving Order No. L-2202/50/99/IR(CM-II) dated 28.06.2000 from the Government of India, 
Ministry of Labour, New Delhi for adjudication of the dispute, a Reference case No. 53 of 2000 was registered on 
0.08.2000/7.0.200l and an order was passed for issuing notice to the parties through registered post, directing 
them to appear and submit their written statements along with relevant documents in support of their claims and a list 
of witnesses. 


2. Koyala Mazdoor Congress filed written statement on 24.07.2002 on behalf of the dependents of Ramswarup 
Dusadh, employee of M/s. Eastern Coalfields Limited. The fact of the case in brief, as disclosed in the written 
statement of the union is that Ramswarup Dusadh was a permanent employee of ECL at Siduli Colliery. He applied 
for voluntary retirement on medical ground under clause 9.4.3 of NCWA-III. The workman was declared medically 
unfit for the job. According to the provisions of NCWA, one dependent family member of the workman is entitled to 
get employment. He applied for providing employment to the dependent. After screening was held at Area level, the 
proposal for employment of dependent was forwarded to ECL Headquarters which is still pending. The GM (P&IR), 
ECL directed to put up the file before him but the file for employment was not put up and the proposal was not 
approved. It is contended that the family of the workman is facing starvation as no dependent of Ramswarup Dusadh 
has been provided employment in the company after he was declared unfit and was out of employment. It has been 
prayed that the dependent son of Ramswarup Dusadh should be provided with employment as his father has been 
terminated from his employment on the ground of medically unfitness. 


3. The Agent, Siduli Colliery, representing the management of ECL filed a written statement on 07.04.20I5 
wherein it is stated that the dispute raised by the union is misconceived. According to the management, Ramswarup 
Dusadh applied for voluntary retirement on medical ground and he was examined by the Medical Board. A 
confidential complaint was received by the competent authority that medical examination was not held properly and 
on the basis of such information, management withdrew the findings of the Medical Board before its final acceptance 
and the concerned workman was directed to continue in his job. It is urged that union cannot raise an Industrial 
Dispute over non-acceptance of the findings of the medical board and there is no illegality on the part of the 
management by not accepting the findings of the medical board regarding fitness of the concerned workman. It is 
asserted that the workman was not terminated from service on the basis of medical report of his unfitness and he is 
not entitled to get any relief in this case. 


4. The only witness examined in this case is Kalamdev Dusadh, son of Ramswarup Dusadh. He filed an 
affidavit-in-chief stating that his father Ramswarup Dusadh applied for voluntary retirement under the provision of 
clause 9.4.0 of NCWA. The management accepted the request and referred his father to appear before the Medical 
Board constituted by the employer company. The Medical Board declared his father unfit for work and the 
management of Siduli Colliery terminated the service of his father. The witness further stated in his affidavit that his 
father nominated him for employment and submitted all documents before the management. After proper screening at 
the Colliery level and Area level and medical examination by the IME Board, the proposal for employment of 
Kalamdev Dusadh was forwarded to the ECL Headquarters but no employment was provided to him. The witness 
stated that management violated clause 9.4.3 of NCWA by not providing employment to the dependent son. In course 
of his evidence, witness did not produce a single document in support of his relationship with Ramswarup Dusadh. 
There is nothing to show that Ramswarup Dusadh was found medically unfit by any Medical Board. No order of 
termination of Ramswarup Dusadh on medical ground has been produced by the union. The union also failed to 
produce any document showing that either Ramswarup Dusadh or Kalamdev Dusadh submitted any application 
before the management claiming employment against premature termination of the workman from his service on 
medical ground. 


5. The workman witness in his cross-examination by the management deposed that his name has been recorded 
in the service record of his father but I find that no service record of Ramswarup Dusadh has been produced. Witness 
further stated that he has no paper to show that he is the son of Ramswarup Dusadh. The witness deposed that all 
other family members furnished their No objection in his favour but no such document has come forth. Evidence of 
witness reveals that he is a married person and 48 years of age in 20l7. He has two daughters, one of which is already 
married. Witness denied the suggestion that he is not entitled to employment as a dependent of Ramswarup Dusadh. 


886 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : FEBRUARY 28, 2026/PHALGUNA 9, 947 [PART II—SEc. 3(ii)] 


6. After several opportunities, management did not adduce any evidence. On perusal of record, I find that due 
to absence of both parties, no order was passed on 23.05.2006 and at the instance of the union, the case was restored 
by order dated 4.03.20l3. After recording of evidence of the sole workman witness, none of the dependents of 
Ramswarup Dusadh appeared before this Tribunal in support of Kalamdev’s claim for employment. On 02.06.2025, 
the case was finally taken up for its disposal on the basis of material on record. Union produced a copy of clarification 
of operation of clause 9.4.3 of NCWA in their circular bearing No. CIL:C-5B:NCWA-IV/3207 dated 3.09.994. It 
laid down three circumstances where employment was to be provided to the dependents of such employees declared 
medically unfit prior to April, [994 i.e. prior to imposition of moratorium on operation of clause 9.4.3. The second 
situation was cases which were already referred to appellate medical board but were not decided on account of the 
moratorium on operation of clause 9.4.3 of NCWA-IV which could thereafter be disposed of and decided and the 
third category of cases were in which Medical Boards were already held but results were not declared on account of 
the moratorium. In such cases, the same should be decided immediately and further action was to be taken 
accordingly. 


2 In the present case, the union has not ventured to disclose any particular date on which Ramswarup Dusadh 
applied for being declared medically unfit and it is also not disclosed if Ramswarup Dusadh was at all terminated 
from his service on account of his unfitness. The witness produced by the union was unable to produce a single 
document of his relationship with employee in support of his claim for employment. None of the family members of 
the employee concerned have been examined to establish that Ramswarup Dusadh wasprematurely terminated from 
service on account of his medical debility and Kalamdev Dusadh was nominated as the dependent son for 
employment. 


8. For the purpose of entertaining any claim for employment on the ground of medical unfitness of any 
workman, there is a condition that he should have atleast two years left in his service. In the present case, no such 
evidence has been adduced. It is an admitted fact that the competent authority imposed a moratorium on clause 9.4.3 
for providing employment to the dependents of employees who were declared medically unfit. According to the 
management, confidential complaint had been received regarding irregularities committed by Medical Board in 
declaring employees as medically unfit. There is absolute dearth of evidence to establish that Ramswarup Dusadh was 
declared medically unfit or the same was accepted by the competent authority before the imposition of moratorium or 
that Kalamdev Dusadh fulfilled the criteria for his employment as dependent against premature termination of 
Ramswarup Dusadh. Therefore, I do not find any merit in the claim of the union. Accordingly, I hold that the action 
of management of Siduli Colliery in not providing employment to Kalamdev Dusadh suffers no illegality. The 
Industrial Dispute is therefore dismissed on contest. 


Hence, 
ORDERED 


The Industrial Dispute is dismissed on contest. Kalamdev Dusadh is not entitled to any employment as per 
clause 9.4.3 of NCWA-IV. Let an Award be drawn up on basis of my above findings. Let copies of the Award in 
duplicate be sent to the Ministry of Labour, Government of India, New Delhi for information and Notification. 


ANANDA KUMAR MUKHERJEE, Presiding Officer

of ECL and their workmen, received by the Central Government on |/02/2026.

SALONI, Dy. Director/Link officer 


[भाग II—ave 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : फरवरी 28, 2026/फाल्गुन 9, 947 887

REFERENCE CASE NO. 39 OF 209 
PARTIES: Mohan Dhangar 
Vs. 
Management of Khas Kajora Colliery, Kajora Area of M/s. ECL 


REPRESENTATIVES: 

For the Workman: Mr. Rakesh Kumar, President, Koyala Mazdoor Congress 
For the Management of ECL: Mr. P.K. Das, Advocate 

INDUSTRY: Coal. 

STATE: West Bengal. 

Dated: 4..2025 


AWARD 


On failure of conciliation proceeding the Government of India through the Ministry of Labour, in exercise of 
powers conferred under clause (d) of Sub-section (l) and Sub-section (2A) of Section 0 of the Industrial Disputes 
Act, 947 (74 of 947), vide its Order No. L-2202/94/209-IR(CM-ID) dated 9.2.209 has referred the following 
dispute between the employer, that is the Management of Khas Kajora Colliery, Kajora Area of M/s. Eastern 
Coalfields Limited (hereinafter referred as ECL) and their workman for adjudication by this Tribunal. 


SCHEDULE 


“(i) Whether the management of ECL adopted principal of natural justice in imposing punishment vide letter 
No. KA/APM (IC)/C-6/Abst/25/2844 dated 27-5-20/52 


(ti) Whether the amount of punishment in letter No. KA/APM (IC)/C-6/Abst/25/2844 dated 27-5-20/5 is 
proportionate in the eyes of Law? If not, what relief the concerned workman is entitled to and from which date? ” 


A corrigendum dated 20.06.2023 was received whereby the last line of terms of reference was modified as “If not, 
what relief the workman Sri Mohan Dhangar is entitled to and from which date?” 


l. On receiving Order No. L-2202/94/209-IR(CM-II) dated 9.2.209 from the Government of India, 
Ministry of Labour, New Delhi for adjudication of the dispute, a Reference case No. 39 of 209 was registered on 
3.2.209 and an order was passed for issuing notice to the parties through registered post, directing them to appear 
and submit their written statements along with relevant documents in support of their claims and a list of witnesses. 


2. Mohan Dhangar filed written statement in this case through the President, Koyala Mazdoor Congress on 
26.0.2022. Management contested the case by filing written statement on 30.2.2022. Fact of the case as narrated in 
the written statement of the workman is that Mohan Dhangar was a permanent employee of Khas Kajora Colliery 
under Kajora Area, designated as Timber Mazdoor Cat-HI. Management of ECL without any written order deployed 
him to work as an attendance clerk. The workman performed his duty without any complain against him. 
Subsequently chargesheet No. 603 dated 04.05.20I5 was issued by the management against Mohan Dhangar for 
recording attendance of Dudhnath Singh, Tyndle on 03.05.20I5 while he was absent from duty on that date. Mohan 
Dhangar submitted reply to the chargesheet disclosing that as per practice in the Colliery, he marked the attendance of 
Dudhnath Singh as present on the basis of information received from Panchanan Chouhan, Tyndle Zamadar that 
Dudhnath Singh and other Tyndle were present and engaged in their work. After marking the attendance of Dudhnath 
Singh, the Tyndle Zamadar informed him that Dudhnath Singh had left the place of work so he deleted his attendance. 
At the time of closing the attendance register, the total count of manpower present was correct and the attendance of 
Dudhnath Singh was not included in the total counting of attendance on that date. Therefore, no theft, fraud or 
dishonesty was committed in connection with employer’s business or property. It was further stated that Mohan 
Dhangar did not willfully neglect his work. Management of ECL not being satisfied with the reply of the charged 
employee initiated a domestic enquiry against him. Mohan Dhanghar participated in the enquiry. According to the 
charged workman, there is a practice in the Colliery to record attendance on basis of oral information of the Head of 


888 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : FEBRUARY 28, 2026/PHALGUNA 9, 947 [PART II—SEc. 3(ii)] 


the Department as workers were deployed at different places due to urgent work and due to over crowding at the time 
of marking attendance at the attendance room, the workman came after sometime. If the concerned workman did not 
turn up in person before closing of attendance register for that particular shift, then his attendance used to be 
deleted by Attendance Clerk. 


3. During enquiry, Panchanan Chouhan, Tyndle Zamadar made statement before the Enquiry Officer and 
supported the case of Mohan Dhangar. The Enquiry Officer concluding his enquiry found Mohan Dhangar guilty of 
charge of wilful neglect of work and management awarded punishment of demotion to Mohan Dhangar by reverting 
him to the post of General Mazdoor Cat-I instead of maintaining his post of Timer Mazdoor Cat-III. The workman 
contended that the punishment awarded to him is disproportionate to the nature of offence. There was no mistake on 
his part. The workman prayed for setting aside the punishment of demotion from Timer Mazdoor Cat-III to General 
Mazdoor Cat-I. The workman prayed for restoration of his original post and basic wage as he was getting at the time 
of awarding punishment and further prayed for payment of difference of wages along with annual increments from the 
date of punishment. 


4 Management in their written statement has urged that Mohan Dhangar working as attendance clerk had 
marked attendance of Dudhnath Singh, Tyndle in the first shift of 03.05.20l5. Complaint was received that false 
attendance has been marked on request of Panchanan Chouhan, Tyndle Zamadar, Pit No. l0 and the attendance was 
not recorded on basis of physical presence and later the same was corrected by him in ‘C’ Form Register. 
Management issued chargesheet No. KKC/P&IR/C-6/CS/5/603 dated 04.05.20l5 against Mohan Dhangar under 
clause 26.l for committing “theft, fraud or dishonesty in connection with the employer’s business or property” and 
under clause 26.5 for “willful neglect of work” of the Certified Standing Order applicable to Coal Industries. The 
matter was referred to domestic enquiry and the workman participated in the enquiry on |4.05.20I5. The Enquiry 
Officer followed the principles of natural justice while holding enquiry and gave full opportunity to the charged 
employee to defend himself. According to the management, the charge under clause 26.5 for “willful neglect of work” 
was established against the workman without any doubt. A second Show Cause Notice was issued bearing Ref. No. — 
KKC/P&IR/C-6/5/653 dated 8.05.205. The Area Personnel Manager (IC), Kajora Area, the Disciplinary Authority 
thereafter on basis of the findings of Enquiry Officer allowed the workman to resume his duty but imposed 
punishment of “demotion to the post of General Mazdoor Cat-I without any pay protection and he shall not be 
deployed as attendance clerk” 


5. The workman was permitted to join and Mohan Dhangar appealed before the General Manager (P&IR), ECL 
Headquarter for review of his punishment. The same was forwarded by Sr. Manager (Personnel) (IR) to Assistant 
Personnel Manager (Kajora Area) with a request to send the certified copy of the enquiry proceeding and the report of 
disciplinary action taken against him. The Area Personnel Manager, Kajora Area in his letter dated 3/I8.08.2020 
forwarded the Enquiry Report, Enquiry Proceedings, Second Show Cause Notice chargesheet and other relevant 
papers to GM (P&IR), ECL Headquarters but the same were returned with a comment that “As per clause 30 of 
Certified Standing Order applicable to all establishments of ECL, the case cannot be considered for review, so, it is 
regretted”. Management claimed that the action of the management was totally justified and the punishment imposed 
was proportionate to the misconduct. It is strongly urged that workman is not entitled to any relief and the Industrial 
Dispute is liable to be dismissed. 


6. Mohan Dhangar has been examined as WW-]. He filed his affidavit-in-chief and reiterated his case. The 
workman denied having committed any fraud, theft or dishonesty and also ascertained that he has never neglected his 
work willfully. In his affidavit-in-chief, he stated that he believed the Tyndle Zamadar marked attendance of 
Dudhnath Singh. After some time, he deleted the attendance and total number of persons who were physically present 
at work was correct. He alsostated that Dudhnath Singh was not included in the total number of workman attending 
duty on that day. Further statement of the workman is that it is a practice in Colliery to mark the attendance of 
workers on the basis of information of the Head of the Department because sometimes workers are deployed to work 
in some urgent job. The witness stated that no monetary loss was caused to the company. In his affidavit, WW-| 
stated that Panchanan Chouhan, Tyndal Zamadar made statement before the Enquiry Officer that attendance of 
Dudhnath Singh was marked on the basis of information given by him and when it was informed by him that 
Dudhnath Singh did not perform his duty due to some work in his quarter, his name was deleted from the attendance 
register. The workman stated that he suffered a huge financial loss as he was awarded a harsh and extreme 
punishment. He was receiving basic wage of Rs. !33.64 per day as Timber Mazdoor Cat-III whereas on his 
demotion to General Mazdoor in Cat-I, he was receiving Rs. 604.33 as initial basic of Cat-I. 


During examination-in-chief, the workman produced the following documents: 
(i) Copy of chargesheet dated 04.05.20I5 is produced as Exhibit W-I. 
(ii) Copy of reply against chargesheet dated 06.05.205 is produced as Exhibit W-2. 
() Copy of Enquiry Proceedings is produced as Exhibit W-3. 
(iv) Copy of Enquiry Report along with findings is produced as Exhibit W-4. 


[भाग II—ave 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : फरवरी 28, 2026/फाल्गुन 9, 947 889 


(५) Copy of letter imposing punishment of demotion is produced as Exhibit W-5. 


(vi) Copy of application dated 08.06.205 for reviewing quantum of punishment is produced as 
Exhibit W-6. 


(vii) Copy of application dated 0.06.205 for reviewing punishment is produced as Exhibit W-7. 


(viii) | Copy of proposal dated 27.07.205 submitted by Agent, Khas Kajora Colliery to General Manager, 
Kajora Area for reviewing punishment is produced as Exhibit W-8. 


(ix) Copy of proposal dated 0.0.205 submitted by Agent, Khas Kajora Colliery to General Manager, 
Kajora Area for reviewing punishment is produced as Exhibit W-9. 


(x) Copy of appeal dated 27.0!.206 is produced as Exhibit W-0. 


(xi) Copy of reply dated 8.02.20I6 in response to appeal dated 27.0.20I6 is produced as 
Exhibit W-I. 


(xii) Copy of application dated 28.02.207 addressed to the Chairman-cum-Managing Director, ECL, 
Sanctoria for reviewing punishment is produced as Exhibit W-2. 


In his cross examination, the witness denied the charge levelled against him or that the punishment rewarded against 
him is justified or proportionate to the misconduct allegedly committed by him. 


ae Mr. Proloy Dasgupta has been examined as MW-|. He filed an affidavit-in-chief wherein he stated that 
Mohan Dhangar working as attendance clerk, falsely marked attendance of Dudhnath Singh, Tyndle, Pit No. 0 in the 
first shift on 03.05.20l5 and the same was not based upon physical presence of Dudhnath Singh but with some 
ulterior motive and later the same was deleted and corrected by him. 


In course of his examination-in-chief, the management witness produced the following documents: 
(i) Copy of chargesheet dated 04.05.20I5 is produced as Exhibit M-I. 
(ii) Copy of reply against chargesheet dated 06.05.205 is produced as Exhibit M-2. 
() Copy of notice of enquiry dated .05.205 is produced as Exhibit M-3. 
(iv) Copy of letter appointing Enquiry Officer is produced as Exhibit M-3/I. 
(v) Copy of Enquiry Proceedings is produced as Exhibit M-4. 
(vi) Copy of Enquiry Report along with findings is produced as Exhibit M-5. 
(vii) Copy of second Show Cause Notice issued to workman is produced as Exhibit M-6. 
(viii) | Copy of letter imposing punishment of demotion is produced as Exhibit M-7. 
(ix) Copy of appeal preferred by the workman for reviewing punishment is produced as Exhibit M-8. 


(x) Copy of letter which informed that case could not be considered for review as per clause 30 of 
Certified Standing Order is produced as Exhibit M-9. 


(xi) Copy of complaint letter submitted by Ranjit Kumar Bhuia to the Agent, Khas Kajora Colliery is 
produced as Exhibit M-0. 


In his cross-examination, the management witness admitted that no letter was issued in favour of the workman asking 
him to work as attendance clerk and he was working as attendance clerk on the basis of verbal direction of the 
Manager of the Colliery. Witness stated that there is no prevailing practice of marking attendance of workman on the 
basis of information from higher authority. Witness stated that he could not produce any report which would reveal 
that attendance of Dudhnath Singh was initially recorded by Mohan Dhangar on 03.05.20I5 and thereafter it was 
cancelled. Witness further deposed that one Ranjit Kumar Bhuia had lodged written complaint before the Agent, Khas 
Kajora Colliery that Mohan Dhangar recorded false attendance of Dudhnath Singh on 03.05.20I5 though he was out 
of the Colliery and the copy of the said complaint has been produced as Exhibit M-l0. Witness deposed that Enquiry 
Officer did not examine Ranjit Kumar Bhuia. The management witness could not produce any document to show that 
a second Show Cause Notice was served upon the charged employee. Witness also failed to state if the employer 
faced any financial loss due to alleged neglect of work by Mohan Dhangar. 


8. The central point for consideration is whether the charge has been established against Mohan Dhangar and 
whether the findings of Enquiry Officer is justified and proper? If so, whether the amount of punishment imposed 
against him is proper and what relief the workman is entitled to. 


9. Mr. Rakesh Kumar, union representative arguing the case for the workman submitted that Mohan Dhangar 
was posted as Timber Mazdoor Cat-III when he was asked to function as attendance clerk. On 03.05.20I5, the 
workman on basis of information received from Panchanan Chouhan, Tyndle Zamadar recorded the attendance of 


890 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : FEBRUARY 28, 2026/PHALGUNA 9, 947 [PART II—SEc. 3(ii)] 


Dudhnath Singh who was later found absent and his name was deleted from the day’s attendance. The management 
did not suffer any loss on account of recording of attendance. Furthermore, the person whose attendance was initially 
recorded was not counted at the time of closing attendance. It is submitted that Tyndle Zamadar was examined as 
Defence Witness and he supported the case of Mohan Dhangar that on basis of his information about Dudhnath 
Singh, his attendance was registered but was subsequently rectified. Mr. Kumar argued that there was no malice nor 
any illegality on part of the workman. Therefore, the workman could not have been found guilty of charge and the 
punishment meted out to him is disproportionate. The union representative strongly urged that the workman who has 
been demoted fromTimber Mazdoor Cat-III to General Mazdoor Cat-I should be restored to his original post and the 
difference of pay of Timber Mazdoor and General Mazdoor from the period of such punishment should be paid to the 
workman. 


0. Management argued that charge under clause 26.5 of the Certified Standing Order against the workman has 
been well established before the Enquiry Officer. The workman in his statement before the Enquiry Officer has 
admitted that he has recorded the attendance of Dudhnath Singh without his physical attendance. Learned advocate 
argued that the misconduct committed was wilful on the part of the workman as he acted in a dishonest manner. 
Learned advocate argued that the workman has been allowed to continue in his service but due to such willfull neglect 
and irresponsible act on his part, the management demoted him from Timber Mazdoor Cat-II to General Mazdoor 
Cat-I. Learned advocate argued that the workman had appealed before the higher authority but his case was not 
considered. 


ll. I have considered the rival arguments advanced in the background of pleadings submitted and evidence 
adduced by the parties. It is evident from the testimony of MW-I, that no letter of appointment or deployment was 
issued to Mohan Dhangar to function as attendance clerk. The management of ECL therefore has not acted in proper 
manner by verbally directing a General Mazdoor to take up the function and responsibility of an attendance clerk. It 
transpires from the cross-examination of the management witness that on the basis of complaint from Ranjit Kumar 
Bhuia, a co-worker, the Agent, Khas Kajora Colliery, noted that Mohan Dhangar recorded false attendance of 
Dudhnath Singh on 03.05.20I5 and a domestic enquiry was initiated over such matter where Ranjit Kumar Bhuia, the 
complainant was not examined. Copy of chargesheet has been produced as Marked as Exhibit. W-l/M-. It appears 
that the charge was levelled against the workman under clause 26.] and clause 26.5 of the Certified Standing Order. 
Mohan Dhangar received copy of chargesheet and submitted his reply to the chargesheet where he admitted that he 
had marked attendance of Dudhnath Singh, Tyndle on 03.05.205 and after one hour, he deleted and corrected the 
attendance of the person concerned. Notice of enquiry was issued to the workman and he participated in the enquiry. 
Enquiry Proceedings has been produced as Exhibit W-3 where the charged employee has admitted that he recorded 
the attendance of Dudhnath Singh and then deleted the same by his pen on 03.05.20I5. In course of enquiry, 
Panchanan Chouhan was examined as DW-|. A question was put to him as to whether Dudhnath Singh requested him 
to arrange for marking his attendance to which his answer was ‘No’. The witness was further questioned as to whether 
he had any authority or power to mark anybody’s attendance, which he answered in the negative. It is clearly 
established that Panchanan Chouhan had no authority to ask Mohan Dhangar to record anybody’s attendance, nor did 
Dudhnath Singh inform him that he would join duty or his attendance had to be recorded. Therefore, a person who 
had no delegation of such power did not have any authority to ask Mohan Dhangar to record attendance of Dudhnath 
Singh who was absent on that date. On examination of Mohan Dhangar by the Enquiry Officer, he answered that his 
act was wrong in the sense that he did not mark the attendance on basis of physical presence of the workman. 


2. In course of enquiry, on examination of MW-| Tarun Mukherjee stated that on previous occasion attendance 
was not recorded on the request of Tyndle Zamadar. From the material on record, I find that it was not a prevailing 
practice to record attendance of a person who remained absent. It was only after lodging of the complaint that the 
matter was looked into and the attendance of Dudhnath Singh was deleted on 03.05.205. The act on the part of the 
workman was unfair and unjust which amounts to misconduct on account of wilful neglect of duty. The management 
of ECL has appropriately found him guilty of the charge of wilful neglect of work. The charge of theft, fraud and 
dishonesty was not proved. I find from the Enquiry Proceeding that reasonable opportunity was granted to the 
workman and the principles of natural justice was followed in the course of enquiry and no prejudice was caused to 
the workman. Mohan Dhangar appealed before the Chairman-cum-Managing Director, for review of punishment 
against him. Manager, Khas Kajora Colliery issued a second Show Cause Notice to Mohan Dhangar on I8.05.205 
providing him an opportunity to explain him within 72 hours of receipt of the letter as to why disciplinary action shall 
not be taken against him for his misconduct of willful neglect of work and the copy of Enquiry Proceedings and report 
were served upon him. The Area Personnel Manager (I/C), Kajora Area in his letter dated 27.05.205 addressed to the 
Agent, Khas Kajora Colliery informed that the disciplinary authority of Kajora Area after going through the findings 
of enquiry and considering the material facts has imposed a punishment of demotion of Mohan Dhangar, Timber 
Mazdoor to the post of General Mazdoor without any pay protection and he should not be deployed to the post of 
Attendance Clerk. A reasoned order of the disciplinary authority awarding the punishment of demotion to Mohan 
Dhangar has not been produced in this case. The Area Personnel Manager (I/C), Kajora Area is not the disciplinary 
authority. The workman has already suffered demotion to the post of General Mazdoor since May 20I5 and his pay 
has not been protected. The workman has suffered financial loss on account of his demotion for ten years. In the 
instant case the notesheet reflecting the approval of disciplinary authority has not been produced for consideration by 


[भाग [ave 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : फरवरी 28, 2026/फाल्गुन 9, 947 89] 


this Tribunal. Under such facts and circumstances, I hold that the punishment of demotion to the post of General 
Mazdoor, Cat-I should not continue any further. The workman should be restored to his original post of Timber 
Mazdoor with protection of pay within one month of communication of this Award. The findings of the Enquiry 
Officer that the charge of wilful neglect of work under clause 26.5 of Certified Standing Order levelled against 
Mohan Dhangar (acting as attendance clerk) is proved beyond any doubt is upheld and not interfered with. However, 
considering the period of punishment already undergone by the workman, the order of punishment issued by the Area 
Personnel Manager (IC), Kajora Area is modified by restoring Mohan Dhangar to his original post of Timber 
Mazdoor within one month of date of communication of this order. He shall get his usual pay attached to his post but 
shall not be deployed as attendance clerk in future. 


Hence, 
ORDERED 


The Industrial Dispute is allowed in part against the management of ECL. The order of holding Mohan 
Dhangar guilty of charge under clause 26.5 of Certified Standing Order is upheld. Considering the period of demotion 
undergone by the charged employee, the order of punishment communicated by the Area Personnel Manager (IC), 
Kajora Area dated 27.05.205 is modified by directing restoration of Mohan Dhangar to his original post of Timber 
Mazdoor Cat-III as well as restoring his pay as attached to the post. However, he shall not be deployed as attendance 
clerk in future. Let copies of the Award in duplicate be sent to the Ministry of Labour, Government of India, New 
Delhi for information and Notification. 


ANANDA KUMAR MUKHERJEE, Presiding Officer 
नई दिल्‍ली, 72 फरवरी, 2026

Government hereby publishes the Award (Reference. 0/2024) of the Central Government Industrial Tribunal-

L. C. APPLICATION NO. 0। OF 2024 
PARTIES: Binod Kumar Yadav 
Vs. 
Agent, Ningah Group of Mines, ECL and 2 Others. 

REPRESENTATIVES: 
For the Union/Workmen: Mr. Aninda Chakraborty, Advocate. 
For the Management: Mr. Prakash Yadav, Advocate. 


INDUSTRY: Coal. 
STATE: West Bengal. 
Dated: 08.2.2025 


892 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : FEBRUARY 28, 2026/PHALGUNA 9, 947 [PART II—SEc. 3(ii)] 


AWARD 


l. Binod Kumar Yadav, petitioner has appeared in person accompanied by Mr. Aninda Chakraborty, learned 
advocate. This is an Application under Section 33C(2) of the Industrial Disputes Act, !947 wherein petitioner has 
claimed Rs. 6,26,636.42/- (Rupees six lakh twenty-six thousand six hundred thirty-six and forty-two paise) from the 
employer Respondents No. | to 3 i.e., (I) the Agent of Ningah Group of Mines, (2) Eastern Coalfields Limited 
represented by the Chairman -cum- Managing Director, and (3) the General Manager of Sripur Area. 


2. Mr. Prakash Yadav, learned advocate for the Management of Eastern Coalfields Limited filed a fresh 
Vokalatnama for all three Opposite Parties and also filed two written statements on behalf of the General Manager of 
Satgram Area and the constituted attorney of the Chairman -cum- Managing Director of Eastern Coalfields Limited. 
Copy served. A separate application is filed by the opposite parties praying time for hearing on the point of 
maintainability of the application. In the written statement management has contended that the claim under Section 
33C(2) of the Industrial Disputes Act, 947 allows a workman to apply before the Labour Court for recovery of 
money as benefits which is due to him on account of enforcing a recognised right, a pre-existing benefit or a 
settlement or award. In this case claim by the petitioner is disputed as workman is residing in company’s 
accommodation and drawing House Rent Allowance, thereby causing loss to the company. 


3. Petitioner filed a verified application through advocate stating that he wants to file an Industrial Dispute 
before the Regional Labour Commissioner and prayed for necessary order permitting him to withdraw the instant 
application. Heard learned advocates for the petitioner and management. Considered. This is a clear case where the 
petitioner has claimed payment of some monetary benefits which is contingent upon an undecided question as to 
whether he is actually entitled to such House Rent Allowance, which is deducted from his salary on account of 
allotment of quarters to him. A claim under Section 33C(2) of the Industrial Disputes Act, [947 can be proceeded 
with if there is a pre-existing right or benefit in favour of the petitioner and such benefit has been decided by way of 
settlement or award. In the present case there is no previous settlement over this matter and therefore, this Application 
under Section 33C(2) of the Industrial Disputes Act, !947 is not maintainable. Prayer of the petitioner is considered 
and he is granted leave to take steps to raise an Industrial Disputes before the Conciliation Officer. Application under 
Section 33C(2) of the Industrial Disputes Act, 947 is dismissed on being withdrawn. Let an Award be drawn up. 


Hence, 
ORDERED 


Let an Award be passed in view of the above discussion. Let Copies of the Award in duplicate be 
sent to the Ministry of Labour, Government of India, New Delhi under Section 33C(4) of the Industrial Dispute 
Act, !947 for information and Notification. 


ANANDA KUMAR MUKHERJEE, Presiding Officer
