REFERENCE CASE NO. 6l OF 2004 
PARTIES: Ram Hari Nayak 
(dependent son of Late Narayan Nayak) 
Vs. 
Management of Madhabpur Colliery, ECL 
REPRESENTATIVES: 
For the Union/Workman: Mr. Rakesh Kumar, President, Koyala Mazdoor Congress. 
For the Management of ECL: Mr. P. K. Das, Advocate. 
INDUSTRY: Coal. 
STATE: West Bengal. 
Dated: 02.2.2025 
AWARD 


In exercise of powers conferred under clause (d) of Sub-section (l) and Sub-section (2A) of Section 0 of the 
Industrial Disputes Act, 947 (/4 of 947), the Government of India through the Ministry of Labour, vide its Order 
No. L-2202/374/2003-IR(CM-II) dated 28.0.2004 has been pleased to refer the following dispute between the 
employer, that is the Management of Madhabpur Colliery under Kajora Area of Eastern Coalfields Limited and their 
workman for adjudication by this Tribunal. 


SCHEDULE 


“Whether the action of the management of Madhavpur Colliery under Kajora Area of M/s Eastern 
Coalfields Limited in denying employment to the dependant son of Late Narayan Nayak, U.G. Loader, UM. 
No. 548284 under N.C.W.A V is legal and justified? If not, to what relief the dependant son of the deceased 
workman is entitled? ” 


On receiving Order No. L-2202/374/2003-IR(CM-II) dated 28.0.2004 from the Government of India 
Ministry of Labour, New Delhi for adjudication of the dispute, a Reference case was registered on 08..2004 and an 
order was passed for issuing notice to the parties through registered post, directing them to appear and submit their 
written statements along with relevant documents in support of their claims. 


2. Koyala Mazdoor Congress appearing for the dependent of Late Narayan Nayak, ex-employee of Eastern 
Coalfields Limited (hereinafter referred to as ECL) filed written statement on 8.02.2005. Fact of the case which 
unfolds from the written statement of the union is that Narayan Nayak was a permanent employment of ECL and 
was posted at Madhabpur Colliery under Kajora Area as an Underground Loader bearing U.M. No. 548284. Narayan 
Nayak died in harness on 05.0.995, leaving behind his wife, Lalita Nayak and a minor son. On 03.03.995 the 
widow applied for her employment as her son was fifteen (!5) years old. She submitted her documents in support of 
her claim for employment. Lalita Nayak appeared before the Screening Committee at the Area Level and was also 
examined by Initial Medical Examination Board (hereinafter referred to as IME Board). On 5.03.996 after Medical 
Examination her age was assessed as forty-nine (49) years. As Lalita Nayak exceeded the age of forty-five (45) years 
on the date of application she was not provided with employment. The management issued letter bearing no. 
KA/PM/C-6/35/293/599 dated 0/07.0.996 informing that Lalita Nayak would be paid maintenance allowance of 
Rs. 2000/- (Rupees two thousand only) in lieu of employment as per Clause 9.5.0 of National Coal Wage Agreement 
— V (hereinafter referred to as NCWA-V). On 23.0.996 the wife of the deceased employee requested the 
management that she is not willing to accept monetary compensation in lieu of employment and further requested to 
provide employment to her son Ram Hari Nayak on his attaining the age of eighteen years as per Clause 9.5.0 (iii) of 
NCWA-V. Once again on 07..996 Lalita Nayak submitted a representation before the management of Madhabpur 
Colliery for providing employment to her son and that she is not willing to accept monetary compensation in lieu of 
employment. According to the union acceptance of maintenance allowance by female dependent is not mandatory and 
she can either accept monetary compensation or refuse the offer and in the present case she refused the monetary 


894 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : FEBRUARY 28, 2026/PHALGUNA 9, 947 [PART II—SEc. 3(ii)] 


compensation in lieu of employment. She categorically stated that she would accept monetary compensation till the 
time her son attained the age of eighteen years and was provided with employment under the provisions of Clause 
9.5.00॥) of NCWA-V. Management of Madhabpur Colliery accepted her request and started paying Rs. 2000/- per 
month. She started receiving maintenance allowance on the condition that her son will be provided with employment 
on his attaining eighteen (8) years of age. 


3. According to the Service Record of Narayan Nayak, Ram Hari Nayak the son was seven years of age in the 
year |987 and he was fifteen years of age as on 05.0.995 i.e., on the date of death of the employee. Lalita Nayak 
submitted relevant documents before the management on 08.05.999 and requested them for providing employment 
to her son. Direction was given by the management in their letter dated 28/30.09.999 for surviving family members 
to appear before the Screening Committee with original documents. After screening was held at Colliery and Area 
level the proposal was sent to the ECL Headquarters but the management of ECL did not accept the proposal on the 
ground that his name was not maintained in the Live Roster. Union contended that the responsibility was upon the 
management to maintain the name of son in the Live Roster of the company till employment was provided to him and 
such responsibility did not rest upon the minor son or widow of the deceased employee. The union called upon the 
management to provide the Live Roster Register in which the names of the minor dependents were recorded by the 
management for providing employment on attaining majority. It is prayed that Ram Hari Nayak, the dependent son of 
Late Narayan Nayak should be provided with employment under Clause 9.5.0 (iti) of NCWA-V at the earliest with all 
other consequential benefits. 


4. Since registration of the case on 08.!.2004 the management remained unrepresented till 25.09.202. Mr. P. 
K. Das, learned advocate appeared for the management of ECL but did not file any written statement. The case was 
fixed up for evidence on 6.03.205 and for filing authorization of ECL in favour of by Mr. Das, Advocate who was 
appearing for the management of ECL on successive dates. 


i Union examined Ram Hari Nayak, the son of the deceased employee as Workman Witness No. |. He filed an 
affidavit-in-chief where he stated that his mother did not accept the monetary compensation in lieu of employment 
and requested the management to provide employment to him on his attaining eighteen ((8) years and till such time, 
she should be paid monetary compensation as per Clause 9.5.0 (iii) of NCWA. He further stated that management 
accepted the request and started paying monetary compensation to his mother. On 08.05.999 after Ram Hari Nayak 
attained the age of eighteen (l8) years his mother again submitted an application along with document to provide 
employment to him. Thereafter, the dependent son appeared before the Screening Committee and the proposal for 
employment was forwarded to the ECL Headquarters for approval. The witness stated that the management did not 
provide any employment to him and deliberately harassed him by not complying the provisions of NCWA. 


6. The witness was cross-examined on 25.05.20I5 where he deposed that the company paid monetary 
compensation to his mother in lieu of employment. At the time of death of his father he was 4 years old. The witness 
admitted that he did not have any document regarding intimation of screening for job issued by the company. The 
witness denied that he did not submit any paper to show that he is the son of Narayan Nayak and that he is not entitled 
to employment against the death of his father. 


he At the initial stage no document was produced by the union for admission in the evidence. Subsequently, on 
04.08.2023 Ram Hari Nayak, the workman witness was recalled and the following documents were brought on 
record. 


(i) Copy of the Identity Card of Narayan Nayak is marked as Exhibit W-] 
(ii) Copy of the Service Record of Narayan Nayak, as Exhibit W-2. 
(0) Copy of Death Registration Certificate of Narayan Nayak, as Exhibit W-3. 
(iv) Copy of the Application of Lalita Nayak dated 03.03.995 for her employment, as Exhibit W-4. 


(v) Copy of the letter dated 23/26.02.996 by which Lalita Nayak was asked to appear before the Initial 
Medical Examination Board, as Exhibit ४-5. 


(vi) | Copy of the letter dated 0/07.0.996 issued by the management to Lalita Nayak allowing her 
monetary compensation of Rs. 2000/- per month in lieu of employment in place of her husband. It is 
also communicated that her age was forty-nine years at the relevant time, as Exhibit W-6. 


(vii) Copy of the application submitted by Lalita Nayak refusing to accept monetary compensation and 
prayed for employment of her son, as Exhibit W-7. 


(viii) | Copy of the letter issued by Lalita Nayak addressed to the Agent, Madhabpur colliery, nominating 
her son for employment as dependent of her husband, as Exhibit W-8. 


(ix) | Copy of the letter issued by the management dated 28/30.09.999, informing that as Ram Hari 
Nayak had attained age of eighteen years and monetary compensation of Lalita Nayak would be 
discontinued, as Exhibit W-9. 


[भाग I—ave 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : फरवरी 28, 2026/फाल्गुन 9, 947 895 


(७). Copy of Note sheet dated 2.09.2000, proposing employment in favour of Ram Hari Nayak, 
initiated by the Personnel Manager and placed before the General Manager, Kajora Area, as 
Exhibit W-0. 


(xi) | Copy of letter dated 25.06.2002 issued by the Dy. Chief Personnel Manager, Kajora Area, 
addressed to the Personnel Manager (Empl), Sanctoria for a clear direction from the Competent 
Authority relating to employment to the son of Late Narayan Nayak, as Exhibit W-I]. 


8. It is strange to find that the management of ECL without filing any written statement to assert their case has 
examined Mr. Ashish Mohan, Deputy Personnel Manager, Madhabpur Colliery as Management Witness No. |. He 
has filed his affidavit-in-chief where the main contention is that Lalita Nayak, wife of the deceased employee has 
been paid Monthly Monetary Cash Compensation (hereinafter referred to as MMCC) in her Account No. 3359 of the 
State Bank of India, Bhaikumarda Branch (623) as per letter no. KA/PM/C-6/35/293/599 dated 0/07.0.996. 
She was paid monetary compensation till she attained the age of sixty (60) years in March 2007. According to the 
management witness the employment of the dependent of Narayan Nayak does not arise as the widow has been paid 
monetary compensation till, she was sixty years of age. Management has produced the following documents in 
support of their case : 


(3) Copy of the Office Order dated 0/07.0.996 for payment of MMCC to Lalita Nayak has been 
produced as Exhibit M-I. 


(0) Copy of the Payment Register showing payment to Lalita Nayak, as Exhibit M-2. 


9. In his examination-in-chief the management witness deposed that a dependent of deceased workman is 
entitled to either receive monetary compensation till her 60 years of age or employment. In this chargesheeted 
employee monetary compensation has been paid to the widow and no other dependent is entitled to employment in 
ECL. 

0. In cross-examination the management witness deposed that in her application Lalita Nayak was willing to 
take monetary compensation till her son is provided with employment. The witness admitted that the management 
issued a letter dated 28/30.09.999, informing Lalita Nayak that her son Ram Hari Nayak attained the age of eighteen 
((8) years and her maintenance allowance was discontinued. Witness deposed that employment to dependent son 
was refused as MMCC was granted to the widow in the year [996. However, it transpires from his testimony that 
widow had submitted application for employment of her son and the dependents of the deceased employee were 
asked to appear before the Screening Committee. Witness admitted that till date no letter had been issued to the 
widow informing her that employment could not be granted to her son. It is also admitted that at the time of death of 
the employee her son was about fifteen (5) years of age. Witness denied the suggestion that management violated the 
provisions of Clause 9.5.0 (0) of NCWA-V. 


Il. The moot point for consideration in this reference is whether the action of the management in denying 
employment to the dependent son of Narayan Nayak is legal and justified. If not to what relief the son is entitled to. 


2. Mr. Rakesh Kumar, union representative advancing his argument on behalf of the dependents submitted that 
according to the provisions of Clause 9.5.0 (iii) of NCWA a male dependent of the deceased employee who is fifteen 
((5) years and above in age and not provided with employment, is required to be kept on the Live Roster of the 
company and management is under an obligation to provide employment to his on his attaining the age of eighteen 
(8) years. During the period the male dependent is on the Live Roster, the female dependent is to be paid monetary 
compensation as per rates at paragraphs (i) and (ii). Mr. Kumar argued that the Service Record of the deceased 
workman (Exhibit W-2) reveals that Ram Hari Nayak is the second son of the deceased employee who was seven and 
a half (7'4) years old on 0.04.987. Accordingly, at the time of death of his father on 05.0I.995 Ram Hari Nayak 
was fifteen ([5) years old and was entitled to be kept in the Live Roster for his future employment on his attaining 
majority. It is argued that the management by letter dated 0I/07.0.996 (Exhibit W-6) granted monetary 
compensation allowance at the rate of Rs. 2000/- per month to the widow of the deceased in lieu of the employment. 
The management witness admitted that Lalita Nayak had submitted a representation before the management (Exhibit 
W-7) in which she stated that since her son was fifteen years old at the time of death of her husband, she wanted her 
son to get employment on his attaining eighteen (l8) years and the management may arrange to pay maintenance 
allowance to her until employment was granted to her son as per the provisions of NCWA. It is argued that the 
management of the company was fully informed and aware about the representation submitted by Lalita Nayak which 
finds reflection in letter dated 2.09.2000 (Exhibit W-l0) which was forwarded to the Headquarters with a proposal 
for employment to her son. My attention was drawn to the cross-examination of the management witness where he 
stated that no letter was issued to the widow informing her that employment could not be granted to her son. Mr. 
Kumar strenuously argued that though the dependent son is entitled to employment, the management of ECL has 
acted in an illegal and arbitrary manner by not providing employment to the dependent son of the deceased employee 
as per the terms of NCWA and causing hardship to the family by not taking action in this regard. 


896 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : FEBRUARY 28, 2026/PHALGUNA 9, 947 [PART II—SEc. 3(ii)] 


3. Mr. P. K. Das, learned advocate for the management of ECL had no explanation as to how the evidence of 
management witness could be appreciated without any pleading. The approach of the management in dealing with the 
case is extremely lackadaisical and nonchalant to the requirement of NCWA. The only argument which Mr. Das 
could advance is MMCC has been paid to Lalita Nayak, the widow of the deceased employee up to her age of sixty 
years which ended in March 2007 as such she cannot claim employment for her son. 
4. I have considered the written statement submitted by the union and materials on record. It is an accepted 
position that the NCWA is a settlement under Section 2(p) of Industrial Disputes Act, !947 and is binding upon the 
employer and employees under Section 8(3) of the Industrial Disputes Act, |947. The admitted position in this case, 
as may be derived from the uncontroverted statements in the written statement filed by the union and evidence of 
workman witness is that on the date of death of Narayan Nayak, the widow applying for her own employment as a 
dependent and she (Exhibit W-4) was found to be over-age for her employment and a monetary compensation was 
approved in her favour at the rate of Rs. 2000/- per month in lieu of employment. On receipt of such communication 
the by letter dated 0/07.0.996 (Exhibit W-6), Lalita Nayak made a representation before the employer company 
(Exhibit W-7), stating that her son was fifteen years old at the time of death of her husband and she claimed 
employment for her son when he attained the age of eighteen (/8) years and till such time, she was ready to accept 
monetary compensation. The management was fully aware about the claim for employment of the son and the 
necessity to maintain his name in the Live Roster of the company. This fact has been further established by Note 
Sheet dated 2.09.2000 (Exhibit W-l0) which was forwarded to the ECL Headquarters by the Personnel Manager, 
Kajora Area. It may be gathered from Exhibit W-9, a letter dated 28/30.09.]999 issued by the Dy. Personnel 
Manager, Madhabpur Colliery addressed to Lalita Nayak, informing her that she had submitted relevant documents / 
certificates in respect of employment of her son Ram Hari Nayak who attained the age of eighteen (8) years and for 
such reason her maintenance allowance was to be discontinued. It is abundantly clear that the dependent son of the 
deceased employee who was seven and a half (7%) years of age on 0.04.987 turned fifteen years of age on the date 
of death of his father on 0!.05.995 and as per the provisions of Clause 9.5.0 (iii) of NCWA-V he is eligible for being 
included in the Live Roster of the company, for his employment on attaining majority. The widow of the deceased 
employee in her representation against letter dated 0l/07.0.!996 made it clear to the management that she was 
willing to accept monetary compensation only till her son was provided with employment on attaining eighteen (8) 
years and not in lieu of employment. The management of ECL is therefore not vested with the power to impose 
their unilateral decision upon the widow and dependent son of the deceased employee that she had no option but to 
accept monetary compensation at the rate of Rs. 2000/- per month till she attains the age of sixty (60) years. 
On a clear reading of Clause 9.5.0 (i) of NCWA-V it appears that in case of death/total permanent disablement due to 
causes other than mine accident and medical unfitness under clause 9.4.0 if the female dependant is below the age of 
forty-five (45) years, she will have the option either to accept the monetary compensation of Rs.2000/- per month or 
employment. 
In case the female dependant is above forty-five (45) years of age she will be entitled only to monetary compensation 
and not to employment. 
Clause 9.5.0 (iit) of NCWA-V provides that In case of death either in mine accident or for other reasons or medical 
unfitness under clause 9.4.0, if no employment has been offered and the male dependant of the concerned worker is 
fifteen (I5) years and above in age he will be kept on a live roster and would be provided employment commensurate 
with his skill and qualifications when he attains the age of eighteen (8) years. During the period the male dependant 
is on live roster, the female dependant will be paid monetary compensation as per rates at paras (i) & (ii) above. 
Clause_9.5.0 (iv) of NCWA-V provides that Monetary compensation, wherever applicable, would be paid till the 
female dependant attains the age of sixty (60) years. 
Clause 9.5.0 (iii) of NCWA-V has made provisions for maintaining the name of male dependent in the Live Roster 
for considering him for employment once he becomes a major and it clearly provides for payment of monetary 
compensation to the female dependent during the period male dependent is on Live Roster. I find from letter dated 
28/30.09.999 that the management of ECL discontinued the payment of monetary compensation to Lalita Nayak 
when Ram Hari Nayak attained the age of eighteen (8) years. Therefore, the management of ECL appears to have 
violated the provisions of NCWA by not considering the son of the deceased employee for providing employment 
after he became a major in the year 999. Such evasive action on the part of the management in dealing with an 
established claim for employment appears deliberate and needs to be mate with impunity. By causing such inordinate 
delay in reaching a finality in respect of claim for employment, the management has thoroughly disrupted the purpose 
and object of the provisions of National Coal Wage Agreement, leading to hardship to the family of the deceased 
employee. In my considered view the action of the management in not providing employment to the dependent son of 
the deceased employee on the pretext of having allowed maintenance allowance to the widow is unjust, unfair, 
improper, illegal and is not sustainable under the NCWA. The management of ECL is therefore directed to consider 
the proposal for granting employment of Ram Hari Nayak and complete the employment procedures within two (2) 
months from the date of communication of the Award. 

Hence, 


ORDERED 
that Industrial Dispute is allowed on contest against the management of Madhabpur Colliery under Kajora 
Area of Eastern Coalfields Limited. The employer company is directed to complete the employment procedure of 


[भाग ए---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : फरवरी 28, 2026/फाल्गुन 9, 947 897 


Ram Hari Nayak, dependent son of Late Narayan Nayak within two (2) months from the date of communication of 
the Award. Let an award be drawn up in light of my above findings. Let copies of the Award in duplicate be sent to 
the Ministry of Labour, Government of India, New Delhi for information and Notification. 


ANANDA KUMAR MUKHERJEE, Presiding Officer 


नई दिल्‍ली, 2 फरवरी, 2026 


का.आ. (85.—siteifte विवाद अधिनियम, i947 (4947 का 4) की धारा ॥7 के अनुसरण में, केन्द्रीय 
सरकार ई सी एल के प्रबंधतंत्र के संबद्ध नियोजको और उनके कर्मकारों के बीच, अनुबंध में निर्दिष्ट औद्योगिक विवाद में 
केन्द्रीय सरकार औद्योगिक अधिकरण-सह-श्रम न्यायालय, आसनसोल के पंचाट(संदर्भ AHAN-4/2022) को प्रकाशित 
करती है, जो केन्द्रीय सरकार को 44/02,/2026 को प्राप्त हुआ था। 


[सं. WaA-2202/42/2022-a7e Ae (AT. TA-II)] 
सलोनी, उप निदेशक/लिंक अधिकारी 


New Delhi, the !2th February, 2026 


5.0. 85.—In pursuance of Section 7 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 947 (l4 of 947), the Central 
Government hereby publishes the Award (Reference No. 4/2022) of the Central Government Industrial 
Tribunal-cum-Labour Court, Asansol as shown in the Annexure, in the industrial dispute between the Management 
of ECL and their workmen, received by the Central Government on /02/2026. 


[No. L-2202/42/2022 — IR (CM-II)] 
SALONI, Dy. Director/Link officer 


ANNEXURE 
BEFORE THE CENTRAL GOVT. INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL -CUM- LABOUR COURT, 
ASANSOL 
PRESENT: Shri Ananda Kumar Mukherjee, 


Presiding Officer, 
C.G.LT-cum-L.C., Asansol. 
REFERENCE CASE NO. 4 OF 2022 
PARTIES: Tanmay Ruidas 
Vs 
Management of Central Hospital, Kalla, ECL 
REPRESENTATIVES: 
For the Union/Workman: Mr. S. K. Pandey, union representative 
For the Management of ECL: Mr. P. K. Das, Advocate. 
INDUSTRY: Coal. 
STATE: West Bengal. 
Dated: 2/2/2025 
AWARD 


In exercise of powers conferred under clause (d) of Sub-section (l) and Sub-section (2A) of Section 0 of the 
Industrial Disputes Act, 947 (/4 of |947), the Government of India through the Ministry of Labour, vide its Order 
No. L-2202/42/2022-IR(CM-II) dated 04/05/2022 has been pleased to refer the following dispute between the 
employer, that is the Management of Central Hospital, Kalla of Eastern Coalfields Limited and their workman for 
adjudication by this Tribunal. 


898 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : FEBRUARY 28, 2026/PHALGUNA 9, 947 [PART II—SEc. 3(ii)] 


SCHEDULE 


“Whether the action of the Management of M/s. Eastern Coalfields Ltd. in relation to its Central Hospital, 
Kalla in non-payment/denial of payment of House Rent Allowance from the month of August, 2079 in respect 
of Shri Tanmay Ruidas, Pharmacist, Central Hospital, Kalla, U.M. No. /95894 is just and legal? If not, to 
what relief the concerned workman is entitled?” 


] On receiving Order No. L-2202/42/2022-IR(CM-II) dated 04/05/2022 from the Government of India 
Ministry of Labour, New Delhi for adjudication of the dispute, a Reference case No. 4 of 2022 was registered on 
04.05.2022/0.07.2022 and an order was passed for issuing notice to the parties through registered post, directing 
them to appear and submit their written statements along with relevant documents in support of their claims. 


2. Written statement on behalf of the aggrieved workman has been filed on 3/0I/2023. Fact of the case 
disclosed in the written statement is that Tanmay Ruidas is a permanent employee of the company and is presently 
posted at Central Hospital, Kalla as Pharmacist since 2/07/209. He came to Central Hospital, Kalla on transfer from 
Sanctoria Hospital of ECL. According to the terms of House Allotment Rules of the company, quarters is provided to 
the employees who are residing beyond five kilometers and House Rent Allowance is to be paid as per the provisions 
of NCWA if quarters is not provided to the employee. Tanmay Ruidas resides at Ruidas Para, Village and PO- 
Pandaveswar, Dist- Paschim Bardhaman which is beyond five kilometers from the place of work. By letter dated 
20/07/20l9 the workman applied before the management to allot quarters to him. Management of ECL without 
allotting quarters stopped payment of House Rent Allowance on his joining the Central Hospital, Kalla. Several 
representations were made by the workman before the management to allot quarters for his accommodation and he 
also prayed for payment of House Rent Allowance as per the extant rules until allotment of quarters. 


3. The Management of ECL by office order No. ECL/CHK/C-6/Housing/209/279 dated 02//209 allotted 
quarters No. AEQ-4/l] to the workman on temporary basis which was reported by him as not livable. Workman 
could not reside in the quarters and requested several times for repairing and electric wiring of quarters but the 
management did not take step. The company by office order No. ECL/CHK/C-6/Housing/209/327 dated 
20/2/20I9 allotted another quarters No. AEQ-l6/l which was also inhabitable as it was in a bad state of repair. 
Workman after receiving keys of quarters from the security department prayed for repairing of the same and for 
making arrangement of water supply but the management did not take action within reasonable time. Finding no other 
alternative, workman prayed for cancellation of allotment of quarters No. AEQ-l6/I by letter dated 05/02/2020. Said 
quarters was subsequently allotted to Smt. Sujata Biswas, staff Nurse of Central Hospital, Kalla by office order No. 
ECL/CHK/Quarter/2020/534 dated 8/06/2020. It is contended in their written statement that the workman was 
getting House Rent Allowance @ Rs. 525.86 per month during his posting at Sanctoria Hospital and he is entitled to 
the same amount of House Rent Allowance. It is contended that action of the management of ECL in denying 
payment of House Rent Allowance is to the workman from August, 209 is unjustified, illegal and House Rent is 
required to be paid to him from August, 209 till date. 


4. Management of ECL contested the case by filing written statement on the same date i.e. 3/0/2023. 
According to the management, workman after joining at Central Hospital, Kalla applied for quarters on emergency 
basis on 20/07/209 on the ground that he was attending duty from his residential address which was thirty seven 
kilometers away from the Hospital and he was facing difficulties. Quarters was allotted to the employee as per 
recommendation of the Housing Committee meeting and he was allotted quarters on two occasions. Initially, quarters 
No. AEQ-4/ll was allotted to him on temporary basis on 02/ll/20l9 and after the decision of the Housing 
Committee in the meeting held on 7/2/20l9, Quarters No. AEQ-l6/l was allotted to him. Workman, thereafter, 
applied for repair of the quarters on 28/0l/2020 within seven days and thereafter on 05/02/2020 he applied for 
cancellation of the quarters and claimed for House Rent Allowance. Management contended that according to the 
guidelines of the company vide ECL/CMD/C-6E/I0/62 dated 2/07/202 issued by the General Manager (P&IR), 
ECL which says “Employees refusing to occupy the allotted quarters or surrender the allotted quarter will not be 
eligible to draw HRA.” According to the management, Tanmay Ruidas is not entitled to get House Rent Allowance as 
he has waived his right by not occupying the quarters that was allotted to him. 


5. Point for consideration is whether the action of the management of ECL in non-payment of House Rent 
Allowance to Tanmay Ruidas from August, 20I9 at Central Hospital, Kalla is justified? If not, to what relief the 
workman is entitled? 


6. In order to prove the claim Tanmay Ruidas, aggrieved workman has been examined as Workman Witness-I. 
He filed his affidavit-in-chief, reiterating the case disclosed in the written statement. During examination-in-chief, the 
workman produced the following documents: 

(i) Copy of application dated 20/07/209 addressed to Sr. Manager (P), CH Kalla for allotment of quarters is 
marked as Exhibit W-I. 


(ii) Copy of another application dated 2/0/209 for allotment of quarters is marked as Exhibit W-2. 


() Copy of office order dated 02/l!/20I9 issued by the management allotting quarters to the workman on 
temporary basis is marked as Exhibit W-3. 


[भाग II—ave 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : फरवरी 28, 2026/फाल्गुन 9, 947 899 


(iv) Copy of minutes of meeting held for allotment of quarters, is marked as Exhibit W-4. 


(v) Copy of letter issued to the management praying for necessary repairing of quarters and installation of water 
tank, is marked as Exhibit W-5. 


(vi) Copy of another application submitted by the workman for cancellation of quarters by letter dated 
05/02/2020 as the management did not undertake repairing work is marked as Exhibit W-6. 


(vii) Copy of office order dated 8/06/2020 by which the housing committee allotted the same quarters, No. 
AEQ-6/I to Smt. Sujata Biswas, Sr. Staff Nurse is marked as Exhibit W-7. 


(viii) | Copy of pay slip of the workman for the month of July, 209 is marked as Exhibit W-8. 


(ix) Copy of pay slip of the workman for the month of September, 209 during his posting at Central Hospital, 
Kalla is marked as Exhibit W-9. 


Workman claimed that he is entitled to get House Rent Allowance @ 8% of the basic as per central government 
tules for coal mines employees at Asansol. In cross-examination by the management of ECL, witness deposed that 
after allotment of quarters, keys were handed over to him. Workman denied suggestion that quarters allotted to him 
was in good and habitable condition and that he is not entitled to get House Rent Allowance. 


7. To fortify their case, management examined Sh. Raj Narayan Bhattacharjee as Management Witness-I and 
filed an affidavit-in-chief raising strong objection against the claim for House Rent Allowance. It is stated that 
according to Clause 70॥) of House Allotment Rules issued in the year [993 “an employee having his own residence at 
the place of posting or at a place within a radius of five kilometers from the place of posting will not be considered 
eligible for the allotment of company’s accommodations. No allotment of house will be made to those who have been 
given house building loans for construction of house within ten kilometers radius from the place of posting.” Witness 
stated that Tanmay Ruidas who attends duty from Pandaveswar to Central Hospital, Kalla travelled a distance more 
than five kilometers and he is entitled to quarters. House Allotment Committee of the management allotted quarters 
No. AEQ-4/l] to Tanmay Ruidas but he did not submit occupancy report as such no initiative was taken for repairing 
of quarters. It is claimed that after quarters is allotted to any employee, civil department takes initiative for repair and 
it has been seen that some quarters were repaired and upgraded for allotment to the employees but they refused to 
occupy, citing improper reasons which resulted in wastage of funds. The management and the union members agreed 
to submit occupation report before starting any repairing work. Subsequently, Housing Committee allotted quarters 
No. AEQ-l6/l to the workman on 6/I2/209 and he received keys but did not submit the occupation report and 
handed over the keys to the security department on 25/02/2020. It has been stated in the affidavit that Tanmay Ruidas 
applied for repairing of quarters on 28/0I/2020 and on 05/02/2020 he submitted application refusing to occupy 
quarters. According to the management, repairing of quarters are done after inspection of the quarters and cost 
estimate, budget certification and approval of the competent authority which is a time consuming process. Quarters 
was repaired during the block repairing, done in February, 20l8 and was after wards vacated by Krishan Guin. From 
February, 20I8 to October, 209 the quarters was in a habitable condition but after allotting the same to Tanmay 
Ruidas, he started raising disputes that quarters was not in a habitable condition. In course of examination, the 
management witness produced the following documents: 


(i) Copy of office order dated 02/l/20I9 by which quarters No. AEQ-4/l was temporarily allotted to Tanmay 
Ruidas and he was advised to occupy the quarters and submit occupancy report, is marked as Exhibit M-I. 


(ii) Copy of office order dated 26/2/20I9 by which quarters No. AEQ-I6/l was allotted to Tanmay Ruidas for 
his residential purpose with immediate effect, is marked as Exhibit M-2. 


(0) Copy of application dated 28/0/2020 of the concerned employee submitted before the CMO(Admn.), 
Central Hospital, Kalla disclosing that due to want of repair he could not occupy the same as there was no water tank 
installed at the quarters and he also claimed for House Rent Allowance for not being allotted a proper quarters, is 
marked as Exhibit M-3. 


(iv) Copy of application dated 05/02/2020 for cancellation of allotment of the quarters as no repairing work was 
done, is marked as Exhibit M-4. 


(v) Copy of declaration dated 25/02/2020 whereby the workman handed over the keys of the quarters No. AEQ- 
6/l, is marked as Exhibit ४-5. 


(vi) Copy of application dated 20/07/20l9 submitted by Tanmay Ruidas for allotment of quarters, is marked as 
Exhibit M-6. 


(vii) Copy of letter dated 2!/03/2009 issued by the General Manager (Admn./Welfare), ECL addressed to CMO 
(I/c), Central Hospital, Kalla communicating that Director (Personnel), ECL in the thirty fifth meeting of Coal India 
Limited Welfare Board Meeting held on 2/04/2007 decided that “payment of House Rent Allowance may be allowed 
in those mines/areas only where there is a shortage of quarters, House Rent Allowance will not be paid if quarters are 
available or allotted but not accepted by the concerned employee” is marked as Exhibit M-7. 


900 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : FEBRUARY 28, 2026/PHALGUNA 9, 947 [PART II—SEc. 3(ii)] 


After granting opportunity to Tanmay Ruidas to cross-examine the Management Witness, he declined to cross- 
examine and the witness was discharged. 


8. Mr. S. K. Pandey, union representative advancing his argument on behalf of the aggrieved workman stated 
that Tanmay Ruidas, Pharmacist was appointed in the service of ECL on 7/2/204 at Pandaveswar Colliery, ECL. 
He was transferred to Sanctoria Hospital on 9/l2/208 and thereafter was transferred to Central Hospital, Kalla on 
2/07/209. House Rent Allowance of Tanmay Ruidas which he received till he was posted at Sanctoria Hospital was 
stopped on his transfer to Central Hospital, Kalla. Copy of pay slip for July, 20l9 has been produced in support of the 
earlier payment as Exhibit W-8. It is urged that pay slip for the month of September, 20l9 (Exhibit W-9) shows that 
payment House Rent Allowance was discontinued. Workman submitted an application dated 2!/0/208 before the 
management for allotment of quarters (Exhibit W-2). An office order was passed on 02/I!/20l9 (Exhibit W-3) 
whereby quarters No. AEQ-4/l] was temporarily allotted to Tanmay Ruidas with a note that finalization of quarters 
will be done in the next Housing Committee Meeting. It is submitted that quarters No. AEQ-6/! was allotted to the 
employee and he was requested to occupy the same as soon as possible and submit the occupancy report to the Asst. 
Manager (P), Central Hospital, Kalla. No occupation certificate has been produced by the management nor by the 
union. Subsequently, on 28/0l/2020, Tanmay Ruidas submitted an application to the CMO (Admin.), CH, Kalla 
(Exhibit W-5) informing that the allotted quarters was not in a livable condition and that it needs some repair and 
water tank was required to be installed. He also claimed House Rent Allowance since his joining at Central Hospital, 
Kalla with further request that if repairing of quarters and installation of water tank was not done on urgent basis, it 
would not be possible for him to occupy the same. After receipt of letter, management neither repaired the quarters 
and installed the water tank nor did they refute the claim of the workman that no repair was required. It is argued by 
the union representative that an application was submitted by Tanmay Ruidas on 05/02/2020 for cancellation of 
allotment of quarters AEQ-6/l due to non-repair of the same and for payment of House Rent Allowance. Union 
claimed that till date no House Rent Allowance has been paid and the workman is entitled to get House Rent 
Allowance from August, 209 till date. 


9. Mr. P. K. Das, learned advocate for ECL in reply argued that Quarters No. AEQ-4/l was allotted to the 
workman (Exhibit M-|) and thereafter another quarters No. AEQ-6/] was allotted in favour of Tanmay Ruidas but 
the workman did not take possession of it and prayed for cancellation of allotment of quarters on the ground that 
quarters was not repaired (Exhibit M-4). Management relied upon a letter dated 2/03/2009 issued by the General 
Manager (Admin./Welfare) addressed to CMO (I/C), C.H., Kalla (Exhibit M-7) that House Rent Allowance would not 
be paid if quarters are available or allotted but are not occupied by the concerned employee. It was mentioned that 
payment of House Rent Allowance would be allowed in cases if there is a shortage of quarters. It is urged that in the 
instant case, the concerned employee consciously did not take possession of quarters and he is eager to enjoy House 
Rent Allowance. It is argued that the management cannot act contrary to their decision that when quarters are 
available and are not accepted by the employee, he is not entitled to House Rent Allowance. The workman is 
therefore, not entitled to get House Rent Allowance in this case. 


0. I have considered the rival arguments advanced on behalf of the union and Id. Advocate for the management. 
I have also considered the backdrop of the fact and circumstances of this case and rules guiding the employees and the 
management of ECL in respect of payment of House Rent Allowance. Grievance ventilated by the workman is for 
non-payment of House Rent Allowance to him after his transfer to Central Hospital, Kalla w.e.f. l2/07/20I9. It 
appears from the materials on record that Tanmay Ruidas is posted as a Pharmacist at Central Hospital, Kalla bearing 
UM No. 95894. It is gathered from the argument advanced by Mr. S. K. Pandey, union representative as well as 
from Exhibit W-8, a copy of pay slip for the month of July, 209 that workman was receiving House Rent Allowance 
@ Rs. 525.84 per month, prior to his transfer to Central Hospital, Kalla. In paragraph three of the affidavit-in-chief, 
Management Witness has admitted that Tanmay Ruidas is a resident of Pandaveswar and attends duty at Central 
Hospital, Kalla travelling beyond five kilometers from his residence and accordingly he is entitled to get quarters. 
Clause 8..3 of NCWA provides that for the purpose of payment of House Rent Allowance to employees in urban 
areas, government notification or clarification on the subject will be followed as provided in previous agreements and 
shall be applicable from 0/02/202 on the revised basic. Other related issues will be discussed in the standarisation 
committee within a period of three months. Clause 8.l.! provides that existing House Rent Allowance will be @ two 
percent of the revised basic per month w.e.f. 0/02/202 and will be paid to employees who have not been provided 
with residential accommodation. From such settled position between the company and the union, it appears that the 
management is conscious of its obligation to pay House Rent Allowance to its employees who have not been provided 
with residential accommodation. Management relied upon Exhibit M-7, a letter dated 2/03/2009 issued by the 
General Manager (Admin./Welfare), ECL addressed to CMO (I/C), Central Hospital, Kalla, ECL quoting observation 
of the Director (Personnel), ECL on the subject of payment of House Rent Allowance to Wage Board Employees. It 
has been communicated that “In the 357 Meeting of the CIL Welfare Board Meeting held on 27/.04.2007 it was 
decided that ‘Payment of HRA may be allowed in those mines/areas only where there is a shortage of quarters. HRA 
should not be paid if quarters are available, or allotted but are not accepted by the concerned employee.” 


iI. In the instant case, Tanmay Ruidas submitted an application on 20/07/209 i.e. soon after his transfer on 
2/07/209 for allotment of quarters on the ground that his residence is situated thirty seven kilometers away from his 


[भाग ए---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : फरवरी 28, 2026/फाल्गुन 9, 947 90] 


place of work and he was facing difficulties in attending duty. He requested the management for allotment of quarters 
AEQ-5/4 on emergency basis. No action was taken by the management and Tanmay Ruidas submitted his second 
application dated 2/0/209 (Exhibit W-2) praying for payment of House Rent Allowance as per prevailing rules of 
House Rent until allotment of quarters. An office order was passed on 02//209 (Exhibit W-3) whereby quarters 
No. AEQ-4/ll was temporarily allotted to Tanmay Ruidas until finalization of allotment on the next Housing 
Committee meeting. Subsequently, on 9/20.2.20l9 Housing Committee members of Central Hospital, Kalla on the 
basis of meeting held on 7/2/209 allotted quarters No. AEQ-l6/l to Tanmay Ruidas (Exhibit W-4). Concerned 
workman received keys of quarters from the security department but could not occupy the same as it was in a bad 
state of repair and no water tank was installed. Furthermore, quarters was not in a habitable condition. Workman 
requested for necessary repairing of quarters, installation of water tank and payment of House Rent Allowance until 
management makes the quarters habitable. It appears to me that the management being aware of such communication, 
neither took steps for repairing to make the quarters habitable nor did the management respond to the application of 
the workman denying his contention that the quarters required repairing or that there was no supply of water at his 
allotted quarters. The nonchalant conduct of the management goes to establish that grievance of the workman that 
quarters was not in habitable condition and lacked facility of water supply is true and the management did not take 
steps to remedy the same. A mechanical allotment of quarters in whatever condition it may be, cannot compel the 
workman to accept such quarters. Allotment of quarters not in habitable condition is not considered to satisfy the 
decision of the Director (Personnel), ECL that House Rent Allowance would not be paid to employees if quarters are 
available or are allotted but are not accepted. It has not been clarified that quarters should be habitable with basic 
facility of supply of electricity and water for its employees to occupy the same. In the instant case, I find that the 
workman after waiting for a considerable period after his transfer and ultimately submitted an application dated 
05/02/2020 for cancellation of allotment of quarters due to its non-repair and claimed for payment of House Rent 
Allowance. I find from Exhibit W-7 that quarters No. AEQ-6/l was subsequently allotted to Smt. Sujata Biswas, Sr. 
Staff Nurse on 8/06/2020. Therefore, it can safely be held that Tanmay Ruidas was not allotted with any suitable 
quarters for accommodation at Central Hospital, Kalla since his transfer on 2/07/209. Workman, therefore, is not 
disqualified from getting House Rent Allowance on the basis of observation of the Director (Personnel), ECL (Exhibit 
M-7). Accordingly, I hold that action of the management in not paying House Rent Allowance to Tanmay Ruidas is 
unjust, arbitrary and illegal. The management of Central Hospital, Kalla is directed to pay House Rent Allowance to 
Tanmay Ruidas w.e.f. 2/07/209 onwards at the prevailing rates till date. Management shall pay arrear of House 
Rent Allowance to the workman within two months from the date of communication of the Award. 


Hence, 
ORDERED 


that the Industrial Dispute is allowed on contest against the management of Central Hospital, Kalla with a direction to 
pay House Rent Allowance to Tanmay Ruidas at the prevailing rates w.e.f. 2/07/209 till date. Arrear of House Rent 
Allowance shall be paid to the workman within two months from the date of communication of the Award. Let an 
Award be drawn up in the light of my above discussion. Let copies of Award be communicated to the Ministry for 
information and Notification. 


ANANDA KUMAR MUKHERJEE, Presiding Officer

सरकार ई सी एल के प्रबंधतंत्र के संबद्ध नियोजकों और उनके कर्मकारों के बीच, अनुबंध में निर्दिष्ट औद्योगिक विवाद में 
केन्द्रीय सरकार औद्योगिक अधिकरण-सह-श्रम न्यायालय, आसनसोल केपंचाट[संदर्भ संक्या-40/2022) को प्रकाशित

REFERENCE CASE NO. 40 OF 2022 
PARTIES: Deblal Majhi 
(dependent husband of Late Laboni Mejhan) 
Vs. 
Management of Girmint Colliery, ECL and Another. 
REPRESENTATIVES: 
For the Union/Workman: Mr. Rakesh Kumar, President, Koyala Mazdoor Congress. 
For the Management of ECL: Mr. P. K. Das, Advocate. 
INDUSTRY: Coal 
STATE: West Bengal. 
Dated: 03.2.2025. 
AWARD 


In exercise of powers conferred under clause (d) of sub-section (l) and sub-section (2A) of Section l0 of the 
Industrial Disputes Act, 947 (/4 of 947), the Government of India through the Ministry of Labour, vide its Order 
No. L-2202/77/2022-IR(CM-II) dated 05.08.2022 has been pleased to refer the following dispute between the 
employer, that is the Management of Girmint Colliery under Sripur Area of Eastern Coalfields Limited and their 
workman for adjudication by this Tribunal. 


SCHEDULE 


“Whether the demand raised by President, Koyala Mazdoor Congress vide its representation dated 
22//0/2049 against the action of the management of Girmint Colliery under Sripur Area of M/s. Eastern 
Coalfields Ltd. for not providing employment under the provisions of National Coal Wage Agreement -VI to 
Shri Deblal Majhi husband of Late Laboni Mejhan, Ex-Wagon Loader, UM No. 07879, who expired on 
26.0/.2074/ while in service, is fair, legal and justified? If yes, what relief Shri Deblal Majhi husband of the 
deceased workman is entitled to? ” 


l. On receiving Order No. L-2202/77/2022-IR(CM-II) dated 05.08.2022 from the Government of India, 
Ministry of Labour, New Delhi for adjudication of the dispute, a Reference case was registered on 0.08.2022 and an 
order was passed for issuing notice to the parties through registered post, directing them to appear and submit their 
written statements along with relevant documents in support of their claims. 


on Deblal Majhi, the person seeking employment against death of Laboni Mejhian, his wife has appeared in 
person accompanied by Mr. Rakesh Kumar, President, Koyala Mazdoor Congress. Mr. P. K. Das, learned advocate 
for Eastern Coalfields Limited appeared accompanied by Mr. Sanjay Bhowmik, Asst. Personnel Manager. The case is 
fixed up today for appearance of both parties and final disposal of the case. 


3 In this case written statements were submitted by both parties and the case was taken for hearing in contested 
form. Deblal Baski (Majhi) was examined on 26.04.2023 as Workman Witness No. |. Mr. Ranveer Rathour, Assistant 
Manager (P), Bhanora-Girmint Group of Mines was examined as Management Witness No. | on 25.08.2023. The 
case was thereafter fixed for hearing of argument since 3..2023. Claim is pending since 26.0.20l! and the 
management of Eastern Coalfields Limited has failed to decide the issue this during long fourteen years. By order 
dated 2.0.2025 management was granted thirty (30) days’ time to complete the entire employment formalities and 
report compliance. On 24.!.2025 management filed an application along with a letter of appointment dated 
5/I7..2025 providing employment to Deblal Majhi and the management prayed for disposal of the case. 


4. Deblal Majhi on appearing before the Tribunal today admitted the issuance of Appointment Letter but he did 
not join his employment till date for the reasons best known to him. Mr. Rakesh Kumar, on behalf of the union filed 
an application stating that the dispute raised over not providing employment to Deblal Majhi has ended as the 
management has issued a letter of appointment to him and at present there is no dispute and the case may be closed. 


[भाग ave 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : फरवरी 28, 2026/फाल्गुन 9, 947 903 
Considering the application and present state of affairs, the Industrial Dispute is disposed of. Let a No Dispute Award 
be drawn up in light of my above findings. 
Hence, 
ORDERED 


The Industrial Dispute is dismissed. A No Dispute Award be drawn up in the above Reference case. Let 
copies of the Award in duplicate be sent to the Ministry of Labour and Employment, Government of India, New Delhi 
for information and Notification. 


ANANDA KUMAR MUKHERJEE, Presiding Officer

Government hereby publishes the Award (Reference No. 9/20il) of the Central Government Industrial

REFERENCE CASE NO. 9 OF 20I 
PARTIES: Tarun Kumar Mondal 
Vs 
Management of Parasea Colliery, Kunustoria Area, ECL 
REPRESENTATIVES: 
For the Union/Workman: Mr. H. L. Soni and Mr. Rakesh Kumar, union representatives 
For the Management of ECL: Mr. P. K. Das, Advocate. 
INDUSTRY: Coal. 
STATE: West Bengal. 
Dated: 05/2/2025 
AWARD 


In exercise of powers conferred under clause (d) of Sub-section (l) and Sub-section (2A) of Section 0 of the 
Industrial Disputes Act, 947 (4 of 947), the Government of India through the Ministry of Labour, vide its Order 
No. L-2202/45/200-IR(CM-II) dated 3/0/20ll has been pleased to refer the following dispute between the 
employer, that is the Management of Parasea Colliery, Kunustoria Area of Eastern Coalfields Limited and their 
workman for adjudication by this Tribunal. 


904 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : FEBRUARY 28, 2026/PHALGUNA 9, 947 [PART II—SEc. 3(ii)] 


SCHEDULE 


“Whether the action of the management of Parasea Colliery under Kunustoria Area of M/s. ECL in not 

providing employment to Mr. Tarun Kr. Mondal, adopted son of Smt. Manabala Mondal, deceased employee 

is legal and justified? What relief the applicant is entitled to?” 
] On receiving Order No. L-2202/45/200-IR(CM-II) dated 3/0/20i from the Government of India 
Ministry of Labour, New Delhi for adjudication of the dispute, a Reference case No. 9 of 20 was registered on 
5..20/09.2.20ll and an order was passed for issuing notice to the parties through registered post, directing 
them to appear and submit their written statements along with relevant documents in support of their claims. 
2. Union of Koyala Mazdoor Congress filed written statement on 2/02/203 through Mr. H. L. Soni, Asst. 
General Secretary. In their written statement filed on behalf of Tarun Kumar Mondal, union stated that Monabala 
Mondal, a permanent employee of Eastern Coalfields Limited (hereinafter referred as ECL for brevity) was a Wagon 
Loader at Parasea Colliery under Kunustoria Area of ECL. She died in harness on /0/l998 leaving behind Tarun 
Kumar Mondal as her adopted son. It is claimed that Monabala Mondal had no issue and she adopted Tarun Kumar 
Mondal as her son on 25/0/985 in the presence of his natural parents as well as her husband, Umapada Mondal. 
Further case of the union is that Monabala Mondal filed an application before ECL along with a copy of deed of 
adoption praying for inclusion of Tarun Kumar Mondal in her Service Record as the adopted son. A Note Sheet was 
initiated by the management for incorporating the name of Tarun Kumar Mondal in the Service Record of Monabala 
Mondal and the same was forwarded to the General Manager, Kunustoria Area vide Note Sheet bearing No. 
ECL/PC/P&IR/98/2443 dated 9/09/998. On receipt of Note Sheet and proposal for incorporating the name of Tarun 
Kumar Mondal as adopted son, legal opinion was sought for from the panelled advocate of the company. Mr. P. K. 
Goswami, advocate of the company submitted his legal opinion stating that adoption of Tarun Kumar Mondal by 
Monabala Mondal is a legal adoption. Legal opinion was submitted to the Personnel Manager, Kunustoria Area by 
letter dated 24/09/998 and Monabala Mondal expired on |/0/998 while in service of the company. 


3. After death of Monabala Mondal, Tarun Kumar Mondal being wholly dependent upon his adopted mother 
claimed for employment before ECL and submitted all documents. It is contended that management did not consider 
the claim for employment of the adopted son for which this Industrial Dispute has been raised by the union. 
According to the union, various death benefits of Monabala Mondal like Gratuity, CMPF and LCS amounts have 
been paid to Tarun Kumar Mondal, the legally adopted son which is a clear proof that Tarun Kumar Mondial is 
entitled to benefit of employment as a dependent. In support of the claim for employment of Tarun Kumar Mondal, 
unionhas asserted that Ration Card and Relationship Certificate issued by local authority in the name of Tarun Kumar 
Mondal and countersigned by the Member of Legislative Assembly have described him as the son of Late Monabala 
Mondal. Referring to School Leaving Certificate dated 23/0!/998 issued by the Head Master of Jote Janki Free 
Primary School it is asserted that the name of Umapada Mondal, husband of Monabala Mondal has been recorded as 
the father of Tarun Kumar Mondal. Union claimed that above documents clearly prove that Umapada Mondal and 
Monabala Mondal adopted Tarun Kumar Mondal as their son when he was four years of age. It is prayed that the 
management of ECL be directed for offering employment to the adopted son of Monabala Mondal at the earliest. 

4. Agent, Parasea Group of Mines, ECL contested the Industrial Dispute by filing written statement on 
3/07/205. It is contended that the instant dispute is not for adjudication of the claim for employment but it is in fact 
a dispute whether Tarun Kumar Mondial is the legally adopted son of Monabala Mondial, as such it is not an Industrial 
Dispute but a dispute of civil nature. Further case of the management is that this Tribunal cannot consider the validity 
of adoption which is required to be adjudicated prior to determination of the justification in not providing 
employment. Management urged that initiating Note Sheet or obtaining legal opinion cannot help in proving that the 
alleged adoption is valid. It is urged that union and Tarun Kumar Mondal are duty bound to prove that adoption is 
valid. Furthermore, there are several irregularities in the claim of adoption made by the union. Management urged that 
the instant case is liable to be dismissed and the concerned person is not entitled to any relief. 

5. Short point for consideration before the Tribunal is whether the action of the management of Parasea 
Colliery, ECL in not providing employment to Tarun Kumar Mondal, claiming to be the adopted son of Monabala 
Mondial, is legal and justified? 

6. In order to establish the claim for employment, union examined Tarun Kumar Mondal as Workman Witness- 
I. An affidavit-in-chief has been filed reiterating their case disclosed in the written statement. Workman witness was 
initially cross-examined on 02/02/206 where he deposed that his father’s name in the school record is registered as 
Umapada Mondal. He also stated that he can produce his certificate from the school and Ration card where his 
father’s name is recorded as Umapada Mondal. He further stated that he received service benefits of Monabala 
Mondal and that Monabala Mondal had no child out of natural birth. He denied the suggestion that he is not the 
lawfully adopted son of Monabala Mondal and is not entitled to any relief. When the case came up for hearing of 
argument, union recalled Workman Witness-I on 4/] [/2023 for his re-examination. Several documents which were 
not admitted earlier were produced as follows: 


(i) Copy of deed of adoption by which Monabala Mondal w/o Late Umapada Mondal adopted Tarun Kumar 
Mondal as son on 25/l0/985 is marked as Exhibit W-. 
(ii) Copy of order passed in favour of Tarun Kumar Mondal for payment of Gratuity in respect of Monabala 


Mondial, in two pages is collectively marked as Exhibit W-2. 


[भाग II—ave 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : फरवरी 28, 2026/फाल्गुन 9, 947 905 


(0) Xerox copy of his Voter ID card is marked as Exhibit W-3. 

(iv) Xerox copy of his Aadhaar card is marked as Exhibit W-4. 

(v) Xerox copy of his Ration card is marked as Exhibit W-5. 

(vi) Copy of his school certificate is marked as Exhibit W-6. 

(vii) Copy of death certificate of Monabala Mondal is marked as Exhibit W-7. 


(viii) Copy of application dated 28/07/200l under which he submitted some documents before the Agent, Parasea 
colliery is marked as Exhibit W-8. 


(ix) Copy of application dated 24/0l/2005 submitted by him before the Agent, Parasea colliery for his 
employment as dependent son is marked as Exhibit W-9. 


Witness was recross-examined. It transpires from the evidence of Workman Witness-I that husband of 
Monabala Mondal died on 07/l2/995. He further stated that before adoption, he had no relation with Monabala 
Mondal. During cross-examination by the management, workman witness identified signature of Monabala Mondal 
as Exhibit -] on her service record. Tarun Kumar Mondal deposed that he is unaware about the procedure by which 
Monabala Mondal took him in adoption in the year 985. Suggestion was put to the witness that deed of adoption was 
prepared only for seeking employment under ECL, which the witness denied. He also denied the suggestion that 
claim for employment under ECL was not justified. 


7. Facts which transpire from evidence of Workman Witness-I is that alleged adoption of Tarun Kumar Mondal 
as son was made by Monabala Mondal in the year 985. At the relevant time, Umapada Mondal, husband of 
Monabala Mondal was alive and he died on 07/2/995. It may also be gathered that Monabala Mondal died in 
harness on |/0/998 and the name of Tarun Kumar Mondal was not recorded in her Service Record as the adopted 
son. 


8. Management examined Mr. Soram Sanjoy Singh as Management Witness-I. He filed an affidavit-in-chief 
where the witness stated that in order to support the claim of employment, Tarun Kumar Mondial filed a copy of 
registered deed of adoption dated 30/09/997., He further stated that in the Service Record, Tarum Kumar Mondal has 
been recorded as grandson. Management produced the following documents in support of the case: 


(i) copy of Death Registration Certificate of Monabala Mondal is marked as Exhibit M-2. 


(0) copy of an application with Attestation form dated ]/]2/2003 submitted by Tarun Kumar Mndal is marked 
as Exhibit M-3. 


(0) copy of an application dated 25/07/2000 submitted by Tarun Kumar Mondal for receiving CMPF, Gratuity 
and life cover in respect of Monabala Mondal and also for getting employment under ECL, which was received by the 
office of the Agent, Parasea colliery is marked as Exhibit M-4. 


(iv) copy of service book of Monabala Mondal in which Tarun Kumar Mondal is stated to be the grandson of 
Monabala Mondal appearing in Column. No. |7 and I8 is marked as Exhibit M-5. 


(v) copy of Nomination form of Monabala Mondal in respect of her Gratuity, in Form-E whereby she nominated 
Tarun Kumar Mondal as her grandson is marked as Exhibit M-6. 


(vi) copy of letter dated 5/03/2004 issued by the management informing Tarun Kumar Mondal that his proposal 
for employment was not approved as it was time barred and his name was recorded as grandson of the deceased 
employee, is marked as Exhibit M-7. 


According to the Management Witness, Tarun Kumar Mondial is not entitled to get employment as per the terms of 
NCWA. It is stated that by letter dated 5/03/2004, management informed Tarun Kumar Mondal that his prayer for 
employment was not in order and it was time barred and as he was the grandson of the deceased, his proposal was not 
processed. During cross-examination, witness deposed that in service book of Monabala Mondal, name of Tarun 
Kumar Mondal has been recorded as grandson and there is no mention of names of son or daughter. He further stated 
that gratuity amount of the employee was deposited with the Assistant Labour Commissioner and he was not in a 
position to state as to whom gratuity amount was paid. It further emerged from the cross-examination that the 
management obtained legal opinion before rejecting the prayer of Tarun Kumar Mondal. Legal opinion dated 
I/l/2006 furnished by Mr. P. K. Das has been produced as Exhibit M-8, legal opinion dated 08/04/200 furnished 
by Mr. Indrajit Mukherjee is produced as Exhibit M-9 and legal opinion dated 24/09/998 furnished by Mr. P. K. 
Goswami has been produced as Exhibit M-0. 


9. Mr. Rakesh Kumar, union representative advancing argument on behalf of the union submitted that 
Monabala Mondal was a permanent employee of ECL and she died in hamess leaving behind Tarun Kumar Mondal 
as her adopted son. Adoption took place on 25/0/985 with the consent of boys’ family and adoptive parents. Tarun 
Kumar Mondal studied in Jote Janki Free Primary School and in the School Leaving Certificate issued by the Head 


906 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : FEBRUARY 28, 2026/PHALGUNA 9, 947 [PART II—SEc. 3(ii)] 


Master on 23/0/998 (Exhibit W-6), name of Umapada Mondal, husband of Monabala Mondal appears as father of 
Tarun Kumar Mondal. Referring to the copy of Voter ID card, Ration card and Aadhaar Card, Mr. Kumar submitted 
that in all documents, father’s name of Tarun Kumar Mondal has been recorded as Umapada Mondal. Monabala 
Mondal during her lifetime had made a representation before ECL to incorporate the name of Tarun Kumar Mondal 
as her adopted son but while the management was considering the matter, a legal opinion was sought for from the 
advocate, Mr. P. K. Goswami. Unfortunately, Monabala Mondal died in harness. Tarun Kumar Mondal after death of 
his adoptive mother, submitted application before the management claiming employment as per NCWA but the 
management did not consider the same. It is urged that as per the provision of clause 9.3.3 of NCWA, a legally 
adopted son has to be considered as dependent for employment. In the instant case, adopted son was wholly 
dependent upon earnings of the deceased and the management is duty bound to provide employment to him according 
to NCWA at the earliest. 


0. Mr. P. K. Das, learned advocate in reply argued that Tarun Kumar Mondial is not a legally adopted son of 
Monabala Mondal and he is not entitled to the benefit of employment as per clause 9.3.3 of NCWA. Referring to the 
copy of Service Book of Monabala Mondal (Exhibit M-5) it is submitted that her date of appointment under the 
company was 07/02/995 and in column. No. |7 and 8, name of Tarun Kumar Mondal has been recorded as 
nominee in the capacity of grandson. Referring to the cross-examination of Workman Witness-I dated 02/02/206, Id. 
Advocate argued that the witness clearly stated that Monabala Mondal had no child, therefore, there cannot be any 
scope for Tarun Kumar Mondal to be the real grandson. Witness in his recross-examination stated that he had no 
relationship with Monabala Mondal before his adoption. Therefore, there cannot be any scope for recording the name 
of Tarun Kumar Mondal as grandson. Ld. Advocate for the management further argued that story of adoption has 
been concocted with the sole object to raise a claim for employment under the management by way of false 
representation. It is argued that if any adoption of Tarun Kumar Mondal actually took place on 25/0/I985, at his age 
of four years, why would it take eleven years to execute the deed of adoption on 30/09/997. It is pointed out that 
only after Monabala Mondal was appointed on 07/2/995, process was started to make provision for employment of 
Tarun Kumar Mondal in the capacity of her adopted son. A deed of adoption was prepared with recital that the act of 
adoption took place on 25/0/985. Said adoption was not a valid adoption for the simple reason that Umapada 
Mondal, husband of Monabala Mondal was alive and no adoption can be taken by the wife when her husband is alive 
and not relinquished the world. On perusal of the deed of adoption dated 30/09/997 (Exhibit ७४-।), it appears that the 
alleged adoption took place on 25/0/985 when Tarun Kumar Mondal was four years of age. It implies that adoption 
did not take place for the first time on the basis of deed on 30/09/997, Monabala Mondal passed away after one year 
and one month from the date of execution of the deed of adoption. Claim for employment was therefore initiated even 
before the management of ECL had opportunity of recording the name of Tarun Kumar Mondial as the adopted son of 
the employee. Ld. Advocate for the management on concluding the argument submitted that Monabala Mondal died 
on I/l0/998 i.e. after one year from executing the deed of adoption but at the relevant time, her prayer for 
incorporating the name of Tarun Kumar Mondal had not been allowed. It is urged that the claim for employment of 
Tarun Kumar Mondal is not legally tenable and the Industrial Dispute is liable to be dismissed. 


il. I have considered the fact and circumstances of the case emerging from the respective pleading, evidence 
adduced by parties and argument advanced by the union and the management. It appears from the evidence on record 
that the date of appointment of Monabala Mondal under the employer company was 07/[2/995. It may be gathered 
from School Leaving Certificate of Tarun Kumar Mondal that his date of birth is 07/0/98I. In the voter ID card of 
Tarun Kumar Mondal his date of birth has been recorded as 0/05/98l. Therefore, age of Tarun Kumar Mondal at 
the time of alleged adoption was four years and at the time of appointment of Monabala Mondal on 07/2/995, age 
of Tarun Kumar Mondal would be near about fourteen and half years if the month of May is taken as the month of his 
birth. Therefore, name of Tarun Kumar Mondal certainly would have been described in Column. 6 for recording 
details of her dependents, their relationship and Date of Birth but I find that Column No. 6 of Service Record of 
Monabala Mondal (Exhibit M-5) is left blank. 


2. It would now be prudent to consider the legality of alleged adoption of Tarun Kumar Mondal by Monabala 
Mondal on 25/0/l985. According to the provision of Section 8 of Hindu Adoption and Maintenance Act, 956, a 
female Hindu can take in adoption if she is of sound mind, not a minor and not married. If a female Hindu is married, 
she can take in adoption only if her marriage has been dissolved or whose husband is dead or has completely and 
finally renounced the world or has ceased to be a Hindu or has been declared by a court of competent jurisdiction to 
be of unsound mind. In the present case, Monabala Mondal at the alleged time of adopting Tarun Kumar Mondal was 
married and Umapada Mondal, her husband was alive. Therefore, during lifetime of a Hindu married male, his wife 
did not have the capacity to adopt a son or daughter. Therefore, adoption of Tarun Kumar Mondal by Monabala 
Mandal on 25/0/[985 is not a valid adoption. Mr. Indrajit Mukherjee, advocate in his letter dated 08/04/200 
addressed to the Agent, Parasea Colliery (Exhibit M-9) has rightly opined that deed of adoption cannot be treated to 
be legal and valid for the reason that Monabala Mondal having a husband living in the year 985, didn’t have the 
capacity or competence to take any child in adoption. 


[भाग ए---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : फरवरी 28, 2026/फाल्गुन 9, 947 907 


3. In view of legal position and facts of the case I hold that the management of Parasea Colliery, ECL is 
justified in not providing employment to Tarun Kumar Mondal as the adopted son of Monabala Mondal and the 
Industrial Dispute is liable to be dismissed. 

Hence, 


ORDERED 


that the Industrial Dispute is dismissed on contest against the union. Tarun Kumar Mondal is not entitled to get 
employment as the dependent son of Monabala Mondal, ex-employee. Let an Award be drawn up in the light of my 
above discussion. Let copies of Award be communicated to the Ministry for information and Notification. 


ANANDA KUMAR MUKHERJEE, Presiding Officer 


नई दिल्‍ली, 2 फरवरी, 2026

Government hereby publishes the Award (Reference./06/2025) of the Central Government Industrial Tribunal-

and their workmen, received by the Central Government on |/02/2026.
