REFERENCE CASE NO. 06 OF 2025 
PARTIES: Fulmoni Bhuia 
(wife of Late Rajdhar Bhuia) 
Vs. 
Management of | and 2 Incline, Jhanjra Project, ECL. 


REPRESENTATIVES: 

For the Union/Workman: Mr. Rakesh Kumar, President, Koyala Mazdoor Congress. 
For the Management of ECL: Mr. P. K. Das, Advocate. 

INDUSTRY: Coal 

STATE: West Bengal. 


Dated: .2.2025. 


908 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : FEBRUARY 28, 2026/PHALGUNA 9, 947 [PART II—SEc. 3(ii)] 


AWARD 


In exercise of powers conferred under sub-Section (5) of Section l2 read with sub-Section (2A) of Section 
0 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 947 (4 of 947), the Ministry of Labour, Government of India through the Office 
of the Deputy Chief Labour Commissioner (Central), Asansol, vide its Order No. (24)/2025/E dated 9.08.2025 has 
been pleased to refer the following dispute between the employer, that is the Management of | and 2 Incline, Jhanjra 
Project under Jhanjra Area of Eastern Coalfields Limited and their workman for adjudication by this Tribunal. 


SCHEDULE 


“Whether the action of the management of I & 2 Incline, Jhanjra Project, Jhanjra Area, M/s ECL in not 
providing employment and not paying monetary compensation to Smt Fulmoni Bhuia, W/o Late Rajdhar 
Bhuia is justified or not? If not, what relief the applicant is entitled to? ” 


l. On receiving Order No. (24)/2025/E dated 9.08.2025 from the Office of the Deputy Chief Labour 
Commissioner (Central), Asansol, Ministry of Labour, Government of India, for adjudication of the dispute a 
Reference case was registered on 27.08.2025 and an order was passed for issuing notice to the parties through 
registered post, directing them to appear and submit their written statements along with relevant documents in support 
of their claims. 


2. The union of Koyala Mazdoor Congress filed their written statement on l3.0.2025 representing the case of 
Fulmoni Bhuia, wife of Late Rajdhar Bhuia. The fact of the case in brief, as disclosed in the written statement is that 
Rajdhar Bhuia was employed as Tyndal at | and 2 Incline of Jhanjra Project under Jhanjra Area of Eastern Coalfields 
Limited (hereinafter referred to as ECL). Due to illness, he was under medical treatment at the Durgapur Steel Plant 
Hospital at Durgapur and Central Hospital at Kalla under ECL. The workman died on 05.2.996 in harness, leaving 
behind Fulmoni Bhuia as his dependent. Initially, Fulmoni Bhuia applied for providing employment to her as per 
provisions of National Coal Wage Agreement (hereinafter referred to as NCWA). The management of the employer 
company processed her claim for employment. She was asked to appear before the Screening Committee and her 
medical examination was conducted by the Initial Medical Examination Board (hereinafter referred to as IME Board). 
The proposal was sent from Area Level to the ECL Headquarters for approval. The Director (Personnel), ECL instead 
of considering the prayer for employment directed payment of Monthly Monetary Cash Compensation (hereinafter 
referred to as MMCC) in lieu of employment but at that time the wife of the deceased was eager for her employment. 
A letter bearing No. AGT/JNR/l&2/P/2008/l80 dated 3/l4.2.2008 was issued by the management of ECL which 
was replied by Fulmoni Bhuia but the management did not take any action. Union raised the Industrial Dispute before 
the Regional Labour Commissioner (C), Asansol where management agreed to send the employment file to the ECL 
Headquarters along with her reply to the queries. In this matter management signed a Memorandum of Settlement on 
7.07.209 before the Regional Labour Commissioner (C), Asansol. Despite such assurance the employment file was 
not sent to the ECL Headquarters for employment of the dependent wife, as a consequence the claim for employment 
of Fulmoni Bhuia is still unresolved. It is stated that after several representation by Fulmoni Bhuia management did 
not communicate their final decision regarding the claim for employment. The dependent wife having no source 
of income to maintain livelihood claimed MMCC from the date of death of her husband till she attained the age of 60 
years. The union claimed that management should either provide employment to the widow or they should provide 
MMCC to her according to the provision of NCWA. 


3 Management contested the claim by filing their written statement on 02.2.2025. The management disclosed 
that Fulmoni Bhuia applied for providing her employment in place of her deceased husband on 09.06.997. After 
screening, her proposal was sent to the ECL Headquarters on 3.l!.!998 where the Competent Authority approved 
payment of MMCC in lieu of employment. Fulmoni Bhuia refused to accept the MMCC and requested for her 
employment. An Industrial Dispute has been raised before the Regional Labour Commissioner (C), Asansol, over 
denial of her employment in the year 206. Thereafter, she submitted a representation claiming MMCC from the date 
of death of her husband. According to the management she is at the verge of attaining sixty years of age and not 
entitled for MMCC as she made such application after lapse of almost 30 years from the date of death of her husband. 
The main contention for the management is that the initial request for employment of the widow was not considered 
instead the Competent Authority offered MMCC to her but she refused the same on 8.0.2000. Since the dependent 
repeatedly refused the MMCC in the year 2000 and 205 she is not entitled to recetve MMCC after nearly thirty years 
and the Industrial Dispute raised is liable to be dismissed. It is alternatively contended that once the wife of the 
deceased exercised the option under Clause 9.3.2 of NCWA, she is not entitled to make any claim under Clase 9.5.0 
of NCWA. On the basis of such assertion management claimed that it is totally justified on the part of ECL in not 
providing employment and not paying monetary compensation to the wife of the deceased employee. 


4. Union examined Fulmoni Bhuia as Workman Witness No. |. She filed an affidavit-in-chief asserting her 
claims for monetary compensation from the employer company till she attained the age of sixty years. The witness 
produced the following documents in support of her case : 


(i) Copy of the Identity Card of Rajdhar Bhuia is produced as Exhibit W-|. 
(li) Copy of the Service Record of Rajdhar Bhuia, as Exhibit W-2. 


[भाग II—ave 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : फरवरी 28, 2026/फाल्गुन 9, 947 909 


(ii) Copy of the Death Certificate of Rajdhar Bhuia issued by the Central Hospital at Kalla, as 
Exhibit W-3. 


(iv) Copy of the Death Registration Certificate of Rajdhar Bhuia, as Exhibit W-4. 


(v) Copy of letter dated 3/l4.2.2008 whereby management asked Fulmoni Bhuia to produce 
documents for her employment, as Exhibit W-5. 


(vi) Copy of the application whereby Fulmoni Bhuia informed the management about death of her 
husband and produced his treatment papers, as Exhibit W-6. 


(vii) Copy of the reply of Fulmoni Bhuia dated 8.08.2009 to the management’s letter dated 4.2.2008, 
as Exhibit W-7. 


(viii) Copy of the letter of Fulmoni Bhuia dated 08.2.20l5 addressed to the Agent, | and 2 Incline, 
Jhanjra Project, seeking employment, as Exhibit W-8. 


(ix) Copy of the application dated 4.07.20l8 of Fulmoni Bhuia submitted before the General Manager, 
Jhanjra Area for payment of Monetary, as Exhibit W-9. 


It appears from the evidence-in-chief that after death of her husband Rajdhar Bhuia she prayed for payment of 
monetary compensation on |4.07.208. 


5: In cross-examination by the management, the workman witness deposed that when she applied for 
employment against the death of her husband management proposed to pay MMCC at the rate of Rs. 3,000/- (Rupees 
three thousand) per month in lieu of employment. She further stated that as the claim for employment was pending, 
she refused to accept monetary compensation at that time. Thereafter, she submitted an application for payment of 
MMCC and also the arrear amount. The copy of the application which was shown to her by the management has been 
marked as Exhibit M-2. On being asked by the Tribunal if the management rejected her application for employment, 
the witness replied in the negative and disclosed the reason of delay on her part in agreeing to accept monetary 
compensation. In course of cross-examination a copy of her application dated 8.0!.2000, refusing monetary 
compensation in lieu of employment was also shown to her by the management and on admission the same was 
marked as Exhibit M-]. 


6. Mr. Jayanta Gorai, Manager, Human Recourses at Jhanjra Project of ECL has been examined as 
Management Witness No. |. He filed an affidavit-in-chief and admitted the entire case of the union regarding death of 
Rajdhar Bhuia on 05.l2.996 and his wife Fulmoni Bhuia applying for employment. The proposal was sent to the 
ECL Headquarters on 3..998. The witness also deposed that the Competent Authority approved payment of Rs. 
3,000/- (Rupees three thousand only) in lieu of employment. It appears from the averment in the affidavit-in-chief that 
there is no scope for providing employment to the claimant dependent who is now fifty-seven (57) years old. It is 
stated by the witness that once she had exercised the option under Clause 9.3.2 of NCWA she is not entitled to claim 
monetary compensation under Clause 9.5.0 of NCWA. Further case of the management is that the family of the 
deceased employee survived thirty (30) years without any assistance as such she is not entitled to any monetary 
compensation. It is their case that the management is justified in not providing employment and not paying monetary 
compensation from the date of death of her husband. 


7 In course of cross-examination the witness deposed that the claim for employment was processed from 2000 
till 20l5 and she filed relevant documents. It further transpires from his testimony that management did not consider 
her prayer for employment and at the same time no communication was made to Fulmoni Bhuia that she was not 
entitled to any employment. The witness admitted that management received application dated 4.07.20l8 submitted 
by Fulmoni Bhuia praying for monetary compensation and till date management has not considered her prayer for 
monetary compensation and went ahead to deposed that management has not committed any illegality by not 
providing employment and monetary compensation. Copies of the Note Sheets filed by the union representative were 
marked as Exhibit M-3 collectively in three pages on admission. 


8. The moot question to be considered is whether the action of the management in not providing employment 
and not paying monetary compensation to Smt. Fulmoni Bhuia is justified or not? If not, what relief the applicant is 
entitled to. 


9. Mr. Rakesh Kumar, Union Representative argued that the management of ECL deliberately did not consider 
the prayer for employment of the wife of the deceased and no communication was made to her as to the fate of her 
prayer. It is argued that after nearly thirty years from the date of death of Rajdhar Bhuia on 05.2.996, the 
management witness in order to avoid the responsibility of ECL has stated in paragraph no. (I0) of his affidavit-in- 
chief that as the claimant is fifty-seven (57) years old and she is not entitled to employment. Mr. Kumar further 
argued that she claimed employment twenty-nine years earlier and she was much younger by 29 years, eligible for 
employment. At the relevant time management of ECL did not reject her claim for employment but issued a letter 
dated 03/05.2.999 asking her to accept the monetary compensation of Rs. 3000/- (Rupees three thousand only) per 
month in lieu of employment. Mr. Kumar submitted that the management arbitrarily did not process her prayer for 


9]0 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : FEBRUARY 28, 2026/PHALGUNA 9, 947 [PART II—SEc. 3(ii)] 


employment and alternatively tried to impose their decision upon her disregarding her right to exercise option under 
NCWA. It is argued that as the management did not provide her with any employment nor rejected her prayer on any 
plausible ground, the wife of the deceased employment cannot be debarred from claiming monetary compensation at a 
later stage. It is contended that management’s claim that once she opted for employment, she had no right to claim 
monetary compensation, is without foundation and there is no such provision in NCWA debarring the wife of the 
deceased employee from receiving monetary compensation when management deliberately withheld her entitlement 
to employment. Mr. Kumar in support of his argument relied upon the ratio of the decision of the Hon'ble High Court 
at Calcutta in the case of Smt. Shanti Devi Vs M/s. Eastern Coalfields Limited and Others [Writ Petition No. 
2243/2022] where the Hon'ble Court observed that twenty-four years have passed between the death of the 
petitioner’s husband but neither compassionate employment nor MMCC has been given. The whole object of Chapter 
IX of NCWA is on the verge of being frustrated. None of the NCWA applicable to the petitioner’s case has taken 
away the benefits of compassionate employment or MMCC in lieu thereof. The Hon'ble High Court on the aforesaid 
fact and circumstances of the case held that the claim made by the petitioner for MMCC is justified. The ECL is 
directed to pay MMCC to the petitioner at the rate prevailing from time to time from the date of death of her husband 
along with an interest at the rate of three percent per annum. Mr. Rakesh Kumar, referring to Exhibit M-3, a Note 
Sheet dated 26.0.999 argued that the Competent Authority accorded approval to process the case vide Note Sheet 
dated 03/0.09.999 and the age of Fulmoni Bhuia was assessed as thirty years as on |2.!2.997 by the P.M.E Board 
at Jhanjra and she was declared fit. Mr. Kumar strenuously argued that after the conditions for employment were 
found in her favour, the management without rhymes and reasons approved payment of maintenance allowance to the 
dependent wife which they are now denying. 


0. Mr. P. K. Das, learned advocate admitted that the management did not issue any letter, informing the widow 
of the deceased that her prayer for employment was regretted. The only argument advanced by the learned advocate 
for the management is that once the prayer for employment is made and the offer for monetary compensation was 
refused by the wife, her payer for monetary compensation at a later stage could be accepted. It is argued that the 
Industrial Dispute is liable to be dismissed. 


ll. I have considered the rival arguments, the pleadings of the parties, evidence adduced and the relevant 
provisions of NCWA related to payment of monetary compensation to the female dependent of the deceased 
employee. Admittedly, after the death of Rajdhar Bhuia, his wife filed an application before the management for her 
employment. It appears from the document of the management that the claim for employment was made on 
2.0.997 and the Screening Committee of | and 2 Incline, Jhanjra, examined the proposal for employment on 
20.06.997. It appears from the evidence of Mr. Jayanta Gorai (Management Witness No. |) the claim for 
employment was under process from 2000 till the year 20I5. It is also admitted by the witness that the management 
did not consider the prayer for employment and no communication was made to Fulmoni Bhuia after 20I5 that she 
was not entitled to get any employment. Exhibit W-8, a letter issued by Fulmoni Bhuia addressed to the Agent, | and 
2 Incline, Jhanjra Project reveals that the widow of the deceased was expecting her employment on 08.2.20I5 as she 
requested the management to issue necessary direction for processing the employment without further delay and that 
her family members are suffering starvation for the last twelve years. According to Clause 9.5.0 (i) of NCWA-V : In 
case of death due to mine accident, the female dependant would have the option to either to accept the monetary 
compensation of Rs. 3,000/- per month or employment irrespective of her age. 


Clause 9.5.0 (ii) of NCWA-V : In case of death / total permanent disablement due to cause other than mine 
accident and medical unfitness under Clause 9.4.0., if the female dependant is below the age of 45 years, she will 
have the option either to accept the monetary compensation of Rs. 3,000/- per month or employment. In case the 
female dependant is above 45 years of age she will be entitled only to monetary compensation and not to 
employment. 


Clause 9.5.0 (iv) of NCWA-V: Monetary compensation wherever applicable, would be paid till the female 
dependant attains the age of 60 years. 


Clause 9.5.0 (v) of NCWA-V : The rate of monetary compensation which stands at Rs. 2,000/- and Rs. 3,000/- 
per month as mentioned above would be reviewed w.e.f. 0!.07.996. 


2. In the present case the wife of the deceased opted for employment while she was thirty years of age but 
management without refusing her prayer for employment approved payment of monetary compensation to 
Fulmoni Bhuia at the rate of Rs. 3,000/- per month by letter dated 03/05.2.999. Such proposal was initially declined 
by the widow in her letter dated 8.0.2000 (Exhibit M-l). Management of ECL exhibited extreme apathy to the legal 
rights of the dependent wife as well as their obligation towards the family of the deceased. After passage of several 
years when the claim for employment became irrelevant to the aged lady, she has been compelled to turn to her right 
for monetary compensation, only to choose between two odds. The management has exhibited extreme lack of 
fairness, firstly, by not considering the claim for employment in the right prospective as per NCWA and thereafter 
refusing to pay monetary compensation on the basis of an imaginary condition that once the dependent opts for 
employment she is not entitled to monetary compensation. In my considered view the management of ECL cannot 
escape its duty in paying monetary compensation to the dependent wife from the date following the date of death of 


[भाग [ave 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : फरवरी 28, 2026/फाल्गुन 9, 947 9]] 


the workman i.e., 06.2.996 till Fulmoni Bhuia attains the age of sixty years. There is no provision in NCWA that if 
the dependent wife opts for employment she cannot later on opt for monetary compensation. The case of the 
dependent wife is also fortified by the decision of the Hon'ble High Court in the case of Smt. Shanti Devi Vs 
M/s. Eastern Coalfields Limited and Others [Writ Petition No. 2243/2022], where the Hon'ble High Court found 
it justified to direct ECL to pay MMCC to the petitioner at the prevailing rate from time to time from the date of death 
of her husband and the arrear to be paid along with the interest of three percent per annum. It appears to me that long 
delay in paying monetary compensation to the widow has caused immense hardship to the dependent family of the 
deceased employee. The management firstly on account of their indecisive nature and subsequently by their inaction 
has frustrated the legitimate claim of the dependent wife. Furthermore, there has been devaluation of money from 
996 to 2025 in many folds. Therefore, it is appropriate that the management of ECL is directed to pay an interest of 
five percent per annum on the arrear of monetary compensation from the date following the date of death of her 
husband till January, 2026. The management should also pay the monetary compensation at the prevailing rate till the 
wife attains sixty years of age. 


Hence, 
ORDERED 


that the Industrial Dispute is allowed on contest against | and 2 Incline, Jhanjra Project under Jhanjra Area of 
Eastern Coalfields Limited. The management of Eastern Coalfields Limited is directed to pay Monthly Monetary 
Cash Compensation to Fulmoni Bhuia at the prevailing rate from 06.2.996 till she attains the age of sixty years. The 
management is also directed to pay the arrear of maintenance up to 3.0.2026 with interest of five percent per annum 
and also pay the current maintenance from time to time. Let an award be drawn up in light of my above findings. Let 
copies of the Award in duplicate be sent to the Ministry of Labour, Government of India, New Delhi for information 
and Notification. 


ANANDA KUMAR MUKHERJEE, Presiding Officer 
नई दिल्‍ली, 6 फरवरी, 2026

Dated 06/0/2026 
AWARD 


2. The case of the workmen as per the petition filed on 05.0.20I3 is that the petitioner along with others was 
working in Swang Colliery and later on the Secretary of the Union namely Sri Suresh Kumar Sharma raised an 
Industrial Dispute demanding regularization of some workmen including the concerned workmen which was referred 


[भाग II—ave 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : फरवरी 28, 2026/फाल्गुन 9, 947 93 


to CGIT No.l, Dhanbad being Ref. Case No. 58 of 992 and ultimately the Award was passed in favour of the 
workmen for their regularization that was confirmed by the Hon’ble Jharkhand High Court as well as by the Hon’ble 
Supreme Court but even thereafter the management did not implement the Award and ultimately the management 
entered into a settlement with the union on 04.08.2009 whereby it was agreed to reinstate the workmen who submit 
individual claim application in prescribed format along with photographs and other testimonials duly attested by the 
Secretary of Sponsoring Union. Subsequently the petitioners complied with the mandatory condition of the settlement 
and thereafter they were reinstated in the year 20l0 as per Award passed by this Tribunal in Ref. Case No. 58 of 
992. But thereafter when the petitioners and other workmen started demanding their back wages, the management 
started victimizing them and issued false, motivated and illegal charesheet dated 06.06.20l2 under the signature of the 
Project Officer under Certified Standing Order 26.9 and 26.22 for misconduct alleging that they had submitted false 
information regarding their name, age, father’s name, academic qualification in connection with their employment 
and deliberately doing such act which breaches the discipline and is subversive of the interest of the company. The 
petitioners submitted their reply denying the charges. Thereafter the enquiry was held through biased and prejudiced 
Enquiry Officer and ultimately he was dismissed illegally by the unauthorized persons by the order dated 0.04.207. 
It has further been stated that the dismissal of the petitioner is illegal and void ab initio because the copies of 
documents, evidence and names of the witnesses were not supplied to the workmen along with the charge sheet. The 
allegations of entering into service below the age of |8 years is not a misconduct as per the provisions of the Certified 
Standing Order of the Company and the management can issue charge sheet and dismissal order only when the 
charges of misconduct are as per provision of the certified standing order. During enquiry the petitioners produced 
authentic documents to prove their identity namely:- Voter I.D. Card, Aadhaar Card, PAN Card, Bank Pass Book, 
Residential Certificate issued by S.D.O., Identification by Ward Members and Union Membership Receipt and other 
documents. As per settlement it was agreed to reinstate on the basis of identification by the Union Secretary and the 
petitioners were identified by the Union Secretary namely Sri Suresh Sharma. The management has also referred the 
Identification matter to the Police and the Police also given report to the management that the petitioners are genuine 
persons. The petitioners were appointed by the G.M. (P&IR) Head Quarters whereas they were dismissed by the 
Project Officer who is below the rank of Appointing Authority and so also dismissal is illegal. The alleged ground of 
entry into service allegedly in underage is neither a crime nor a misconduct under the provisions of the Standing 
Order and on this score neither any charge sheet can be issued nor any Disciplinary action can be taken and in the 
worst case if it is accepted, the management has committed crime and is liable to be prosecuted under Child Labour 
Act and at last it was prayed to set aside the dismissal order and reinstate the workmen concerned. 


3. On the other hand the case of the management as per written statement cum rejoinder filed on 8.02.209 is 
that the petition u/s 2A of the I.D. Act, 947 is not maintainable. The petitioners entered into the employment of M/s 
CCL claiming themselves as workmen concerned of Ref. Case No. 58 of 992 fraudulently. As the act of the 
petitioners are serious misconduct in view of clause 26.9 and 26.22 of the Certified Standing Orders. The 
management of Swang Washery issued a charge sheet dated 05.06.20l2 to the petitioners and the petitioners 
submitted their reply that was not found satisfactory and therefore the disciplinary authority appointed Enquiry 
Officer to conduct domestic enquiry in accordance with principles of natural justice. The enquiry was conducted in 
presence of the petitioners/workmen. They engaged Sri Chandra Shekhar Jha, Zonal Secretary, United Coal Workers 
Union, Regional Office, Jarangdih, Bokaro as co-worker. The management witnesses were examined in presence of 
the petitioner workmen and their co-worker. The Enquiry Officer allowed the petitioner workmen as well as the co- 
worker to cross examine the management witnesses. The workmen were allowed to adduce evidence in their defence 
who also produced the same as well as examined witnesses in their defense. The enquiry conducted by the Enquiry 
Officer is fair and proper and in accordance with principles of natural justice and ultimately the Enquiry Officer 
submitted report holding therein the allegation leveled against the petitioner workmen in the aforesaid charge sheet as 
duly proved and they were found guilty of the charges and thereafter the second show cause notice dated 08.03.20I7 
was issued to the petitioner/workmen who again submitted their explanation to the same but that was not found 
satisfactory and therefore the Disciplinary Authority dismissed them from the services of the company vide dismissal 
letter dated 0.04.207 for proved misconduct. The dismissal of the petitioner/workmen is legal and justified and it 
was submitted to decide the fairness of enquiry in a preliminary hearing and if it is found that the enquiry is not fair 
and proper then allow him to adduce evidence afresh. In rejoinder it has been stated that in reply to the statement of 
the petitioners in Para-| of the petition it is submitted that the petitioners fraudulently entered into the employment of 
CCL. It is stated in reply of Para-2 that the petitioners were dismissed for commission of serious misconduct after 
holding fair and proper enquiry. In reply to Para 3 it is submitted that the dismissal order was passed by the 
management which is legal and valid and the petitioners are not entitled to any relief. The statement of Para-4 and 5 
of the petitioners is matter of record. In reply to the statement of Para-6 and 7 it is stated that for want of 
identification, the management could not be able to implement the Award. In reply to Para-8 and 9 it is stated that 
these are matters of record. In reply to Para-l0 to 2 it is stated that the petitioners entered into the employment 
fraudulently. In reply of Para-l3 it is stated that as they committed serious misconduct the management issued them 
charge sheet. In reply to Para-l4 of the petition it is stated that the reply of the petitioners were not satisfactory. In 
reply to Para-l5 and 6 it is stated that the Enquiry Officer conducted domestic enquiry in accordance with the 
principles of natural justice. In reply to Para-7() to (xiv) it is stated that the ground mentioned by the petitioners are 
not good grounds. The petitioners committed serious misconduct which was duly proved in the domestic enquiry that 


9]4 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : FEBRUARY 28, 2026/PHALGUNA 9, 947 [PART II—SEc. 3(ii)] 


was conducted in accordance with the principles of natural justice and at last it was prayed to hold that the action of 
the management in dismissing the concerned workmen namely Sri Binod Rajak (ID-9/207), Sri Bodhan Rajak 
(ID-20/207), Sri Jyoti Ram (ID-2/207) and Smt. Dropadi Bai (ID-22/207) is legal and further to be pleased to 
hold that the petitioners are not entitled to get any relief. 


4. A rejoinder in reply to the W/S of the management was also filed on behalf of the workmen on 4.03.20I9 in 
which they have denied the entire claims and allegations of the management. 


5. To decide the fairness and propriety of enquiry report as Preliminary point, the following evidence has been 
adduced:

From the side of the workman 
No Evidence 


6. On perusal of the case record it transpires that vide order dated 09.0.2020 this Tribunal has held the 
domestic enquiry conducted by the management as fair and proper deciding the same on preliminary point and 
thereafter the case record was posted for final argument. 


he: Heard both sides and perused the materials on record. 


8. The Ld. Counsel on behalf of the workmen has submitted that the concerned workmen were working in 
Swang Washery Colliery as Slurry Remover since long but when they were not regularized by the management, they 
raised industrial dispute through the union and ultimately the same was referred to this Industrial Tribunal vide Ref. 
Case No. 58 of 992 that was awarded by this Tribunal in favour of the workmen and they were directed to be 
regularized by the management. That Award was confirmed by the Hon’ble Jharkhand High Court as well as by the 
Hon’ble Supreme Court but the management was not ready to implement the award and ultimately a settlement was 
entered into between the management and the union on 04.08.2009, whereby it was agreed to reinstate the workmen 
on their submission of claim application in prescribed format along with photographs and testimonials duly attested 
by the Secretary of Sponsoring Union and accordingly the concerned workmen submitted their required documents 
and the management reinstated their services in the year 20l0 but later on, the management issued a false and 
motivated charge sheet on 05.03.20l2 when they demanded their back wages and ultimately without following the 
principles of natural justice, dismissed the services of the concerned workmen vide order dated 0.04.207 on the 
ground of misconduct calculating their age from 3list March 982 which has no basis. It has further been submitted 
that the workmen were appointed by G.M. (P&IR) Head Quarters whereas they were dismissed by the Project Officer 
who is below the rank of the Appointing Authority and so the dismissal is illegal. The workmen were identified by the 
Union Secretary and therefore the management cannot be allowed to raise any doubt in the identity of the workmen. 
The management also referred the identification matter to the Police and Police has given report to the management 
that the petitioners are genuine persons. The workmen have also filed their Voter I.D. Card, Aadhaar Card, PAN 
Card, Bank Pass Book, Residential Certificate issued by S.D.O., Identification by Ward Members, Union 
Membership Receipt and all these things go to establish that they are the real workmen and the Enquiry Officer also 
in his cross examination accepted that no rival claimants appeared in place of concerned workmen claiming 
themselves as genuine workmen. The Ld. Counsel for the petitioners has further submitted that the age of the 
petitioners were assessed by the Medical Board only on the basis of their appearance and no ossification test was 
conducted and therefore the assessment of age by the Medical Board is not reliable rather illegal and for all these 
reasons prayed to set aside the dismissal order and issue direction for reinstatement of the workmen. 


9. On the other hand the Ld. Counsel on behalf of the management has submitted that this Tribunal vide order 
dated 09.0.2020 held the domestic enquiry conducted by the management as fair and proper and therefore the 
workmen cannot be allowed to raise the matter of legality in conducting the enquiry. He has further submitted that the 
petitioners have committed serious misconduct in view of clause 26.9 and 26.22 of the Certified Standing Order by 
fraudulently submitting their false claim for reinstatement as is evident from the age that they have claimed, medical 
board assessment as well as their date of initial joining and thus their age comes between 7 to |] years at the date of 
their initial joining which go to establish that they are not the actual awardee of Ref. Case No. 58 of 992 and after 
proper and fair domestic enquiry they were dismissed on the ground of serious misconduct and they are not entitled to 
any relief and prayed to dismiss this case. 


0. Issue 


Whether the action of the management in dismissing Sri Binod Rajak (ID-9/207), Sri Bodhan Rajak (ID- 
20/207), Sri Jyoti Ram (ID-2/207) and Smt. Dropadi Bai (ID-22/207) on the allegation of fraudulent entry into 
services of CCL is legal and justified and whether they are entitled to get any relief ? 


9]6 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : FEBRUARY 28, 2026/PHALGUNA 9, 947 [PART II—SEc. 3(ii)] 


Il. On perusal of the entire case record it transpires that this Tribunal in Ref. Case No. 58 of 992 passed an 
Award dated 03.0.996 in favour of the workmen directing the management to regularize them. It further transpires 
that the management challenged the Award before the Hon’ble High Court as well as before the Hon’ble Supreme 
Court but that was confirmed up to that stage and it further transpires that later on, a settlement was entered into 
between the management and the union on 04.08.2009 and accordingly the petitioners were also taken into services of 
the company in the year 20l0 by the management on being identified by the union secretary but subsequently the 
management issued charge sheet on 05.06.20I2 and 06.06.202 respectively against the petitioners on the ground of 
misconduct under clause 26.9 and 26.22 of the Certified Standing Order in which it was alleged that they had 
fraudulently entered into the services of the management by wrongly submitting their documents, certificates, name 
and father’s name in their employment that was a deliberate act which breaches discipline and is subversive of the 
interest of the company. The reply were given to the charge sheets but those were not found satisfactory by the 
management and therefore Enquiry Officers were appointed and enquiry was conducted by them and ultimately the 
Enquiry Officers found the charges against the petitioners proved and then second show cause notices were issued 
and reply of the same were also submitted by the petitioners but that were also not found satisfactory by the 
management and ultimately vide office order dated 0!.04.20l7 the management/Project Officer imposed the penalty 
of dismissal on the petitioners from the services of the CCL with immediate effect on the ground of serious 
misconduct. 


2. Though, the Ld. Counsel on behalf of the concerned workmen has raised the issue of not following the 
principles of natural justice during the enquiry proceeding but as this Tribunal vide order dated 09.0.2020 has held 
the enquiry conducted by the management as fair and proper on preliminary point, that point cannot be re-opened. But 
the Tribunal has to examine whether the penalty of dismissal is legal and justified in the facts, circumstances and the 
materials on record. 


3. On perusal of Ext.M 3 Series in ID No.l9/20l7 and ID No.20/20l7 and Ext.M 2 Series in ID 
No.2/20l7 and 22/20l7 it is evident that on the point of date of birth the petitioners have not adduced any 
matriculation or school leaving certificate or panchayat/municipal birth certificate but they have submitted notary 
affidavits sworn in on 8.0.2009 in which the petitioner Binod Rajak has declared his age as 35 years, Bodhan Rajak 
has declared his age as 34 years, Jyoti Ram has declared his age as 38 years and Dropadi Bai has declared her age as 
33 years and though the original statement of claim or the Award does not speak any specific initial date of joining by 
the workmen but the management has calculated the age of the petitioners since 3]7 March 982 and found the age of 
petitioner Binod Rajak as 7 years, 05 months and 22 days on the date of his initial joining and likewise the age of the 
petitioner Bodhan Rajak has been found as 6 years, 05 months and 22 days, the age of the petitioner Jyoti Ram has 
been found as l0 years, 05 months and 23 days and that of the petitioner Dropadi Bai has been found as 5 years, 05 
months and 2! days on their respective date of initial joining which is not at all practically feasible in view of section 
67 of the Factories Act, 948 which strictly forbids anyone under the age of 4 from working in any factory aiming to 
safeguard children’s health, safety and education aligning with constitutional mandates and international labour 
standard and to prevent child labour and protect children from exploitation in industrial settings. 


4. It further transpires from the above two management exhibits that the age of these petitioners were also 
assessed by medical examination in which their age was ascertained by their appearance which is not legally 
acceptable for want of ossification test as was also rightly submitted by the learned counsel of the petitioners. 


5. As regards the submissions of the learned counsel of the petitioners that there is no basis of the date 3.03.82 
from which the management is calculating the age of the petitioners, he is right because in the statement of claim/WS 
of the workmen of Ref.no.58/92 they have not claimed any specific date from which the workmen initially started 
working but on perusal of the testimony of WW 2 Binod Rajak in Ref. no.58/l992, it is evident that he has been 
working there since 978 and if we calculate his age from the year 978 to 8.0.2009 when he sworn in notary 
affidavit and declared his age as 35 years, his age at the time of initial joining ie in the year 978, comes to 4 years 
only which further complicates the age of the petitioners in this case and makes the story of the petitioners out and out 
impossible. 


l6. Now as regards the contention of the learned counsel of the petitioners that the petitioners were appointed 
by G.M. (P&IR) Head Quarters whereas they were dismissed by the Project Officer who is below the rank of the 
Appointing Authority and so the dismissal is illegal, this Tribunal is of the view that the petitioners who were found 
to be imposters by the CCL and their services were terminated on that basis, can not claim reinstatement merely on 
the ground that the dismissal order was issued by an officer lower in rank than the appointing authority. The 
protection under Article 3l! of the constitution of India which mandates that a person can not be dismissed or 
removed by an authority subordinate to that by which they were appointed, primarily applies to government servants 
ie civil servants whereas the workmen of the company like CCL are governed by the company’s Articles of 
Association and Service Rules and not by the direct constitutional guarantee of Article 3l() in the same manner as 
civil servants. 


Th So in view of the age of the petitioners as declared by them in their respective notary affidavits sworn in 
on 8.0.2009, it is crystal clear that they are not the actual workmen in whose favour the award dated 3.0.96 was 


[भाग ए---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : फरवरी 28, 2026/फाल्गुन 9, 947 97 


passed by this Tribunal rather their documents appear to have been procured subsequently and they appear to have 
entered in the services of the CCL on those basis which is a serious misconduct being fraudulent and accordingly the 
dismissal order does not appear illegal in the above facts and circumstances of this case. 


8. In the result, on the basis of the above discussed facts, circumstances and materials available on the case 
record, this Tribunal finds and holds that the action of the management in dismissing Sri Binod Rajak (ID-9/207), 
Sri Bodhan Rajak (ID-20/207), Sri Jyoti Ram (ID-2/207) and Smt. Dropadi Bai (ID-22/207) on the ground of 
fraudulent entry into services of CCL, is quite legal and justified and the petitioners are not entitled to get any relief. 


Let the copies of Award in duplicate be sent to the Ministry of Labour & Employment, Government of India, 
New Delhi for information and notification. 


SACHINDRA KUMAR PANDEY, Presiding Officer

AWARD 


In exercise of powers conferred under clause (d) of sub-section (l) and sub —section (2A) of Section l0 of the 
Industrial Disputes Act, 947 (4 of 947) Government Of India through the Ministry of Labour, vide its Order 


9]8 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : FEBRUARY 28, 2026/PHALGUNA 9, 947 [PART II—SEc. 3(ii)] 


No. L-2002/283/99l-IR(C-IT) dated 7/l2/l992 has been pleased to refer the following dispute between the 
employer i.e. management of Rajrappa Coal Washery of M/s. CCL, Hazaribagh and their workman through Secretary, 
Colliery Kamgar Union, Rajrappa, Hazaribaghfor adjudication by this Tribunal: 


SCHEDULE 


“Whether the action of the management of Rajrappa Coal Washery of M/S C.C.L. in not regularising S/Shri 
Kartik Mahato and 60 other workers is legal and justified? If not, what relief they are entitled to?” 


2 It is important to mention here that the present Reference Case No. 2/993 was previously disposed of vide 
Award dated 25.0.2000 but thereafter the management challenged the said Award before the Hon’ble Jharkhand 
High Court by filing WP(L) No. 306 of 200] and in that writ petition the Hon’ble Court was pleased to set aside the 
award of this Tribunal and remanded the matter to this Tribunal to decide the case in the light of ratio laid down by 
the Hon’ble Supreme Court in case of Steel Authority of India Ltd. Thereafter the Tribunal again decided this case by 
passing Award dated 9.05.20l4 in favour of the workmen and the said Award was again challenged before the 
Hon’ble Jharkhand High Court in W.P.(L) No. 6327 of 20I4 that was allowed vide his Lordship order dated 
28.07.2022 and thereafter the union filed LPA No. 609 of 2022 before the Hon’ble Jharkhand High Court against the 
said order that was disposed of by the Hon’ble Court vide their Lordship order dated 0.02.2024 and the matter was 
again remanded back to this Tribunal for passing fresh award after giving opportunity to the parties to adduce 
evidence and in the light of the judgment passed by the Hon’ble Supreme Court in case of Steel Authority of 
India Ltd. 


3. The case of the workmen as per the written statement filed on behalf of the workmen is that Kartik Mahato 
and 60 other concerned workmen had been working as Slurry Cleaning Mazdoor since long with unblemished record 
of service. The concerned workmen had been working in permanent nature of job with the precinct and premises of 
the Washery under the direct control and supervision of the management. They had been working in permanent nature 
of job and have put in 240 days attendance in each calendar year. The slurry pond is a mine as decided by the Hon’ble 
Supreme Court. All the implements for execution of the job were being supplied by the management. For all purposes 
the workmen were the employees of the management still then the anti-labour management had been paying the 
concerned workmen much below the rate of NCWA. The management to camouflage the real issue and to perfect 
paper arrangement was not maintaining the statutory record of the workmen. They had been working in prohibited 
nature of job since long with unblemished record of service. The job of slurry removal/cleaning was declared 
prohibited category of job from November, 990 and the job of loading coal was declared prohibited category of job 
in February, [975. The concerned workmen had been working in prohibited category of job illegally at least on and 
from the date of prohibition the workmen shall be deemed to be glided into the service of the management. All the 
employees of the mines/washeries are getting wages as per NCWA. The workmen also represented before the 
management to pay their wages as per NCWA but till date without any effect. All other slurry cleaning mazdoors at 
Sudamdih Washery, Dugda Washery and other Washeries have been regularized by the management of M/s. BCCL 
after the pronouncement of the award and after the order of prohibition. The concerned workmen and the union 
represented before the management several times for their regularization as slurry cleaning mazdoors with 
retrospective effect and wages as per NCWA but without any effect. Seeing no other alternative the union on behalf 
of the concerned workmen raised an industrial dispute before the A.L.C.(C), Hazaribagh for their regularization on 
.4.99l. The management submitted its comments on |8.06.99] taking various plea to defect legal claim of the 
concerned workmen. During the course of conciliation proceeding the management made defence that the job of 
slurry cleaning is not permanent nature of job and all the concerned workmen whose name and photographs enclosed 
by the union along with copies of complain were not working. The union challenged the contention of the 
management by drawing the attention of the management to the various decisions of Hon’ble Supreme Court and also 
the prohibition order declared by the Govt. of India. The union also challenged to produce the statutory documents to 
prove that all the concerned workmen were not working but the management failed to establish the same and 
ultimately this case was referred to this Tribunal by the Govt. of India, Minisry of Labour. During the pendency of the 
dispute the management stopped the concerned workmen from duty w.e.f. 0.08.992 and the action of the 
management in doing so is illegal, malafide, unfortunate. The action of the management in not regularizing the 
service of the concerned workmen was illegal and unjustified. For doing the same and similar nature of job other 
slurry cleaning mazdoors are getting wages as per NCWA and at last it was prayed to answer the reference in favour 
of the workmen. 


4. On the other hand the case of the management as per the written statement cum rejoinder filed on their behalf 
is that the reference is bad in law and not maintainable. There is no industrial dispute in the present case within the 
meaning of Section 2(k) of the I.D. Act, 947. The so called persons referred to in the list attached to the reference 
order are not the members of the union and it is not competent to sponsor their case or raise any dispute with 
reference to them. There is no employer-employee relationship between the management and the so called persons 
referred to in the list. They are the strangers to this management. The reference order is misconceived and baseless 
because it calls upon the management to justify an action which is not required to take and the proposition contained 
in reference order is preposterous. The Rajrappa Coal Washery is one of the coal washeries of the C.C.L located in 
Hazaribagh District. It is a factory registered under the Factories’ Act, |948 and it is not a Mine within the meaning of 


[भाग II—ave 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : फरवरी 28, 2026/फाल्गुन 9, 947 99 


Section 2(j) of the Mines Act, 952 or a colliery. The said washery is headed by a Project Officer all along who 
directly reports to the General Manager of Rajrappa Area under which Rajrappa Colliery and other connected units 
fall. Rajrappa Washery is engaged in washing raw coal and to produce clean coal suitable for use in the steel plants. 
The raw coal is medium coking coal and in process of benefication, the volatiles like ash and moisture are reduced to 
the maximum extent possible through the very costly machinery of an elaborate nature and the carbon content of the 
final product, called the clean coal, is retained. In this process, some fluid with fine particles of sand and other coal 
rejects etc. and the rejects flows out of the Washery which is referred to as the slurry. The slurry is collected outside 
the washery premises as the washery is a factory under the Factories Act. For area into which the slurry flows, the 
appropriate Govt. is not the Central Govt. and on this ground the reference order made by the Central Govt. is without 
jurisdiction. The slurry i.e. tailings and rejects flows out into the ponds located far away from the washery. The ponds 
are not a part of the washery. The job of washery is complete when it produces the clean coal which is transported by 
rail to the steel plants for the purpose of iron, smelting and waste products like tailings/rejects generally referred to as 
slurry is not a part of the process of the washery. For some time the removal of slurry from the slurry ponds referred 
to above was entrusted on a contract basis to a Co-operative of some persons who all work by themselves. The 
workers are the shareholders of the Co-operative in question which is called the “Asangathit Shramik Sahayog Samiti 
Ltd.”, RajrappaWashery. The said society is a Co-operative society registered under the Co-operative Societies Act. It 
never had so many shareholders/workers as referred to in the Reference Order and the union concerned has 
fraudulently inflated the number of persons for securing employment for them by unfair means. The contract for 
removal of slurry was awarded to the above Co-operative Society during the following periods in different spells and 
the quantities of slurry removed by the said Co-operative Society during the different periods for which payment was 
made to it are indicated below:- 


Period quantity of slurry removed in 
Metric Tonnes. 
A .4.88 — 25.8.88 3,320.295 
B 24.0.88 — 23..89 4,999.408 
C 20.5.89 — ..90 2,56.285 
D 27.8.90 — 26.2.9] ,743.093 


The central Government purported to issue a notification prohibiting the removal of slurry through the contractors 
under section |0 of the Contract Labour (Regulation & Abolition) Act, 970 in December, 990. It was a defective 
notification and it is non est in the eye of law. It did not refer to any particular Coal Washery and its name has not 
been mentioned. In contradistinction, a similar notification issued in !975 by the Labour Ministry prohibiting contract 
system in some jobs in Coal Mines specifically mentioned that such prohibition would apply to all collieries. There is 
no such mention of all washeries in the notification issued prohibiting engagement of contractors in slurry removal in 
respect of coal washery as referred above. Considering the duration of each spell of contract and also the quantity of 
slurry removed the number of workers required would be only fraction of the number indicated in the Annexure to the 
Reference Order. At any rate after the aforesaid notification issued by the Labour Ministry towards the end of 990, 
the contract system in regard to the removal of slurry from the above ponds at Rajrappa Coal Washery was stopped. 
Even if the notification issued by the Labour Ministry is valid and if it is sought to be applied to the Rajrappa 
Washery it could not interfere with the contract running. The industrial establishments have no obligation to provide 
employment to the ex-workers of an ex-contractor and therefore the present reference is misconceived. The employers 
are not required to take any action such as that referred to in the Reference Order and it was prayed to hold that the 
management is not required to regularize any of the persons referred to this reference order and they are not entitled to 
any relief. By way of rejoinder it has been stated that the statement made in Para-| is denied. It is submitted that only 
a very small no. of workers were working as slurry cleaning mazdoors for different periods. The statement made in 
Para-2 and 3 are false and baseless and are denied accordingly. The job is also not permanent. There is no demand for 
clean coal the functioning of the washery will stop. The concerned workers did not work continuously nor under the 
control or supervision of the management. The statement made in Para-4 is also false and denied the nature of job on 
which they are engaged is not permanent. The statement made in Para-5 is also false and denied accordingly. It is 
stated that only some of the persons worked for some time. The statement made in Para- 6, 7 and 8 are 
misrepresentation and denied being false. It is submitted that the contractor had provided implements to the workers 
engaged by him as per the contract agreement. The co-operative society to pay workers wages as agreed between 
them. The statement made in Para-9 and l0 are false and not correct. As principal employer, the management did 
maintain all necessary records. The statement made in Para-l] and |2 are false and misconceived hence denied. The 
loading and unloading of coal manually is prohibited under C.L. (R&A) Act only in coal mines. The statement made 
in Para-l3 and 4 are not correct hence denied. There are some coal mines and other mines and in the coal mines of 
B.S.M.D.C. workers are not getting wages as per NCWA and the contractors workers are also not entitled to the 
wages as per the same. The statement made in Para-|5 and |6 are not correct and relevant hence denied accordingly. 
In reply to the statement made in Para-I7 it is submitted that the union concerned raised a purported dispute before 


920 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : FEBRUARY 28, 2026/PHALGUNA 9, 947 [PART II—SEc. 3(ii)] 


the A.L.C.(C), Hazaribagh and this is a matter of record. In reply to the statement made in Para-8 it is submitted that 
save and except what is a matter of record the rest of the statements are denied. It is denied that the management 
failed to establish its stand or that it took any frivolous plea. In reply to Para-9 it is stated that the management did 
not employ any of the persons so no question of stopping them from duty. In reply to Para-20, 2] and 22 it is stated 
that these are denied being false and misconceived. In reply to Para-23 it is stated that the Eastern Coalfields Ltd. does 
not have any coal washeries. Therefore, the reference to E.C.L. is irrelevant. In reply to Para-24 it is stated that it is 
based on misrepresentations and the prayer is not legally tenable at any rate and at last it was prayed to reject the 
prayer/claim of the union. 


5. A rejoinder has also been filed on behalf of the workmen to the W/S filed by the management in which all 
claims of the management have been denied.

0. Heard both sides and perused the materials on record. 


Il. The Ld. Counsel on behalf of the concerned workmen has submitted that all the workmen had been working 
in slurry removal/cleaning work in Rajrappa Coal Washery since 988 regularly and continuously under the direct 
control and supervision of the management and they had been producing goods for the benefit of the management. 
The work of slurry removal is permanent nature of job but they had not been paid by the management their wages as 
per the rate of NCWA in the name of intermediaries namely Asangathit Shramik Sahayog Samittee Ltd. Rajrappa. 
They were not member of that alleged Samittee and they had never worked under the control and supervision of the 
same. The slurry cleaning job was prohibited by the Central Government notification from |.2.990 but even 
thereafter they have been continuously working in slurry removal and therefore they are entitled for regularization. 


2. On the other hand the Ld. Counsel on behalf of the management has submitted that the work of removal of 
slurry from the slurry pond was entrusted on contract basis to the co-operative of persons who all worked by 
themselves. The workers are the shareholders of the co-operative which is called Asangathit Shramik Sahayog 
Samittee Ltd., Rajrappa Washery and the said Co-operative Society is registered under the Co-operative Societies Act 
and it never had so many shareholders/workers as referred to in the reference order and the union has fraudulently 
inflated the number of persons for securing employment for them by unfair means. A contract for removal of slurry 


922 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : FEBRUARY 28, 2026/PHALGUNA 9, 947 [PART II—SEc. 3(ii)] 


was awarded to the above Co-operative Society from 988 to 990 in different spells of the period. The Central 
Government issued notification under section l0 of the C.L.(R&A) Act, 970 in December, 990 prohibiting the 
Category-I of Slurry through the contractors but that was meant for the collieries and are not applicable to coal 
washery. The concerned workmen are the contractor workmen and they have neither been issued the appointment 
letter nor the pay slip nor identity cards by the management and the contractor’s workmen are not entitled to 
regularization. Even after Central Government notification under Section 0 of the C.L. (R&A) Act, 970 the contract 
workers are not entitled to automatic regularization and at last it was prayed to reject the reference and the prayer of 
the union for regularization of the workmen. 


3. Issue 


Whether the action of the management of Rajrappa Coal Washery of M/S C.C.L. in not regularising S/Shri 
Kartik Mahato and 60 other workers is legal and justified? If not, what relief they are entitled to? 


4. Now as regards the oral evidence of the workmen, WW-|, Brij Lal Mahato has deposed that he has been 
working at Rajrappa Washery since 988, engaged in slurry removal work. The concerned workmen removed this 
slurry from the tanks which are also called ponds, stack at outside the pond and also load the same on trucks daily. 
This work is of permanent nature and the management of CCL supervised their work and they are paid their wages. 
He has denied that they were appointed by a Co-operative Society and they worked under it. The slurry work shall 
continue so long the washery is functioning there. He has denied that slurry is removed only when electricity is off. 
All the concerned workmen have worked for more than 240 days in a year but they are not getting the wages as per 
NCWA. They demanded wages and facility of a permanent worker and have claimed regularization from the date 
they started working with back wages. In the cross-examination he has stated that they have not been provided 
Identity Cards nor were given appointment letters, wage slips. They entered into the premises of the washery without 
using any identity card and he has denied that no one can enter into the washery premises without identity cards. He 
has also denied that the management never had put them to this work or that sometimes this work is given by the 
management to Co-operative Societies. He has further denied that for this work Co-operative Society appoints its own 
work-force and for doing this work the management makes payment to those Co-operative Societies and that the 
workers are paid by the Co-operative Society. He has also denied that neither the management has made any payment 
to the concerned workmen nor ever gave them working implements nor supervised their work. Daily 40 to 50 trucks 
are loaded with slurry and I6 to 8 tons of slurry are loaded on one truck. 


WW-2 Sohan Bedia and WW-3 Dhaniram Mahato have also supported and corroborated the facts stated by WW-I in 
his examination- in-chief. In the cross-examination WW-2 has stated that he can not produce any appointment order 
given to him by the management. He can not produce any I.D. Card of Mine to show that he was working in CCL. 
Their union supplied photo copies of documents that they have filed and he can not produce any document that CCL 
was paying to him. 


Likewise WW-3 has stated in his cross-examination that he was not issued any I.D. Card nor anyone else was issued 
the same. He was working since 20.04.988 but neither any appointment letter nor any pay slip was issued to him. 
They were not engaged through the contractor. The Officers of CCL were directly taking work from them. The work 
of slurry removal was done manually by them and not by the machine. Even if the work of slurry removal was given 
to workers Co-operative Society prior to the deployment of machine but that was being done by these workmen 
themselves. He has very clearly admitted that they were working under Workers Co-operative Society.

dumping trucks.

8. On perusal of the entire oral as well as documentary evidence available on the case record it is evident that 
M/s. Asangathit Shramik Sahayog Samiti Ltd is a licensed contractor (co operative samiti) and under agreement and 
under different work orders issued by the CCL Rajrappa Area it has been awarded the work of slurry removal 
manually from the settling ponds (outside washery) and stacking the same within a radius of 30 Mtrs and WW-3 
Dhaniram Mahato in Paragraph 5 of his cross-examination has very clearly admitted that they were working under 
Co-operative Society and in Paragraph-|0 of his cross-examination he has admitted that he was working since 20.4.88 
and the oral evidence of the workman as well as the agreement and work orders of the different dates goes to establish 
and support the case of the workmen that since the year 988 these workmen have been doing the work of removal of 
slurry from settling ponds of Rajrappa Washery manually and judicial notice is taken to the fact that on l]% 
December |990 the Central Govt. issued a notification u/s 0 of the C.L.(R&A) Act, 970 prohibiting the removal of 
slurry manually by the contract workers and this is also a fact that the work of removal of slurry in a coal washery is a 
perennial and permanent nature of job and after the said notification by the Central Government it became the 
prohibited category of job but the case record shows that even after this notification the Rajrappa Washery continued 
to take this work from the concerned workmen and even after that they are not being paid the wages equal to those of 
the permanent employees. The Hon’ble Supreme Court in case of Steel Authority of India Ltd. & Others Vs. 
National Union Waterfront Workers and others, have been pleased to hold that on issuance of prohibition 
notification under Section 0(]) of the C.L.R.A Act, 970 prohibiting employment of contract labour or otherwise, in 
an industrial dispute brought before it by any contract labour in regard to the conditions of service, the industrial 
adjudicator will have to consider the question whether the contractor has been interposed either on the ground of 
having undertaken to produce any given result for the establishment or for supply of contract labour for work of the 
establishment under a genuine contract or is a mere ruse/camouflage to evade compliance of various beneficial 
legislations so as to deprive the workers of the benefit thereunder and if the contract is found to be not genuine but a 
mere camouflage, the so called contract labour will have to be treated as employees of the principal employer who 
shall be directed to regularize the services of the contract labour in the establishment concerned subject to the 
conditions as may be specified by it for that purpose in the light of Para 6 hereunder and in Para 6 of the said 
judgment it has been held that if the contract is found to be genuine and prohibition notification under section l0() of 
the CLRA Act, 970 in respect of the establishment concerned has been issued by the appropriate government 
prohibiting employment of contract labour in any process, operation or other work of any establishment and where in 
such process/operation or other work of the establishment, the principal employer intends to employ regular 
workmen, he shall give preference to be erstwhile contract labour, if otherwise, found suitable and if necessary, by 
relaxing the condition as to maximum age appropriately taking into consideration the age of the workers at the time of 
their initial employment by the contractor and also relaxing the condition as to academic qualifications other than 
technical qualifications. 


9. Here in the instant case the Contract vide Ext.M-4/l made on 37 July 989 between Central Coal Field Ltd. 
Rajrappa Area, Ranchi and M/s. Asangathit Shramik Sahayog Samittee Ltd. Rajrappa as contractor is quite genuine 
and the Central Government has also issued a notification on !" December 990 in respect of the prohibition of 
slurry removal prohibiting the employment of contract labour, it is directed to the management that if he intends to 
employ regular workmen for the removal of slurry he shall give preference to the concerned workmen, if they are 
found otherwise suitable by relaxing the conditions as to maximum age appropriately taking into consideration the 
age of the concerned workmen at the time of their initial employment by the contractor and also by relaxing the 
conditions as to academic qualifications if any. The reference is answered accordingly. 


Let the copies of Award along with list of workmen in duplicate be sent to the Ministry of Labour & 
Employment, Government of India, New Delhi for information and notification. 


SACHINDRA KUMAR PANDEY, Presiding Officer

का.आ. 494.--औद्योगिक विवाद अधिनियम, i947 (4947 का 4) की धारा ॥7 के अनुसरण में, केन्द्रीय 
सरकार बीसीसीएल के प्रबंधतंत्र के संबद्ध नियोजकों और उनके कर्मकारों के बीच, अनुबंध में निर्दिष्ट औद्योगिक विवाद में 
केन्द्रीय सरकार औद्यागिक अधिकरण-सह- श्रम न्यायालय, धनबाद-। के पंचाट (संदर्भ संख्या 332/2000) को

No. L-2002/30/2000 (C-I) dated 22/ /2000 has been pleased to refer the following dispute between the employer 
i.e. management of Sijua Area of M/s. BCCL, Dhanbad and their workman through Organizing Secretary, Rashtriya 
Colliery Mazdoor Sangh, Dhanbad for adjudication by this Tribunal: 


SCHEDULE 
“क्या बी. सी. सी. ve, मुदिदीह कोल्ियरी के प्रबंधतंत्र द्वारा श्री निदरवा agar को उनकी अनुपस्थिति के 
कारण दि. 8.7.94 & बर्खास्त किया जाना विधिवत, उचित एवं न्‍यायसंगत है? यदि नहीं तो कर्मकार अथवा 
उनके आश्रित किस राहत के पात्र हैं?” 


[भाग ए---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : फरवरी 28, 2026/फाल्गुन 9, 947 927 


2. On receiving order no. L-2002/30/2000 (C-I) dated 22/]/2000 Government of India, Ministry of Labour, 
New Delhi for adjudication of the dispute, Reference case no. 332 of 2000 was registered on 26.2.2000 and 
thereafter the notices were sent to the parties with a direction to appear and submit their written statements along with 
relevant documents and witnesses in support of their claims. 


3. After service of notice, both parties appeared previously and filed their authorities, W/S and subsequently 
rejoinder. It appears from the case record that this case is received from the Ministry for workman namely Nidekha 
Bhuiya but on 5.0.2002 a corrigendum letter dated 0.0!.2022 issued by Ministry received by this Tribunal stating 
therein that the name of workman is corrected as “Nidarwa Bhuiya” in place of “Nidekha Bhuiya”. After that this case 
is running in the name of concerned workmen namely Nidarwa Bhutya. It further appears that after 2006 this case was 
put up after a long gap on 0.0.209 and registered notices were issued to both the parties but both the parties failed 
to appear before this Tribunal. Though on 26.09.2025, Sri N. Nath, O.S. Legal HQ appeared for the management but 
workman/union again failed to appear. It transpires from the case record that workman/union failed to appear before 
the Tribunal since the year 20l9 which shows that workman/union has lost his interest in this case and therefore, this 
Tribunal is of the opinion that the case deserves to be dismissed due to non prosecution. 


4. Hence, 
ORDERED 


that this case is hereby dismissed for non prosecution and a “No Dispute Award” be drawn up in respect of the 
above reference case. Let the copies of Award in duplicate be sent to the Ministry of Labour & Employment, 
Government of India, New Delhi for information and notification. 


SACHINDRA KUMAR PANDEY, Presiding Officer

In exercise of powers conferred under clause (d) of sub-section (l) and sub —section (2A) of Section l0 of 
the Industrial Disputes Act, |947 (4 of 947) Government Of India through the Ministry of Labour, vide its Order 
No.L-2002/463/996-IR(C-I) dated 29/03/200l has been pleased to refer the following dispute between the 
employer i.e. management of Katras Area of M/s. BCCL, Dhanbad and their workman through Secretary, Rashtriya 
Colliery MazdoorSangh, Dhanbadfor adjudication by this Tribunal:

“क्या राष्ट्रीय कोलियरी मजदूर संघ की मांग कि भा. को. को. ल्रिमि.,कतरास क्षेत्र-4 के प्रबंधतंत्र द्वारा श्री दिलीप कुमार 
एवं सूची में दिए गए 3 अन्य कर्मकारों (जनरल मजदूर, ड्रेसर एवं स्वीपर) को जिन्हें वर्ष 985 से काम से हटाया गया, 
सेवा मे पुरःस्थापित किया जाए तथा भा. को. को. लिमि. में नियमित किया जाए उचित एवं न्‍यायसंगत है? यदि हां तो 
उक्त कर्मकार किस राहत के पात्र हैं तथा किस तारीख से?”

2. The case of the workmen as per the written statement filed on their behalf is that Sri Dilip Kumar Gope and 


3 others as mentioned in Annexure of the order of reference had been performing the work in different jobs as a 
casual workman at Central Hospital, Katras under Katras Area No. IV of BCCL prior to 983. They had been 
engaged in different jobs against the permanent vacant post and they continuously worked in different jobs upto 985. 
Prior to their illegal & arbitrary stoppage the management had not complied section 25F & 250 of the I.D. Act, 947. 
Central Hospital Katras was earlier known as Regional Hospital, Katras and was directly under the control and 
supervision of the then Coal Mines Welfare Organizgation (Ministry of Labour, Govt. of India). At the time of take 
over of this Regional Hospital there were more than 2] casual workers who had been performing work in different 
jobs. Although this strength of casual workers were not sufficient for such a big hospital yet due to sincerity of these 
workmen this Hospital was smoothly running. After prolong discussion between the union and management, 8 casual 


[भाग I— ave 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : फरवरी 28, 2026/फाल्गुन 9, 947 929 
workers out of 22 were taken back in employment and allowed to do the job in the year 988., At that time it was 
assured that rest workmen shall also be taken in employment immediately but later on the management declined to 
allow them in duty. These workmen were continued in service up to 985 on the basis of the approval accorded by 
the General Manager and the other higher authority. For the purpose of their regularization the Medical 
Superintendent, Central Hospital, Katras had initiated several note sheet but all went in vain. On 0.0.84 the Medical 
Superintendent, Central Hospital, Katras had requested the higher authority to accord approval for permanent 
deployment of these workmen as there was an acute shortage of Para Medical Staffs & Workers. The Medical 
Superintendent of Central Hospital Katras has highly recommended and forwarded the case of the workmen 
concerned to the General Manager, Katras Area for their regularization vide letter dated 2!..85. In response to the 
said letter the Personnel Manager had also requested to the Medical Superintendent to furnish some information vide 
letter dated 4.2.85. The Medical Superintendent had sent a reply of the same on 24.2.85 and thus the Medical 
Superintendent several times had recommended and forwarded their case for regularization through different letters 
but all went in vain. The workmen concerned had also made several representations to the higher authority for their 
regularization but nothing was done. Their matter was also discussed in the meeting held between the union and 
management on several dates but they were only assured. When the management regularized Sri Jadu and 7 others of 
similar situated case then by not regularizing the services of Sri Dilip Kumar Gope and 3 others in their respective 
posts itself shows discriminatory policy of the management and all these facts and circumstances clearly show that the 
action of the management is unjustified and ultimately this industrial dispute was raised and it was prayed to pass 
award directing the employer to reinstate Sri Dilip Kumar and ]3 others in the services of the M/s. BCCL in their 
respective posts with retrospective effect and all consequential benefits. 


3. On the other hand the case of the management as per the written statement cum rejoinder filed on their behalf 
is that the reference is not legally maintainable. The persons concerned were never appointed by the employer and 
they never worked in BCCL in any permanent sanctioned post. They were in fact engaged by Central Hospital Katras 
which was under the control and ownership of Coal Mines Welfare Organization purely on casual basis as and when 
required. Consequent upon taking over of Central Hospital Katras by BCCL from Coal Mines Welfare Organization 
all the permanent employees who were on the roll of Central Hospital Katras were taken over by BCCL in the roll of 
the Company. The persons concerned were not on the roll of the erstwhile owner of the Central Hospital Katras, 
hence the question of taking over their services in BCCL does not arise. The Union under the banner of Rastriya 
Colliery Mazdoor Sangh who has raised the present dispute had earlier raised an industrial dispute in the year 988 
demanding regularization of casual workers in the roll of BCCL. The aforesaid dispute ended in an amicable 
settlement and a bipartite settlement was arrived at between the management and union on 30.3.88 and it was agreed 
that 8 casual sweepers who had completed 240 days attendance in one year would be taken as Badli Sweeper. In fact 
there were total 22 casual workers under Coal Mines Welfare Organization out of which 8 persons were taken on roll 
of BCCL as explained above, and the remaining |4 persons i.e. concerned persons did not complete 240 days 
attendance in any year starting from 983, [984 and I985. The attendance of these persons for 3 years has been given 
as under:- 


SI. No. Name 983 984 985 
2 S/Sri Dilip Kr. Gope 36 05 03
