It is clear from the above that none of the person concerned has got any claim for regularization in the roll of BCCL 
as they have failed to fulfill the requisite conditions for demanding regularization. By way of rejoinder it has been 
stated inter-alia that as the persons concerned were not retrenched from the services, Section 25F and 25G of the I.D. 
Act, 947 do not apply. None of these persons had put on continuous service for 240 days in any year during 983, 
984 and 985, hence they are outside the purview and jurisdiction of Section 25F of the I.D. Act, 947 and 
consequently Section 25G of the said Act is not applicable and all other claims of the workmen have been denied 
stating that Sri Dilip Kumar Gope and |3 others have not completed 240 days attendance in a year. 


4. In support of the case of the workmen, ४५७-] Ashok Kumar Singh has been examined. 

5: He has also adduced the following photo copies of the documentary evidence: 

Mark X for Identification- Request of Approval for deployment of casual workers. 

Mark 2/] - Copy of proforma for regularization of casual workers. 

Mark X/2 - Letter dated 7.09.200 and 0.02.20I written to Joint Secretary, R.C.M.S by the workmen. 
Mark X/3 — Copy of Award dated 0.09.204. 

Mark X/4 — Notary Affidavit 

Mark X/5 — Copy of order dated 29.8.2023 passed by Hon’ble Jharkhand High Court. 


Mark X/6 — Note sheet and copy of Memorandum of Settlement arrived at between management and union dated 
7.3.988. 


6. The Management has examined MW-! Rana Santosh Kumar Singh but has not adduced any documentary 
evidence. 
hs Heard both sides and perused the materials on record. 


The workman namely Sri Ashok Kumar Singh himself advanced his arguments and submitted that total 22 casual 
labourers were working as Sweeper, Dresser and general mazdoor in Central Hospital Katras that was subsequently 
taken over by the BCCL out of whom 8 were taken on the roll of the company and their services were regularized as 
Badli Mazdoor in the year 988 but the others whose cases were similar, were left by the BCCL which is 
discrimination and they are entitled to be regularized like the other casual workers and prayed to allow his case and 
pass award in favour of the workmen. 


8. On the other hand the Ld. Counsel on behalf of the management has submitted that out of 22 casual workers, 
8 casual workers/sweepers who had completed 240 days attendance in a year, were taken on the roll of the BCCL as 
Badli Sweeper and the cases of the concerned workmen were not considered because none of them had completed 
240 days of the attendance in a year and thus they have no right to regularization nor they have right to the 
compensation under section 25F and 250 of the I.D. Act, 947 and prayed to reject the award. 


9. Issue 


Whether the demand of the Rashtriya Colliery Mazdoor Sangh that Sri Dilip Kumar and |3 other workers 
(General Mazdoor, Dressers and Sweepers) listed in the annexure, who were removed from service by the 
management of BCCL, Katras Area-4 in 985, be reinstated and regularized in BCCL, is just and proper? If so, to 
what reliefs are the said workers entitled to and from which date? 


0. As regards the oral evidence of the workmen, WW-l Ashok Kumar Singh who is one of the concerned 
workmen, has deposed that he was appointed in the Central Hospital Katras BCCL as Casual Worker on 0.03.983 
for the post of Dresser. Total 22 persons had been working on different posts on casual basis since 0.03.983 and 
they were being paid wages at the rate of Rs. 8.70 per day. They submitted their application to the management of 
BCCL requesting to declare their services as permanent but the management on 9.05.988 declared only 8 persons 
as permanent employee and remaining others were left on the ground that their attendance were less than 240 days in 
a year but the true fact is that none of the workmen including those 8 persons, had completed attendance of 240 days 
in a year. As the management has shown discriminatory policy and violated Articles of law and denied regularization, 
which is totally unjust step taken against them by the management. In the cross-examination he has stated that he was 
working in the BCCL as Casual worker and not as permanent worker. He was paid Rs. 8.70/- per day. He has annexed 
the documents that has details of 22 employees and all of them were working as casual workers and were being paid 
Rs. 8.70/- per day. 


Il. As regards the documentary evidence though all the documents are photo copies but all are important for the 
purpose of this case out of which mark ‘X’ for Identification is the request letter for approval written by Medical 
Superintendent Central Hospital Katras dated 0.0.984 for deployment of casual workers describing that vide note 
sheet dated 7.7.83 approval for deployment of casual workers was given earlier which has been extended up to 
December 984. On its perusal it is evident that the strength of the workers on roll are few in comparison to the 


[भाग II—ave 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : फरवरी 28, 2026/फाल्गुन 9, 947 93] 


minimum required strength and that is why to run the hospital deployment of casual workers will continue till 
minimum requirement is recruited to maintain cleanliness of hospital as well as day to day work and the names of the 
concerned casual workmen are also mentioned therein. Mark X/I is Proforma for regularization of casual workers 
that contains the names of the casual workers which shows that they were working as sweepers, dressers etc since 
.3.83 and they worked up to 985. Mark X/2 is the letter written by the concerned workmen to the Joint Secretary of 
R.C.M.S. for change of the panel advocate. Mark X/3 is the copy of Award dated 0.09.204 and on its perusal it 
transpires that previously No Dispute Award was passed in this case. Mark X/4 is notary affidavit of Sri Giridhar 
Mishra whch is not legible. Mark X/5 is the copy of order dated 29.08.2023 passed by the Hon’ble Jharkhand High 
Court in L.P.A.No. 28 of 2022 and on its perusal it transpires that the Hon’ble Court have been pleased to set aside 
the award passed by this Tribunal as well as the order of the Hon’ble High Court in W.P. (L) No. 557 of 20l9 and 
this reference has been restored with direction to provide opportunities to the parties to lead evidence and pass an 
award in accordance with law. Mark X/6 Series are the note sheet of the Medical Superintendent dated 06.2.984 
by which the General Manager, Katras Area was requested to extend the approval earlier given for the deployment of 
casual workers in the hospital etc. Another important document of this series is the photo copy of Memorandum of 
Settlement arrived at between the management of Area-IV and Shri L.P. Singh, Secretary, R.C.M.S. on 7.3.988 
and on its perusal it transpires that 8 casual workers who were working as Sweeper in Regional Hospital Katras, after 
it was taken other by the BCCL, they were kept in casual pool of sweepers at Central Hospital Katras for deployment 
as Badli Sweeper against leave and sick vacancy of permanent sweepers in the hospital. 


2. On the other hand १॥५७४-], Rana Santosh Kumar Singh has deposed that in the year 983, BCCL had taken 
over the management of Katras Central Hospital in which 22 casual workers were working at that time out of whom 8 
suitable persons who had complete 240 days attendance in a year were regularized whereas the attendance of Sri 
Ashok Kumar was only II] days and therefore, he was not regularized. In cross-examination he has stated that he 
does not remember the names of all the 22 workers working on casual basis and he also does not remember the names 
of those 8 workers who were regularized. He also does not remember the date of settlement. It has further stated that 
he also does not remember in which year Ashok Kumar Singh has ll days attendance and he also does not 
remember whose attendance out of those 8 suitable persons, was 240 days or above the same. 


3. Admittedly total 22 casual workers were working in Central Hospital Katras some of whom were working as 
Sweeper and others were working as Dresser etc. out of whom 8 casual workmen/sweepers were regularized vide 
settlement arrived at between the management and the union on 7.03.988 but rest l4 casual workers were left and 
not considered for the same. 


4. Now the Management has taken the plea that those casual workers who had completed 240 days attendance 
in a year were regularized as Badli Sweeper and as the other casual workers had not completed 240 days attendance 
therefore, their cases were not considered for the same and in support of the same the management has also given oral 
evidence of ५७- Rana Santosh Kumar Singh who has deposed that the 8 casual workers had completed 240 days 
attendance in a year so they were regularized and the concerned workmen had not completed 240 days attendance in a 
year so they were not considered for regularization but to corroborate the same the management has failed to 
submit/furnish/produce even a single chit of paper which may reveal/prove that 8 casual workers who were 
regularized by the BCCL, had completed 240 days attendance in a year and on the contrary WW-|l Ashok Kumar 
Singh has given his testimony that those 8 workmen had also not completed 240 days attendance in a year which goes 
to prove that the management has adopted pick and choose policy which is illegal. 


I5. Thus on the basis of the above discussions of the facts, circumstances and evidences on record it is crystal 
clear that the Management regularized the services of only 8 casual workmen but left other similarly situated 
workmen which is out and out discrimination and violation of principles of natural justice as well as the spirit of 
Article-l4 and 6 of the Constitution of India. The principle of equality and non- discrimination is the corner stone of 
Indian Labour Jurisprudence. This is settled principle of law that similarly situated workmen can not be discriminated 
solely on the ground that they have not completed 240 days of attendance in a year. 


l6. So in the light of the above this Tribunal comes to the conclusion that all the concerned l4 workmen are 
entitled to be reinstated by the BCCL but it appears that they were stopped from working in the year 985 and now 40 
years have already gone so in the best interest of justice the Management is directed to give compensation of Rs. 
5,00,000/- to each of the concerned workmen within a period of 3 months from the date of publication of the Award. 
The reference is answered accordingly. 


Let the copies of Award in duplicate be sent to the Ministry of Labour & Employment, Government of India, 
New Delhi for information and notification. 


SACHINDRA KUMAR PANDEY, Presiding Officer

AWARD. 


In exercise of powers conferred under clause (d) of sub-section (l) and sub —section (2A) of Section l0 of 
the Industrial Disputes Act, |947 (/4 of 947),Government Of India through the Ministry of Labour, vide its Order 
No.L-2002/46/200l-IR(C-I) dated 29//200l has been pleased to refer the following dispute between the 
employer i.e. management of Sudamdih Incline Mine of M/s. BCCL, Dhanbad and their workman through Vice 
President, Rashtriya Colliery Mazdoor Sangh, Dhanbad for adjudication by this Tribunal: 


SCHEDULE 


“Whether the management of Sudamdih Incline Mine is justified in dismissing Sri Dhani Bhuian from 
service? If not, to what relief is the workman entitled?” 


2. On receiving order no. L-2002/46/200l-IR(C-I) dated 29//200l Government of India, Ministry of Labour, 
New Delhi for adjudication of the dispute, Reference case no. 257 of 200! was registered on 06.2.200l and 
thereafter the notices were sent to the parties with a direction to appear and submit their written statements along with 
relevant documents and witnesses in support of their claims. 


[भाग ए---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : फरवरी 28, 2026/फाल्गुन 9, 947 933 


3. After service of notice, both parties appeared previously and filed their authorities, W/S and subsequently 
rejoinder. It further appears that after 2007 this case was put up after a long gap on |8.0.202] and registered notices 
were issued to both the parties but workman/union did not appear before this Tribunal and the notice of the workman 
returned with endorsement “Addressee Left”. However Sri D.K. Verma, Ld. Advocate on behalf of the management 
appeared. It transpires from the case record that workman/union failed to appear before the Tribunal since the year 
202! which shows that workman/union has lost his interest in this case and therefore, this Tribunal is of the opinion 
that the case deserves to be dismissed due to non prosecution. 


4. Hence, 
ORDERED 


that this case is hereby dismissed for non prosecution and a “No Dispute Award” be drawn up in respect of the 
above reference case. Let the copies of Award in duplicate be sent to the Ministry of Labour & Employment, 
Government of India, New Delhi for information and notification. 


SACHINDRA KUMAR PANDEY, Presiding Officer 
नई दिल्‍ली, 6 फरवरी, 2026

AWARD 


In exercise of powers conferred under clause (d) of sub-section (l) and sub —section (2A) of Section l0 of 
the Industrial Disputes Act, |947 (4 of 947) Government Of India through the Ministry of Labour, vide its Order 
No. L-2002/96/2005-IR(C-IT) dated 04/0/20l0 has been pleased to refer the following dispute between the 
employer i.e. management of Bhowra (N) Colliery of M/s. BCCL, Bhowra, Dhanbad and their workman through 
General Secretary, Colliery KarmcharySangh, Patherdih, Dhanbadfor adjudication by this Tribunal: 


SCHEDULE 


“Whether the action of the management of Bhowra(N) Colliery of M/s. BCCL in dismissing Shri 
Bishnu Kumhar and Dwarka Kumhar Bhowra(G) Colliery of M/s. BCCL is justified? If so, to what 
relief the concerned workmen are entitled?” 


2 The case of the workmen as per the written statement filed on 5.2.20l0 is that the workmen Bishnu 
Kumhar and Dwarika Kumhar respectively were taken into employment vide order dated 04.05.987-2.05.987 No 
GM-PER/TEMP-Rectt (M) L/87-5807 -ll and dated 04.05.987-2.05.!987 No.GM-PER/TEMP-Rectt. (M) 
L/87/582-6. Both of them were charge sheeted vide No. PS/95/(N) U.G. Mines/CS/I52 dated 09-/2/l995 and 
PS/95/(N) U.G. Mines/CS/I5 dated 09-I/2/995 respectively and dismissed from employment by the management 
vide office order dated 03.2.996, No. PS/96/(N) U.G. Mines/Dismiss/I584 and dated 03-09/2/996, No. PS/96 (N) 
U.G. Mines/Dismiss/l583 respectively. It has further been stated that they were taken into employment by the 
management against especial vacancy of 5000 Miners/Loaders for SC/ST/Land Losers/prospective land-Losers in the 
year |986 whose land acquired by the Govt./Govt. Coal Company, vested in BCCL and name sponsored by the 
employment exchange as per guidelines of the management, meant for the purpose followed by Circular No. 
D(P)/PS/87/22 dated 03.02.987. Enquiry was ordered by the Disciplinary Authority after submission of reply of 
charge sheet of the workmen concerned. The enquiry was conducted and ultimately the concerned workmen were 
held guilty by hook & Crook proving charge on which basis the concerned workmen were dismissed by the 
Disciplinary Authority on concocted allegation. The claim of the concerned workmen to reinstate them are just and 
proper and must be considered to save the family of dismissed workmen from starvation and at last it was prayed to 
set aside the action of the management and pass order for reinstatement of the workmen concerned with full back 
wages and attendance. 


3. On the other hand the case of the management as per the W/S filed on 0.07.202 is that the above named 
workmen i.e. Bishnu Kumhar S/o Sri Jagarnath Kumhar was appointed under Land Looser Scheme to the post of 
U.G. Miner/Loader in Group VA vide offer of appointment No. 5807-l] dated 4/2.5.87 and posted at Bhowra 
(North) Colliery initially for a period of 3 months and Sri Dwarika Kumhar S/o Sri Sahadeo Kumhar under Land 
Looser Scheme to the post of UG Miner/Loader in Group VA vide offer of appointment No. 582-l6 dated 
4/2.05.987 and posted at Bhowra (North) Colliery initially for a period of 3 months with the laid down terms and 
conditions. The above named persons/their father had offered the land for getting employment under the Land Looser 
Scheme as under:- 


Bishnu Kumhar S/o Jagarnath Kumhar of Bhowra: 


Mauza___ Mouza No. Khata No. Plot No. Area 
Bhowra l2 37 2028 0.0 
2347 0.24 
Dwarika KumharS/o Sahdeo Kumhar: 
Mauza_Mouza No. Khata No. Plot No. Area 
Bhowra 2 37 8i2 0.02 
49 29 0.06 


After scrutiny of the papers it was detected that Sri Bishnu Kumhar had got employment fraudulently and a charge- 
sheet was issued to him for making false declaration that he owns 0.34 decimal of land under Khata No. 37 as per 
details indicated in Para 3 above and subsequently it was proved that one Sri Munilal Kumhar also put his 
representation dated 3!.05.995 for getting employment having 50% of the land in question which was declared by 
the above named employee. Consequently he was issued a charge-sheet No. 52 dated 9/I.2.95 under Para 26..] 
& 26..5 of the Certified Standing Orders of the Company by the Disciplinary Authority. In case of Sri Dwarika 
Kumhar it was detected on scrutiny later on that the said quantum of land did not belong to Sri Sahdeo Kumhar as one 
Sri Munilal Kumhar has submitted his representation dated 3.0.995 before the management claiming that 50% of 
land under Plot No 87, Khata No. 37 Area 0.2 decimal in question belong to him and had requested for getting 
employment against the said land. Thus it was found that the above named employee had got employment by giving 
false declaration. He was issued a charge-sheet vide No. I5 dated 9/I.2.995 under Para 26.l.l] and 26..I5 of 
the Certified Standing Order of the company by the Disciplinary Authority. The reply submitted by them were not 


[भाग II—ave 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : फरवरी 28, 2026/फाल्गुन 9, 947 935 


found satisfactory and so the Disciplinary Authority decided to get the matter enquired into by appointing the Enquiry 
Officer and M.R. Both the above named workmen had fully participated in the enquiry, ample opportunities were 
afforded to them in the course of enquiry by way of natural justice. The notices were issued by the Enquiry Officer 
with copy to all concerned and submitted his findings. The Disciplinary Authority had given the copy of the enquiry 
report by way of 270 show cause notice vide letter no. PS/96/(N)/UG ४श॥०४/ध534 dated 29..996 to Sri Bishnu 
Kumhar and vide No. PS/96(N)/UG Mines/l533 dated 29..996 but no reply was submitted to the above notice. 
The Disciplinary Authority on getting the Enquiry Report from the Enquiry Officer and exhibited documents etc. had 
applied his mind and come to the conclusion that the charges leveled against both the workmen were fully established 
and had decided to impose the penalty of dismissal from services of the company upon both the above named 
employees. The Disciplinary Authority had issued the letter vide No. 584 dated 3/9.2.996 to the workman Sri 
Bishnu Kumhar for his dismissal from the services of the company with immediate effect and thus he was dismissed 
and accordingly advised to handover all the articles and quarter etc. if any, allotted and get the legal dues from the 
office. The Disciplinary Authority had issued the letter vide No. !583 dated 3/9.2.996 to the workman Sri Dwarika 
for his dismissal from the services of the company with immediate effect and thus he was dismissed and accordingly 
advised to handover all the articles and quarter etc. if any, allotted and get the legal dues from the office. Both the 
above named ex-employees had filed a Writ vide CWJC No. 20I/200l and 202/200! and subsequently had 
withdrawn the same which were allowed with the liberty of raising an Industrial Dispute. It has further been stated 
that it is evident that the action of the management of Bhowra (North) Colliery under E.J. Area, Bhowra of M/s. 
BCCL is just, fair and quite reasonable in dismissing Sri Bishnu Kumhar and Dwarika Kumhar from the services of 
the company who got the employment fraudulently by giving false declaration of land although the said quantum of 
land did not belong to them and thus they are not entitled to get any relief in the case. In rejoinder it has been stated 
that the contents vide Para-| of the W/S are about the reference. The contents vide Para-2 of the W/S of the workmen 
are about getting employment against Land Looser Scheme. The contents vide Para-3 of the W/S of the workmen are 
about taking disciplinary action by the Disciplinary Authority. The contents of Para-4 of the W/S are about the 
method of employment and selection after declaration of land. The contents vide Para-5 of the W/S are about holding 
of the enquiry after getting reply. The contents of Para-6 of the W/S are not correct and baseless. The enquiry was 
held by the Enquiry Officer where full opportunity was provided to both the charge-sheeted workmen to defend 
themselves. No complaint against the enquiry officer was there about holding the enquiry. The contents of Para-7 of 
the W/S are not correct and baseless. The pre-condition of the offer of appointment was declaration of quantum of 
land and upon that the employment was provided to both of them but later on it was found that the said quantum of 
land did not belong to them and another claimant from the same plot etc. has demanded employment apart from the 
employment provided once to the workmen. Thus the employment was obtained fraudulently by the false 
information. The contents of Para-8 of the W/S has not been commented and at last it was stated that if the enquiry 
was not found fair and proper, the management may be allowed to lead evidence to prove their case before this Court 
and prayed that necessary Award may be passed in view of the forgoing submissions. 


4 A rejoinder dated 27.2.202 has also been filed on behalf of the workmen to the W/S of the management in 
which all the submissions of the management made in his W/S has been denied submitting that the above workmen 
Bishnu Kumhar and Dwarika Kumhar were taken into employment against special appointment of 5000 vacancies 
and their names were sponsored by the employment exchange and after passing departmental test satisfactorily they 
were offered employment in Cat-IV and Certificate of the Land Loser was meant for purpose to meet out with the 
employment condition and has also challenged the fairness and proprietary of the enquiry conducted by the 
management as well as the dismissal order passed by the Disciplinary Authority. 


5: It transpires from the case record that on Preliminary Point no evidence has been laid from the side of the 
workmen in this case whereas the management has examined ५७४] Ram Gahan Ram and adduced the following 
documentary evidence in his favour:- 


In case of Sri BishnukKumhar

Ts On perusal of the case record it transpires that vide order dated 09.2.204, this Tribunal has held the enquiry 
conducted by the management as fair and proper. 

6. Heard both sides and perused the materials on record. 

7. The Ld. Counsel on behalf of the workmen has submitted that both the concerned workmen were taken into 


employment by the management against Land Loser Scheme of the year 986 by the management following circular 
No. D(P)/PS/87/l22 dated 03.02.987 but they were illegally issued charge sheet on the false allegation that they had 
entered into the services of the BCCL fraudulently by suppressing the material facts. Reply of the same were given by 
the concerned workmen submitting that prior to their appointment in the year 987 the petitioners were already 
registered as Land Loser and were temporarily working there and later on they were given training and after interview 
they were finally selected and posted. Neither the petitioners nor their father had ever shown themselves to be the 
absolute and full owner of 34 Decimal of land bearing Plot No. 2028 and 2347 and the allegation levelled against the 
petitioners are totally false and fabricated. They were given appointment because their fathers were deprived of some 
land for the purpose of collieries works. They had never denied 50% shares of Munilal Kumhar upon their ancestral 
land and in the Land Loser Scheme there is no mention of any prescribed area for the job but their reply was illegally 
rejected and enquiry was conducted illegally and without following the principles of natural justice and their services 
were terminated without any basis and fault which are illegal and prayed to hold the action of the management of 
Bhowra Colliery in dismissing the workmen unjustified and illegal and set aside the dismissal order and pass Award 
in their favour. 


8. On the other hand the Ld. Counsel on behalf of the management has submitted that both the concerned 
workmen were appointed by the management under Land Loser Scheme on the post of UG Miner/Loader on the 
ground of their claim that they were absolute owner of the land that were acquired by the BCCL but subsequently it 
was detected that Sri Bishnu Kumhar has got employment fraudulently and a charge sheet was issued to him for 
making false declaration that he owns 0.34 decimal of land under Khata No. 37 as per details indicated but it was later 
on found that Sri Munilal Kumhar who submitted his representation on 3.0!.995 for getting employment was also 
having 50% of the land in question which were declared by the concerned workmen and ultimately this charge sheet 
was issued and after conducting fair and proper enquiry their services were legally terminated by the BCCL on the 
ground of entry into services of the company by false declaration and misrepresentation that they were the absolute 
owner of the acquired land and at last prayed to hold that the action of the BCCL in terminating the services of the 
concerned workmen is justified and they are not entitled to any relief. 


9. Issue 


Whether the action of the management of Bhowra(N) Colliery of M/s. BCCL in dismissing Shri Bishnu 
Kumhar and Dwarka Kumhar Bhowra(G) Colliery of M/s. BCCL is justified? If so, to what relief the concerned 
workmen are entitled?” 


0. As regards the oral evidence of the management, ७७४] Ram Gahan Ram was the enquiry officer in this case 
and he has supported the story of the management deposing that the enquiry was held fairly and properly and ample 
opportunity was provided to both the charge sheeted workmen by way of principles of natural justice to defend their 
case. In his cross-examination he has admitted that the workmen were given employment as they were land loser and 
no area for acquisition required for employment. 


Now as regards the documentary evidence available on the case record, on perusal of Exhibit M-I it 
transpires that this is a copy of charge sheet dated 09/l!.2.995 issued to Sri Bishnu Kumhar Miner/Loader by the 
Project Officer, Bhowra (N) UG, Mines and on its perusal it is evident that the management had acquired 34 decimal 
of land under Khata No. 37 Plot No. 2028 & 2347 under Bhowra Mouza No. |2 through Land Acquisition Act in the 


[भाग II—ave 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : फरवरी 28, 2026/फाल्गुन 9, 947 937 


year 98]-82 and his father Sri JagarnathKumhar had claimed proprietary right over 34 decimal of land as mentioned 
above and nominated Sri BishnuKumhar for the employment and accordingly he was offered employment and posted 
at Bhowra (N) UG Mines vide letter dated 0/l8.06.987. One Sri Munilal Kumhar put up a representation dated 
3.0.995 before the management claiming that 50% of the land in question is belonging to him and requested the 
management to provide employment against said land and on verification it has been found true and so it was 
established that he had entered into the employment in the Company fraudulently and his act constitutes misconduct 
under section 26.l.! and 26.!.I5 under the Certified Standing Order and he was directed to submit his written and 
signed explanation within 72 hours after receipt of this charge sheet. Exhibit M-2 is the reply of Sri Bishnu Kumhar 
dated 5.2.95 of the charge sheet issued to him in which he had mentioned that the charges leveled against him are 
absolutely false, baseless, unjustified, concocted and motivated. Similar allegation was made against him by the 
Estate Manager Sri J. Prasad vide letter dated 97 May’94 and in reply he had given his explanation that was found to 
be satisfactory and hence further enquiry was dropped and it is surprising that on the same false and baseless 
allegations again a charge sheet has been submitted which is absolutely illegal and void ab initio. Prior to his 
appointment in the year 987, the petitioner was already registered as a Land Looser and thereafter he was given 
training and after interview he was selected and posted on 2.05.987. Neither petitioner nor their father had ever 
shown themselves to be the absolute owner of 34 decimal of land. They had never denied 50% share of Munilal 
Kumhar in the same. Neither in letter of appointment nor in the circular dated 28..986 there is any mention of the 
area of land for which he was regarded as a land looser etc. Exhibit M-3 is the copy of letter dated 04.05.987 to Sri 
BishnuKumhar regarding appointment to the post of Temporary UG Miner/Loader in Group VA and on its perusal it 
transpires that the terms and condition of the appointment have been mentioned therein. Exhibit M-4 is the application 
dated 7.02.996 of Sri BishnuKumhar written to Enquiry Officer with request to engage Sri GupteshwarNath Mishra 
as Co-worker. Exhibit M-5 is the entire enquiry proceeding in which the statement of Sri J. Prasad, M.R has been 
recorded in which he has disclosed regarding the facts of the case of the management. The statement of the concerned 
workman Sri BishnuKumhar has also been recorded in which he has admitted 50% share of MunilalKumhar. 
Thereafter M.R. has been cross-examined by the co-worker in which he has admitted that there is no mention in the 
Circular regarding the quantum of land for employment. It further transpires that the statement of Sri Navin Prasad 
Sharma, Revenue Inspector has also been recorded. The statement of co-workman has also been recorded. Exhibit M- 
6 is the Enquiry report which further reveals that the statement of M.R C/S workmen, Revenue Inspector and 
witnesses of C/S workmen has been recorded and ultimately the Enquiry Officer has held the charge sheeted 
workman BishnuKumhar guilty for not intimating the management for being only 50% shareholder of the land at the 
time of appointment. Exhibit M-7 is the note sheet dated 04/2.0.996 and on its perusal it transpires that the Project 
Officer has come to the conclusion that as the charges leveled against Bishnu Kumhar have been fully established and 
are of serious nature, he may be dismissed from the services of the Company. Exhibit M-8 is the dismissal order dated 
3/9.2.996 addressed to Sri Bishnu Kumhar and on its perusal it transpires that on the basis of the enquiry report in 
which the charges leveled against charge sheeted workman Bishnu Kumhar were proved and the same constituted 
misconduct, he has been dismissed from the services of the Company with immediate effect by the Project Officer, 
Bhowra (४) UG Mines. Exhibit M-9 is the copy of charge sheet dated 09/.2.995 issued to Sri Dwarika Kumhar 
and on its perusal it transpires that it has been issued by the Project Officer stating that the management had acquired 
8 decimal of land under Khata No. 37, Plot No. |8l7 Area .2 decimal and Plot No. 2l9, Khata No. 49 Area 0.6 
decimal under Bhowra Mouza No. |!2 through the Land Acquisition Act in the year 98l-82 and his father Sri 
Sahadeo Kumhar had claimed proprietary right over 8 decimal of land as mentioned above and nominated him for 
employment and accordingly he was offered employment vide letter dated 4/2.5.87 and posted at Bhowra (N) UG. 
Mines vide letter dated 0/l8.6.87 but Sri Munilal Kumhar put up his representation dated 3.0!.995 before the 
management claiming 50% of land and requested to provide him employment and on verification it was established 
that Sri Munilal Kumhar is 50% shareholder of Plot No. !8l7 Area 0.2 decimal and thus it has established that he 
entered into employment in the Company on fraudulent manner which constitutes misconduct under section 26.].l]

72 hours of the receipt of charge sheet. Exhibit M-l0 is the reply of Sri Dwarika Kumhar dated 5.2.95 of the charge 
sheet issued to him in which he has mentioned that the charges leveled against him are absolutely false, baseless, 
unjustified, concocted and motivated. Similar allegation was made against him by the Estate Manager Sri J. Prasad 
vide letter dated 97 May’94 and in reply he had given his explanation that was found to be satisfactory and hence 
further enquiry was dropped and it is surprising that on the same false and baseless allegations again a charge sheet 
has been submitted which is absolutely illegal and void ab initio. Prior to his appointment in the year 987, the 
petitioner was already registered as a Land Looser and thereafter he was given training and after interview he was

owner of 34 decimal of land. They had never denied 50% share of Munilal Kumhar in the same. Neither in letter of 
appointment nor in the circular dated 28.!!.986 there is any mention of the area of land for which he was regarded 
as a land looser etc. Exhibit M-] is the copy of letter dated 6.02.996 written to Dwarika Kumhar in which he has

Sri R.G. Ram, Sr. P.O., Bhowra (N) is appointed as Enquiry Officer and Sri J. Prasad is appointed as Management 
Representative and he was advised to appear on 7.02.996 at l:00 A.M. along with his witness and documents, if 
any, for his defence and he will be given full opportunity to defend and cross-examin the witnesses, if any.

Exhibit M-|2 is the application dated 7.02.996 of Sri Dwarika Kumhar written to Enquiry Officer with request to

which he has admitted that there is no mention in the Circular regarding the quantum of land for employment. It 
further transpires that the statement of Sri Navin Prasad Sharma, Revenue Inspector has also been recorded. The 
statement of co-workman has also been recorded. Exhibit M-|4 is the Enquiry report which further reveals that the

dated 04/2.0.996 and on its perusal it transpires that the Project Officer has come to the conclusion that as the

Personnel Manager, Bhowra Area No.-XI to the Project Officer stating that the competent authority has accorded his 
approval to award punishment as Dismissal from service in respect of Sri Dwarika Kumhar on 6..996 and further 
action may be taken from his end and he has been requested to ensure supply of enquiry report to the workman before 
issuance of dismissal letter. Exhibit M-8 is the dismissal order dated 3/9.2.996 addressed to Sri Dwarika Kumhar 
and on its perusal it transpires that on the basis of the enquiry report in which the charges leveled against charge 
sheeted workman Dwarika Kumhar were proved and the same constituted misconduct, he has been dismissed from 
the services of the Company with immediate effect by the Project Officer, Bhowra (N) UG Mines. 


ll. On perusal of the entire evidence on record as adduced from the side of the management it is evident that the 
concerned workmen were given employment under Land Loser Scheme on the basis that their land that was acquired 
by the BCCL and in pursuance of their appointment letter they joined in the services of the BCCL as UG. 
Miner/Loader in the year 987 and it further transpires that subsequently about 8 years later ie on 3l.0.995, one 
Munilal Kumhar submitted his representation before the BCCL claiming that 50% of the land belonged to him and 
requested for providing employment against the said land and on verification it was found that Sri MunilalKumhar 
was 50% shareholder of the land in question and thereafter the charge sheets were issued to both the concerned 
workmen i.e. Bishnu Kumhar and Dwarika Kumhar alleging that they have entered into the services of the BCCL 
fraudulently claiming themselves to be the full owner of the land in question that was acquired by the BCCL under 
land loser scheme and thereafter both the concerned charge sheeted workmen submitted their reply that were not 
found satisfactory by the management and enquiry was conducted and on the basis of the enquiry report, ultimately 
both the charge sheeted workmen were dismissed from the services of the company. Though the Ld. Counsel for the 
workmen has contended that the enquiry was conducted without following the principles of natural justice but vide 
order dated 09.2.204 this Tribunal has already held the enquiry as fair and proper which makes it clear that the 
procedure of the enquiry was fair and there is no procedural illegality but despite that the legality or illegality of the 
issuance of charge sheet as well as the dismissal order can still be challenged by a workman and here in the instant 
case on perusal of the entire materials on record it is evident that the management has alleged that concerned 
workmen entered into the services of the BCCL by claiming themselves to be the full owner of the land in question 
whereas they are only the 50% shareholder but in its reply the workmen have repeatedly submitted that they never 
claimed or submitted any application to the effect that they are the full owners of the land in question rather they have 
already admitted that they have only 50% share under the same and the management, though has submitted 
overwhelming documents regarding the enquiry proceeding but has utterly failed to submit even a single chit of paper 
/document either before this Tribunal or during enquiry proceeding itself, which may prove that the concerned 
workmen had ever claimed before the BCCL that they were the absolute/full owners of the land in question and thus 
there is no evidence on record to prove any misrepresentation/misconduct by the workmen or that they ever misled 
the management which breaks down the very back bone of the case of the management and secondly, on perusal of 
Exhibit M-5 as well as Exhibit M-3 it is crystal clear that the management representative, J. Prasad in his cross- 
examination has very clearly admitted that there is no mention in the Circular regarding the quantum of land for 
employment and the same has also been admitted in the cross-examination by MW-|. At the same time, on perusal of 
Rule 6.l(iii) of the copy of Circular No. D(P)/PS/87/l22 dated 03.02.987 of the BCCL it is evident that the quantum 
of land would not be a criteria offering appointment and thus it is crystal clear that there is no specific 
mention/requirement of the quantum of land under the Land Loser Scheme of the BCCL to offer and get employment 
and therefore, in the above facts, circumstances and materials available on the case record, this Tribunal comes to the 
conclusion that as the management has utterly failed to prove any misrepresentation/misconduct by the concerned 
workmen, the issuance of charge sheets dated .2.95 to the concerned workmen as well as the dismissal orders 
dated 9.2.96 passed against them, are itself highly unwarranted and illegal and accordingly the charge sheets are 
hereby quashed and dismissal orders are set aside and it is held that the action of the management of Bhowra (N) 


[भाग ए---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : फरवरी 28, 2026/फाल्गुन 9, 947 939 


Colliery of M/s. BCCL in dismissing Sri Bishnu Kumhar and Dwarika Kumhar Bhowra(G) Colliery of M/s. BCCL is 
highly unjustified and illegal and they are entitled to their reinstatement and accordingly the management is directed 
to reinstate them with all consequential benefits from the date of dismissal itself within | month from the date of the 
publication of this Award. This reference is answered accordingly. 


Let the copies of Award in duplicate be sent to the Ministry of Labour & Employment, Government of India, 
New Delhi for information and notification. 


SACHINDRA KUMAR PANDEY, Presiding Officer 
नई दिल्‍ली, 76 फरवरी, 2026

Employer in relation to the management of Incline Mine

Presiding Officer

Appearances: 

For the Employers :- Sri Dhiraj Kumar, (Legal Inspector). 

For the workman :- None. 

State : Jharkhand. Industry:-Coal 


Dated 3/0/2026 
AWARD. 


In exercise of powers conferred under clause (d) of sub-section (l) and sub —section (2A) of Section l0 of 
the Industrial Disputes Act, |947 (/4 of 947),Government Of India through the Ministry of Labour, vide its Order 
No.L-2002/95/2002-IR(C-I) dated 0/l2/2002 has been pleased to refer the following dispute between the 
employer i.e. management of Incline Mine Sudamdih Colliery of M/s. BCCL, Dhanbad and their workman through 
Joint Secretary, Janta Shramik Sangh, Jharia, Dhanbad for adjudication by this Tribunal: 


SCHEDULE 


“Whether the management of BCCL, Incline Mine Sudamdih is justified in dismissing Sri Chandan 
Roy Manjhi from service w.e.f. 24.2.2000? If not to what relief is the workman entitled?” 


2. On receiving order no. L-2002/95/2002-IR(C-I) dated 0/2/2002 Government of India, Ministry of Labour, 
New Delhi for adjudication of the dispute, Reference case no. 03 of 2003 was registered on 0.0.2003 and thereafter 
the notices were sent to the parties with a direction to appear and submit their written statements along with relevant 
documents and witnesses in support of their claims. 


3. After service of notice, both parties appeared previously and filed their authorities, W/S and subsequently 
rejoinder. It further appears that after 2007 this case was put up after a long gap on 6.08.202] and registered notices 
were issued to both the parties but both the parties failed to appear before this Tribunal and the notice of the 
workman/union returned with endorsement “Not located”. Though on 27.06.2025 and 9.09.2025, Sri Dhiraj Kumar, 
Legal Inspector on behalf of the management appeared. It transpires from the case record that workman/union failed 
to appear before the Tribunal since the year 202] which shows that workman/union has lost his interest in this case 
and therefore, this Tribunal is of the opinion that the case deserves to be dismissed due to non prosecution. 


4. Hence, 
ORDERED 


that this case is hereby dismissed for non prosecution and a “No Dispute Award” be drawn up in respect of the 
above reference case. Let the copies of Award in duplicate be sent to the Ministry of Labour & Employment, 
Government of India, New Delhi for information and notification. 


SACHINDRA KUMAR PANDEY, Presiding Officer
